# Concussions (Mild Traumatic Brain Injury)

*Knee & Sports · 9 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**Definition** — subset of **mild TBI** → **acute transient impairment of neurologic function** 2° to an **impulsive force transmitted to the head** (blow to head, face, neck or elsewhere)

**Functional, not structural** — **standard CT / MRI normal** in the vast majority\
✗ Wrong options: MRI often diagnostic

**LOC** — **not required** for diagnosis · occurs in only **\~10%**

**Incidence** — **1.6–3.8 million** sports concussions / yr (Orthobullets) · "nearly 4 million" (OB) · **5–9% of all sports injuries** · \~6% of NCAA football injuries · rising (awareness, youth)

**Who / where** — **TBI = leading cause of sport-related death** · **American football** = majority · **competition > practice**

### Pathophysiology — neurometabolic cascade

| Step | Clinical result |
|---|---|
| Abnormal **neuronal ionic flux** | Headache, **photophobia, phonophobia** |
| Altered neurotransmitter release (**glutamate**) | Impaired cognition, **amnesia**, slowed reaction time |
| **Energy depletion** (correcting imbalance) | **Vulnerable to second injury (second-hit)** |

**After concussion** — **re-injury risk significantly ↑** (biochemistry altered, neuroprotection disrupted)\
✗ Wrong options: risk same as pre-injury

### Risk factors & prevention (helmets, prior concussion)

| Risk factor | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Prior concussion** | **Strongest** · **2–8×** (Orthobullets) · **2–6×** (OB) — read the options |
| **Female** | Higher risk than males (✗ Wrong options: male gender) — possibly oestrogen / cerebral blood flow |
| **Age < 18 (younger)** | Less "cognitive reserve" (✗ Wrong options: increasing age) |
| **Player-to-player contact** | **Football, wrestling, soccer, basketball** highest |
| Mood / learning disorders, migraine, anxiety / depression | **Complicate diagnosis + recovery** — not proven to predispose |
| Equipment quality | **No relationship established** |

**Helmets / headgear** — ↓ impact force · ↓ head + neck injuries in **youth hockey, alpine, equestrian, cycling, motor sports**

**Equestrian injuries** — **helmets** = greatest opportunity\
✗ Wrong options: knee pads, wrist guards, boots, quick-release stirrups

**Risk compensation** — protective gear may ↑ injury by enabling dangerous technique

**Rule changes** — work when one clear mechanism · **upper limb-to-head contact banned in American football (\~50% of concussions)** · red cards for high elbows (soccer)

**Preparticipation evaluation** — number of prior concussions, symptoms, recovery length · **low-impact injury + disproportionate symptoms → particularly vulnerable athlete**

### Genes & lab markers

**Apolipoprotein E** — genetic test under research (prevention)

**CTE proteins** — **tau**, **beta-amyloid**, **TDP-43** (after repetitive diffuse axonal injury)

### Consensus guidelines (International Conference on Concussion in Sport)

**Meets every 4 years** — consensus statement · 1st 2001 · **grading removed 2004** · **all classification systems removed 2008** · latest focus = assessment tools + **timing of return to play**

**SCAT origin (TO)** — **2nd conference, Prague 2004**

**Zurich 2008 (3rd)** — **no same-day RTP even if symptoms resolve** · applies to **minimal- and noncontact** athletes · applies to **children ≥ 10 yr** (younger → age-appropriate checklists) · young elite athletes → more conservative · **CT contributes little** to early diagnosis (use only if structural lesion suspected) · multidisciplinary approach

## 3 Presentation

### Symptoms & mandatory signs

**Headache** — **most common symptom — 70%** · myofascial tension (posterior neck, base of skull) vs post-traumatic (forehead / vertex pressure)

**Somatic** — dizziness, balance problems, **nausea / vomiting**, vision change, **diplopia**, **tinnitus**, photophobia, phonophobia

**Cognitive** — **"in a fog"** / slowed, poor concentration, forgetful

**Emotional** — **lability**, irritability, sadness · **sleep** — insomnia, drowsiness, more or less sleep

**Mandatory signs** — **LOC (\~10%)**, **lying motionless > 5 s**, slow to get up, **confusion / disorientation**, clutching head, **amnesia**, **vacant look / blank stare**, motor incoordination, **ataxia / gait instability** → further evaluation ± removal (sport-specific)

**Cervical muscle tenderness** — **not a concussion sign** → C-spine evaluation, but no 5–10-day concussion protocol (✓ EXCEPT answer)

**Handball player faints, plays on, progressively aggressive, can't recall last goal (TO)** — concussion → **immediately remove from play area** + **Maddocks score**\
✗ Wrong options: counsel 15 min · electrolyte drink · "plays best aggressive"

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Sideline evaluation

**Primary survey** — ABC · **altered mental status → assume C-spine injury** (collar + board) · **never leave player alone**

**Secondary survey** — **remove from field** + immediate **cognitive evaluation by licensed healthcare provider** · no provider → remove entirely + urgent physician referral

**Diagnosis** — **clinical judgement = gold standard** · no tool diagnoses concussion alone

### Red flags — skull base fracture, diffuse axonal injury & emergent imaging

| Red flag → ER for advanced imaging | Not a reason for transport |
|---|---|
| **Deteriorating mental status**, restless, agitated, combative | **Transient unilateral UE numbness** resolving (stinger) |
| **Severe / worsening headache** | Headache alone, normal cognition |
| **Focal deficit** — unequal pupils, abnormal EOM, motor / sensory | Mild headache + shoulder pain + **prior concussion** |
| **Neck pain / tenderness** (C-spine) | **Photophobia + dizziness < 15 min**, no focal / cognitive signs |
| **Bloody otorrhea**, **mastoid ecchymosis** (skull fracture), **blepharohematoma** (eyelid haematoma) | |
| GCS < 15, LOC, amnesia / confusion (OB list) | |

**LOC + bloody discharge from external auditory meatus** — skull fracture → **transfer to ER for advanced imaging**\
✗ Wrong options: SCAT5 · graduated RTP · practice tomorrow

**Mastoid ecchymosis + eyelid haematoma, no LOC** — **emergent transport** to ED

**Diffuse axonal injury (severe closed head injury)** — **no return to football / contact sports**\
✗ Wrong options: return after 6 mo, 1 yr, 2 yr asymptomatic

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| Imaging overall | **Usually unnecessary** · indicated for red flags above, or **subacute / chronic trauma with persistent symptoms** |
| **CT head** | **Most used acutely** · normal in vast majority · shows fracture, haemorrhage, contusion, mass effect, herniation |
| **MRI brain** | Superior detail · **DWI most sensitive to shear injury** · standard MRI normal |
| **Functional MRI** | **↑ cerebrovascular reactivity** in acute post-concussive period |
| Negative CT | **Does not clear RTP** · not diagnostic of concussion |

**Most sensitive test for deficits after mTBI** — **neuropsychologic testing** · its RTP role not fully defined\
✗ Wrong options: head CT · MRI · skull radiographs · sideline assessment

![Subdural haematoma in a boxer — midline shift, effaced cisterns, craniectomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3113-subdural-hematoma.jpg)

### Assessment tools — SCAT

| SCAT fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Use | **Standardised tool, athletes ≥ 13 yr** · **Child SCAT for ≤ 12 yr (5–12)** (TO) |
| SCAT5 on-field | **Red flags, observable signs, Maddocks questions (memory), GCS, C-spine assessment** |
| SCAT5 off-field (private, quiet) | Background, symptom evaluation, cognitive screen (**SAC**), neuro screen, **mBESS**, delayed recall, decision |
| SCAT5 additions (TO) | On-field section, C-spine assessment, **10-word list** (≥ 5 min between immediate + delayed recall), neuro screen |
| **SCAT5 time** | **Minimum 10 min — can't be done correctly in less (TO, OB key)** · OB text: "< 10 min" / "on-field < 15 min" — read the options |
| SCAT history (TO) | SCAT 2005 → SCAT2 2009 → SCAT3 2013 → **SCAT5 2017** (5th conference) |
| SCAT6 (TO) | Ideally **within 72 h**, up to **7 days** · **> 7 days → SCOAT6** / Child SCOAT6 |
| SCAT6 immediate (TO) | Observable signs, GCS, C-spine, **coordination + ocular / motor screen**, Maddocks |
| SCAT6 off-field (TO) | Background, symptoms, cognitive screen, coordination + balance, delayed recall, decision |
| Limits | **Not stand-alone** for diagnosis, recovery or RTP · rapid screen, not a comprehensive neuro exam |
| Cost | **Free**, no technology → widely used in **high school** |

**Current SCAT** — **SCAT6** (TO, recent key) · **SCAT5** (TO, FNB 2021 key; Orthobullets "most commonly used") — read the year / options

**SCAT combines (TO)** — **GCS** (consciousness) + **modified Maddocks** (orientation) + **PCSS** (21 symptoms, 4 categories — somatic, cognitive, emotional, sleep — 7-point Likert) + **SAC** (orientation, concentration, immediate + delayed memory) + neck, balance, coordination exam

**Boxing doctor — recovery from prior concussion, fit for sport? (TO)** — **SCAT**\
✗ Wrong options: GCS · PCSS · SAC

**Not a documentation tool (TO)** — **SCAT** · documentation = **SOAP notes** (Subjective, Objective, Assessment, Plan of first aid), **daily sideline injury report**, **training room treatment log**, **Daily Red Cross List** (no / limited / full participation)

### Assessment tools — ImPACT & neuropsychological tests

| Test | Assesses |
|---|---|
| **ImPACT** | **Computer-based** · **verbal + visual memory, processing speed, reaction time, impulse control, symptoms** (attention, working memory) · vs **baseline** or historical controls |
| **SAC** | **Orientation** (month?), **immediate + delayed memory** (5 words × 3 trials + 1 at end), **concentration** (5 digits backwards, months in reverse) · pencil-and-paper · no reaction time, balance |
| **BESS** | Balance — **3 stances (bipedal, unipedal, tandem) × 10 s** |
| **SOT** | **Entire balance system** — vestibular, visual, somatosensory |
| **King-Devick** | **Saccadic eye movements**, language, concentration — read numbers L→R, **3 tests** · portable, **2 min** |
| **GCS** | **Depth + duration of impaired consciousness** · for **more severe** head injury |
| EyeBoxCNS | Eye movements · clinic computer — **not sideline** |
| SCORE | Smartphone app guiding parents + coaches |
| Helmet sensors | Measure head impact, **not brain** → **cannot diagnose SRC** |

**ImPACT logistics** — preseason **baseline** · off-field, quiet · **fee-based, computer / tablet, \~30 min** → collegiate / pro · **not stand-alone**; return to baseline alone ≠ RTP

**ImPACT baseline** — stable over 2 yr in **college** (no annual retest) · **retest annually in high school / youth**

**Low baseline neuropsych score** — athlete's own reference → **not** a reason to withhold play (✓ EXCEPT answer)

**Post-injury neuropsych testing** — **not required for all**; no defined role in RTP criteria · RTP stays a **clinical decision**

![BESS — bipedal, unipedal, tandem on firm and foam surfaces](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3113-bess.jpg)

![King-Devick test cards](../../MCQ/attachments/q8804-illb-kdtest.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Head trauma in athlete
├─ Red flag (worsening GCS, focal deficit, otorrhea, Battle sign) → ER + CT
├─ Any sign / symptom of concussion → remove, NO same-day RTP
│      └─ rest 24–48 h → graduated RTP (≥ 24 h / stage, ≥ 1 wk)
└─ Unilateral transient UE paresthesia, normal exam, no concussion signs → stinger → same-day RTP
```

### Removal criteria — no same-day return to play

| Remove from play (no same-day RTP) | Not a criterion |
|---|---|
| **Any sign / symptom** of concussion ("if concerned at all") | **Low baseline neuropsych testing** |
| No provider experienced in concussion present | Cervical muscle tenderness alone |
| **History of prior concussion** | Parental waiver / consent (doesn't permit RTP) |
| **LOC** · **amnesia** | Normal neuropsych testing or imaging |
| **Positive exertional stress test** (symptoms recur with exertion) | |
| **Symptoms > 15 min** | |

**Same-day RTP** — **contraindicated in every diagnosed / suspected concussion**, even if symptoms resolve · test to pass for same-game return → **none**\
✗ Wrong options: neuro exam · SCAT5 · ImPACT · K-D · SAC · SOT clears

**Only same-day RTP** — **stinger**: unilateral transient UE pain / paresthesia / deltoid weakness, **complete resolution**, normal exam, no concussion signs

**Bilateral hand paresthesias / weakness** after helmet-on-helmet hit — **spinal cord work-up**, no same-day RTP

**NCAA — out ≥ 5–10 days + stepwise rehab** — **momentary LOC**, **can't recall events before tackle**, **blank stare**, **gait instability**\
✗ Wrong options: cervical muscle tenderness

**Lacrosse ball to head, headache + photophobia, better after minutes** — **hold out for rest of the day**\
✗ Wrong options: RTP on resolution · CT + MRI · "≥ 2 wk rest"

### Graduated return-to-play protocol & timing

| Stage | Activity | Goal |
|---|---|---|
| **1 Symptom-limited activity** | Daily activities not provoking symptoms | Reintroduce work / school |
| **2 Light aerobic** | **Walking, swimming, stationary cycling**, HR **< 70% max** · no resistance | ↑ HR |
| **3 Sport-specific exercise** | **Running** or skating drills · **no head impact** | Add movement |
| **4 Non-contact training drills** | Complex drills (passing) · **start progressive resistance / strength training** | Exercise, coordination, cognition |
| **5 Full-contact practice** | Normal training | Confidence, functional skills |
| **6 Return to play** | Normal game play | |

**Each stage** — **≥ 24 h** (✗ Wrong options: 12 h · 48 h · 72 h · 96 h) → **earliest RTP = 1 week** (✗ Wrong options: 30 min · 24 h · 4 wk · 2 mo) · usually **7–10 days** (OB)

**Number of steps** — **6** (Orthobullets, 2012 consensus) · **5** (OB211942, counting asymptomatic day → full-contact practice) — same sequence

**Symptoms recur at a stage** — **24 h rest → drop back to previous asymptomatic stage**

**Correct sequence** — **stationary bike → running on a track → running + catching a football**\
✗ Wrong options: squats / leg press before stage 4 · track before bike

**14-yr-old, dizzy + can't focus after head clash** — **RTP after ≥ 1-week graduated protocol**\
✗ Wrong options: negative CT · ImPACT back to baseline · later in game · next day

**Confirmed concussion with LOC** — **RTP after graduated protocol while symptom-free**\
✗ Wrong options: next day · after 7 days rest alone · neuropsychiatric clearance

**1 wk later, headache + blurred vision at rest, cognition back to baseline** — **withhold from all contact** — **symptoms at rest = absolute contraindication**\
✗ Wrong options: graduated RTP · day-7 ImPACT · outpatient CT (symptoms may last up to 2 wk)

**Friday concussion, still symptomatic → Monday** — **sub-symptom threshold aerobic exercise after baseline symptom reassessment**\
✗ Wrong options: strict rest until symptoms resolve · aerobic despite headache · accelerated drills · fixed 7 days regardless

### Cognitive rest & predictors of prolonged recovery

**Treatment** — **cognitive + physical rest 24–48 h** → graduated RTP · **no prolonged strict rest (weeks)**

**Cognitive rest** — minimise concentration: reading, schoolwork, **video games**, texting, working online, chess → **limiting video games** ✓

**Cognitive rest** — **↓ symptom duration** ✓ · **↑ activity in post-concussion period → prolonged symptoms** (OB217826)

**Early aerobic activity (days after injury)** — **encouraged, speeds recovery** (OB217914, OB219980) — must stay **below symptom threshold**\
✗ Wrong options: delay aerobic ≥ 1 wk

**Prolonged recovery predictors** — **subacute headache + depression symptoms** ✓ · **ADHD no longer** thought a significant risk factor

**Natural history** — cognitive deficits, balance + symptoms **improve within the first 2 weeks** · **small % persistent** ✓

## 6 Complications

### Second impact syndrome

**Definition** — **second head trauma before first concussion's symptoms resolve** — second hit can be trivial

**Mechanism** — **catastrophic cerebral oedema** from **loss of cerebral autoregulation**\
✗ Wrong options: venous / arterial microvascular bleeding

**Course** — **rapid** progression · **brain stem** often involved · CT shows oedema · **poor prognosis** · **mortality \~50%**\
✗ Wrong options: gradual

**Prevention** — **restrict RTP until symptom-free** ✓ — the reason for no same-day RTP

**Associations** — **male**, **young age**, **American football** \
✗ Wrong options: leading cause of CTE · short-term memory loss

### Postconcussion syndrome

**Definition** — persistent symptoms (headache, confusion, lethargy) **> 10–14 days adults** · **> 4 weeks children** (younger → slower RTS)

**Management** — **RTP contraindicated** · formal **neuropsychiatric evaluation** · pharmacotherapy controversial · **self-limiting** (vs CTE)

### Intracranial haemorrhage

**Subdural haematoma** — **most common**

**Epidural haematoma** — **lucid interval** before decline → **neurosurgical decompression + seizure prophylaxis**

### Chronic traumatic encephalopathy

**Definition** — **progressive neurodegeneration** from **repetitive brain trauma** · **permanent**\
✗ Wrong options: temporary neuronal derangement

**Onset** — **years–decades later**, **midlife** · **mean 42 yr** · boxing, football, hockey, wrestling\
✗ Wrong options: by age 30

**Early features** — **behaviour / affect: apathy, irritability, suicidal ideation**, aggression, "short fuse", loss of impulse control · or cognitive: poor episodic memory, **executive dysfunction**, dementia

**Late** — depression, disinhibition + **parkinsonism** (bradykinesia, tremor, rigidity)

**Pathology** — tau, beta-amyloid, TDP-43 · **superficial cortical laminae II–III** (Alzheimer: layer III and deeper) · **gross changes present**: cerebral atrophy, enlarged ventricles, diffuse senile plaques\
✗ Wrong options: no gross changes

**Diagnosis** — **post-mortem neuropathology only** · no consensus clinical criteria

**45-yr-old ex-NFL running back, cognitive + mood + parkinsonism** — **CTE**\
✗ Wrong options: early Alzheimer (rare in 40s) · Parkinson's (usually > 60) · ALS (no cognitive change) · FTD

**40-yr-old surgeon, ex-college football, worsening irritability + forgetting steps** — **CTE**\
✗ Wrong options: postconcussion syndrome

## X vs Y

| | **Concussion** | **Stinger** | **Cervical cord neurapraxia** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Symptoms | Cognitive, somatic, emotional | **Unilateral** UE pain, paresthesia, **C5-6 weakness** | **Bilateral** / ≥ 2 limbs |
| Same-day RTP | **Never** | **Yes** if complete resolution, full strength, painless neck ROM | **No** |
| Work-up | Clinical ± SCAT; CT if red flags | X-ray if recurrent | **MRI** |

| | **Second impact syndrome** | **Postconcussion syndrome** | **CTE** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cause | 2nd hit before recovery | Single concussion, slow recovery | **Repetitive** trauma |
| Timing | Minutes | > 10–14 d adult · > 4 wk child | Years–decades (mean 42) |
| Outcome | **\~50% mortality** | **Self-limiting** | **Progressive**, post-mortem diagnosis |

| | **ImPACT** | **SCAT5** |
|---|---|---|
| Format | **Computer**, fee, \~30 min | Paper, **free**, ≥ 10 min |
| Setting | Off-field, baseline comparison | On-field + off-field |
| Users | College / professional | High school (widely) |

## How it's asked

**"Handball player faints, aggressive, can't recall last goal (TO)?"** → **immediately remove from play area**

**"Remove from competition for each EXCEPT?"** → **low baseline neuropsychological testing**

**"Confused pro footballer, no LOC — same-day RTP?"** → **return to play should not be allowed**

**"Which athlete may return same day?"** → **unilateral transient arm paresthesia / deltoid weakness that resolves (stinger)**

**"Same-game return — hit to left side of neck, no LOC, normal cognitive exam, left arm paresthesias resolve after several minutes?"** → **yes — transient unilateral brachial plexopathy (stinger)**\
✗ Wrong options: LOC 10 s · amnesia · can't repeat words · bilateral paresthesias

**"NCAA 5–10 day protocol for each EXCEPT?"** → **cervical muscle tenderness**

**"Zurich 2008 — symptoms resolved after injury?"** → **may not return to play**

**"Test to pass for same-game return?"** → **none of the above**

**"Headache + photophobia, better after minutes on sideline?"** → **held out for the remainder of the day**

**"Minimum time per RTP stage?"** → **24 hours**

**"Earliest full return if asymptomatic through protocol?"** → **1 week**

**"Correct activity sequence after symptoms resolve?"** → **stationary bike → track running → running and catching**

**"Safe return for 14-yr-old basketballer?"** → **after a one-week minimum graduated protocol**

**"Headache + blurred vision at rest 1 week later?"** → **withhold from all contact activity**

**"Confirmed concussion with LOC — when to return?"** → **after graduated protocol while symptom-free**

**"Still symptomatic on Monday after Friday concussion?"** → **sub-symptom threshold aerobic exercise after reassessment**

**"Which is not a documentation tool (TO)?"** → **SCAT**

**"Score to declare boxer fit after concussion (TO)?"** → **SCAT**

**"Which SCAT is current (TO)?"** → **SCAT6 (recent key) · SCAT5 (FNB 2021 key)**

**"Computer-based test of attention, memory, processing speed?"** → **ImPACT**

**"Most sensitive test for deficits after mTBI?"** → **neuropsychologic testing**

**"Tool correctly paired with description?"** → **ImPACT — memory, processing speed, reaction time, impulse control**

**"True about assessment tools?"** → **SCAT5 must take at least 10 minutes**

**"Describe ImPACT?"** → **computer-based: verbal + visual memory, processing speed, reaction time, impulse control**

**"Example of cognitive rest?"** → **limiting video games**

**"True after brief LOC in 15-yr-old?"** → **cognitive rest decreases duration of symptoms**

**"Correlates with prolonged recovery?"** → **subacute headache + depression symptoms**

**"Most accurate statement about SRC?"** → **improves within 2 weeks; small % persistent**

**"Second impact syndrome characterised by?"** → **preventable by restricting RTP until symptom-free**

**"Second impact syndrome — true?"** → **mortality approaching 50%**

**"CTE characterised by?"** → **apathy, irritability, suicidal ideation**

**"Retired running back, dementia + parkinsonism?"** → **chronic traumatic encephalopathy**

**"Ex-football surgeon, irritability + forgetfulness?"** → **chronic traumatic encephalopathy**

**"Diffuse axonal injury — return to football?"** → **no return to football**

**"Sign warranting emergent transport?"** → **mastoid ecchymosis + eyelid haematoma**

**"LOC + bloody discharge from ear canal?"** → **transfer to ER for advanced imaging**

**"Greatest equipment opportunity in equestrian injury?"** → **helmets**

**"Most increases concussion risk?"** → **history of previous head injury**

# Discoid Meniscus

*Knee & Sports · 10 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — abnormal development → **hypertrophic, disc-shaped meniscus**

**Incidence** — **3–5%** of the population · highest in **Japanese + Korean** populations (TO)

**Side** — **lateral** ≫ medial (✗ Wrong options: medial commonest) · **bilateral in 25%** (Orthobullets, OB) · 25–50% (OB) — read the options (✗ Wrong options: > 75%) · examine the other knee

**Commonest cause** — of **symptomatic clicking / clunking in a child's knee**

**Discoid medial (TO)** — much rarer · disc-shaped · linked with **trauma / tears**

### Aetiology & pathology

**Aetiology** — **failure of apoptosis in utero** (Orthobullets, OB) · the normal meniscus develops from a cartilaginous anlage and **is never discoid** during development (OB)

**Aetiology traps** — ✗ Wrong options: **COL5A1** (female ACL rupture) · landing biomechanics · quadriceps dominance (ACL) · softening of cartilage with intact surface (OCD)

**Tissue** — **disorganised type I collagen** — fewer fibres, heterogeneous course, lost continuity (Atay, Papadopoulos) · TO: ↓ collagen density, **mucinous change**, ↑ thickness, enlarged surface, **↓ peripheral vascularity**

**Pathology traps** — ✗ Wrong options: incomplete ossification of ACL tibial insertion (tibial eminence fracture) · patellar ossification variant (bipartite patella) · proximal tibial dyschondrosis (Blount) · cartilage sleeve–ossific nucleus separation (patellar sleeve)

**Why it tears** — **↑ thickness**, **poor tissue quality**, **instability / lack of peripheral attachments**, ↓ vascularity

**Tear site + pattern** — most tears **posterior or middle** (Kramer) · commonest pattern **horizontal** (TO) · OB cases: longitudinal LM tear, radial tear

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain, clicking, **mechanical locking** · often symptomatic in **adolescence** · most children **asymptomatic**

**Snapping knee syndrome** — commonest presentation of a symptomatic discoid · classic for the **unstable (Wrisberg) variant** · clunk going **flexion → full extension** · TO: "meniscal thud" at **terminal extension (10–20°)**

**Age (TO)** — **3–4 y**: often painless snapping · **8–10 y**: pain with activity, more acute

**Exam** — mechanical symptoms **most pronounced in extension** · **loss of extension** (lacks 10°, ROM 10–85°) · lateral fullness, lateral joint line tenderness

**Painless clunk at 5–6 y, no trauma** — **discoid lateral meniscus (abnormal meniscal morphology)**\
✗ Wrong options: ACL agenesis (hypoplastic LFC, shallow notch, absent tibial spine) · medial plica (older athletes) · grade IV chondral flap (rare < 9 y) · PVNS · osteomyelitis (systemically ill)

**12 y, snapping, lacks 10° extension, wide lateral joint** — **discoid meniscus**\
✗ Wrong options: OCD · medial plica (medial snapping) · Osgood–Schlatter (tibial tubercle) · Sinding-Larsen–Johansson (inferior pole patella)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| Radiographs (AP + lateral) | **Normal in most children** · severe discoid: **widened lateral joint space (up to 11 mm)**, **squaring of lateral femoral condyle**, **cupping of lateral tibial plateau**, **hypoplastic lateral intercondylar spine** |
| **MRI** — study of choice, most sensitive | **≥ 3 consecutive 5-mm sagittal slices with meniscal continuity ("bow-tie" sign)** — pathognomonic |
| MRI sagittal / coronal | Thick, flat meniscus across the **whole lateral compartment** · ↑ lateral meniscus-to-tibia width ratio · tear in symptomatic cases |
| MRI pitfalls (TO) | **Vacuum phenomenon** mimics a tear in a discoid · **Wrisberg type mimics a floating meniscus** |

![Discoid lateral meniscus — widened lateral joint, squared lateral condyle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3007-ap-radiograph-of-the-knee-showing-widening-of-the-lateral-jo.jpg)

**Most likely X-ray sign** — **squaring of the lateral femoral condyle** (though radiographs are most often normal)

**X-ray traps** — ✗ Wrong options: joint-space **narrowing** · cupping of the **condyle** · squaring of the **plateau** · **hyperplastic** spine · shallow notch (ACL) · Segond (ACL) · patella alta · chondrocalcinosis (CPPD) · tricompartmental OA

| Imaging finding | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| **≥ 3 consecutive sagittal bow ties** | **Discoid meniscus** |
| Double anterior horn · double PCL | **Bucket-handle tear** (double PCL usually medial) |
| T2-bright cyst within meniscus | **Meniscal cyst** |
| High T2 / PD signal in patellar cartilage | **Chondromalacia patellae** |

### Diagnostic criteria

**MRI criterion** — continuity of anterior + posterior horns on **≥ 3 consecutive 5-mm sagittal images**

### Watanabe classification

| Type | Shape / coverage | Posterior attachment | Key fact |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I Complete** | Disc / block — **entire** lateral plateau | Normal | **Most common** (OB) · instability commoner in complete + younger |
| **II Incomplete** | Semilunar — **≤ 80%** (OB) · < 80% / up to 80% (TO) | Normal | |
| **III Wrisberg** | Near-normal size + shape | **No meniscotibial (coronary) attachment — only Wrisberg MFL** | **Least common**, hypermobile → **snapping knee** · needs **stabilization** |

![Watanabe classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3007-watanabe-classification.jpg)

**Frequency order** — complete > incomplete > Wrisberg (OB) · TO: complete + incomplete commoner, Wrisberg least common

**Watanabe (1969)** — arthroscopic appearance · **plateau coverage + posterior attachment** (TO) \
✗ Wrong options: "classified by Wrisberg" (TO)

**Neuschwander (TO)** — Wrisberg type = "**lateral meniscal variant with absence of the posterior coronary ligament**" (not truly discoid)

**Wrisberg type (TO)** — occurs **younger**, **no trauma**

**Instability (Klingele)** — **28% peripheral rim instability** · **anterior horn / anterior third commonest** > posterior horn > body · complete detachment least common · Good: tears + instability each **77%**\
✗ Wrong options: instability uncommon

**Tears at surgery (Klingele)** — **69% (69.5%)**

## 5 Management

### Algorithms

```
Discoid meniscus (OB / Orthobullets)
├─ Asymptomatic, no tear (incl. incidental at arthroscopy) → observe / do nothing
├─ Pain, mechanical symptoms, tear → arthroscopic saucerization ± repair
│      └─ Unstable / detached rim (Wrisberg) → saucerization + stabilization (repair)
└─ Total meniscectomy (open or arthroscopic) → avoid — early OA
```

```
Discoid lateral meniscus (TO)
├─ Asymptomatic → nonop, close follow-up
├─ Child (usually complete), mechanical symptoms → partial meniscectomy ± repair
├─ Adult, complete, mechanical symptoms → partial meniscectomy ± repair
└─ Adult, incomplete
       ├─ Pain ± mechanical symptoms → partial meniscectomy ± repair
       └─ Clicking only → nonop, close follow-up
```

### Observation — asymptomatic discoid

**Painless clicking, 8 y, incomplete, no tear** — **observation only**\
✗ Wrong options: diagnostic arthroscopy · saucerization · + microfracture · + stabilization

**Direct blow, 8 y, asymptomatic discoid on MRI, normal X-ray** — **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: debridement + repair · ligament reconstruction · cast at 45° · immobiliser + NWB 6 wk

**Incidental, untorn discoid at arthroscopy, 38 y with medial pain** — **do nothing surgically** to the lateral meniscus · not treated prophylactically\
✗ Wrong options: saucerization · meniscectomy · repair · microfracture

### Operative criteria

| Operate (saucerize ± repair) | Do not operate |
|---|---|
| **Persistent pain**, recurrent **locking**, swelling | **Asymptomatic** discoid without tear |
| **Mechanical symptoms / loss of extension** | Incidental finding at arthroscopy |
| **Meniscal tear** or **meniscal detachment** | Painless clicking only (TO: adult incomplete) |

### Saucerization ± repair / stabilization

**Indication for saucerization** — **symptomatic meniscal tear**\
✗ Wrong options: asymptomatic abnormal shape · valgus instability · varus malalignment · hyperextension

**11 y, 6 months snapping, 10° extension loss** — **arthroscopic saucerization ± meniscal repair**\
✗ Wrong options: PT strengthening · NWB 6 wk · **open lateral meniscectomy — historical** · microfracture

**9 y, partial central tear of stable discoid** — **arthroscopic saucerization** (partial meniscectomy) · repair only for peripheral instability\
✗ Wrong options: total lateral meniscectomy (poor long-term results) · transplant · repair of central tear · cast 6 wk

**13 y, torn discoid, effusion, failed 4 wk PT** — **arthroscopic stabilization (repair vs partial meniscectomy) + saucerization**\
✗ Wrong options: hinged brace · meniscectomy alone (premature OA) · open saucerization

**Watanabe I with radial tear, locking, 14 y** — **partial meniscectomy ± repair**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · repair only (leaves discoid tissue to re-tear) · complete meniscectomy · transplant (viable tissue present)

**Technique** — debride to an **anatomic-looking meniscus** · **repair if detached** (Wrisberg) · saucerization = remove central disc, keep peripheral crescent

**Rim** — saucerize to a **6–8 mm peripheral rim** — keeps function, avoids re-tear of larger remnants

**Saucerization alone vs with repair** — **comparable** ROM, pain, complications · Carter: stabilization doesn't change outcomes or complications\
✗ Wrong options: repair inferior

**Small knees** — **all-inside repair difficult** in young children (small knee, **deep posterior perforation** risk)

### Outcomes

**Saucerization** — successful in **93% (28 / 30 knees)**; rest needed complete meniscectomy for complex tears (Good) · full flexion > 135°

| Long-term degenerative change (OB) | Rate |
|---|---|
| **Arthroscopic saucerization** | **~20%** |
| **Partial meniscectomy** | **~40%** |
| **Complete meniscectomy** | **~90%** |

**Lee (< 15 y)** — partial meniscectomy → better radiographic results / less OA than subtotal / total\
✗ Wrong options: similar degenerative change

### Total meniscectomy — TO view vs OB view

**TO** — **< 16 y: total meniscectomy may give the longest symptom-free period** · **Wrisberg type → total meniscectomy** (subtotal leaves an unstable rim) or saucerization + suture rim to capsule · stable complete / incomplete → defer unless grooving / chondromalacia · hypermobile medial segment, intact periphery → subtotal meniscectomy / saucerization

**Conflict** — TO above vs OB / Orthobullets: **avoid complete meniscectomy** (~90% degenerative change); **repair / stabilize** a detached Wrisberg rim with saucerization — read the options

## X vs Y

| | **Complete / incomplete** | **Wrisberg type** |
|---|---|---|
| Shape | **Disc** / semilunar | **Near normal** |
| Posterior attachment | **Present** | **Absent** (Wrisberg MFL only) |
| Stability | Stable; symptomatic when torn | **Hypermobile** |
| Presentation | Asymptomatic until tear | **Snapping knee**, younger, no trauma |
| Treatment | Saucerize ± repair if symptomatic | Saucerize + **stabilize** (OB) · total meniscectomy (TO) |

## How it's asked

**"6 y painless clunk, wide lateral joint space?"** → **abnormal meniscal morphology (discoid)**

**"True about discoid menisci?"** → **squaring of affected condyle with cupping of tibial plateau**

**"8 y trampoline, discoid on scope — imaging?"** → **wide lateral joint, squared lateral condyle, cupped plateau, hypoplastic lateral tibial spine**

**"Scope + 3 consecutive sagittal bow ties?"** → **discoid lateral meniscus**

**"5 y clicking, meniscal band filling lateral compartment?"** → **discoid lateral meniscus**

**"Lateral discoid — most likely X-ray finding?"** → **squaring of the lateral femoral condyle**

**"Flat LFC + cupped plateau, locked 10–85° — MRI?"** → **longitudinal lateral meniscus tear**

**"12 y snapping, lacks extension — diagnosis?"** → **discoid meniscus**

**"MRI horn continuity on 3 slices — which X-ray?"** → **lateral joint widening + squared lateral condyle**

**"Aetiology?"** → **failure of apoptosis during in-utero development**

**"Underlying pathology?"** → **disorganised type 1 collagen**

**"11 y snapping, 10° extension loss?"** → **arthroscopic saucerization of the lateral meniscus and / or meniscal repair**

**"Asymptomatic discoid after a direct blow?"** → **observation**

**"Painless clicking, incomplete discoid, no tear?"** → **observation only**

**"Commonest indication for saucerization?"** → **treatment of symptomatic meniscal tear**

**"9 y discoid with central partial tear?"** → **arthroscopic saucerization of the meniscus**

**"Incidental untorn discoid at 38 y?"** → **do nothing surgically to the lateral meniscus**

**"True about saucerization?"** → **a 6–8 mm peripheral rim is recommended**

**"Torn discoid, failed PT?"** → **arthroscopic meniscus stabilization and saucerization**

**"Watanabe I with radial tear?"** → **partial meniscectomy ± repair**

**"Snapping knee — which type (TO)?"** → **Wrisberg type**

# Ear, Eye, Mouth Injuries

*Knee & Sports · 11 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Nose** — **nasal bones = most commonly injured facial bone** · nasal fractures **up to 50%** of sports facial fractures · basketball 26.4%, baseball 22.0% · elbow (basketball), ball (baseball), head (soccer)

**Eye** — baseball, basketball, racquetball, boxing, martial arts · **hyphema = most common intraocular sports eye injury (24%)**

**Ear** — **auricular haematoma**: wrestling, boxing, martial arts

**RTP after facial trauma** — \~**3 weeks** (limited evidence) · full face shields

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Test | Use |
|---|---|
| **CT** | **Facial fractures** (study of choice) · **emergent CT** for ↓ acuity / field cut → traumatic optic neuropathy, orbital + optic canal fracture, nerve sheath bleed, foreign body, brain injury |
| **Fluorescein + UV light** | **Corneal abrasion** / corneal foreign body only |
| **Otoscopy** | **First step** in auricular haematoma — **TM perforation** |
| Penlight | Septum — **septal haematoma**, CSF leak, cribriform injury |

## 5 Management

### Auricular haematoma

**Mechanism** — blunt **shear separates perichondrium from cartilage** → blood collects

**First step in ED** — **otoscopic evaluation** (TM rupture; rule out skull fracture)\
✗ Wrong options: compressive dressing + PCP referral · formal surgical decompression · needle decompression **before** otoscopy · head MRI

**Treatment** — **aspiration (urgent needle decompression)** + **wrapping (compressive dressing)** to prevent re-accumulation · **formal surgical evacuation → recurrent or delayed** cases · prevent with **headgear**

**Untreated** — **cauliflower ear** (fibrous / abnormal cartilage), perichondritis, infection, necrosis

![Acute auricular haematoma](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3120-3-moved.jpg)

### Nasal septal haematoma

**What** — sub-perichondrial / subperiosteal septal haematoma (submucosal vessel rupture) · uni- or bilateral (bilateral → severe trauma) · **any age**\
✗ Wrong options: mostly children

**Commonest symptom** — **nasal congestion / obstruction (\~95%)** · exam: **purple-red bulging septum**\
✗ Wrong options: epistaxis

**Treatment** — **urgent decompression (I&D) under local anaesthesia** + anterior packing\
✗ Wrong options: phenylephrine packing (that's for epistaxis)

**I&D prevents** — **septal cartilage (chondral) avascular necrosis → saddle nose** · **septal abscess within 72 h** (common, not unlikely)\
✗ Wrong options: deviated septum · recurrent epistaxis · olfactory nerve damage · turbinate swelling

![Septal haematoma — bilateral purple septal bulge](../../MCQ/attachments/q218940-illa-septal-hematoma.jpg)

### Ocular injury — emergent CT

| Injury | Key finding | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Traumatic mydriasis** | **Dilated pupil**, **contusion of iris sphincter**, transient | **Bed rest** |
| **Corneal abrasion** | **Fluorescein stain + UV light** | Topical antibiotics + NSAIDs, patch, **non-urgent** ophthalmology |
| **Hyphema** | **Blood in anterior chamber** (haze) — may mean vitreous / retinal injury | Patch + **ER / emergent ophthalmology** |
| **Ruptured globe** | **Sunken** eye, vitreous exudate | Emergent |
| **Orbital wall fracture** | **Extraocular muscle entrapment** | Emergent |
| **Retinal detachment** | **Bright flashes**, stabbing pain, **field cut** | Emergent |
| **Traumatic optic neuropathy** | ↓ acuity, ↓ colour vision, field cut | **Emergent CT** |

**Red-flag symptoms** — vision loss, ↓ acuity, floaters / flashers, diplopia

**Boxer: hyphema + ↓ acuity + ↓ colour vision + field cut** — **emergent CT**\
✗ Wrong options: patch + ophthalmology in 2 days · fluorescein · high-dose steroids before CT · observation

**Traumatic optic neuropathy** — **blunt 78%** / penetrating 22% · sports + MVA · **optic canal fracture on CT = poor prognosis** · treatment controversial (steroids, optic canal decompression, sheath fenestration) · children: only 29–44% improve

### Mandible fracture — airway

**Most serious acute complication** — **airway obstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: blood loss · CVA (carotid injury, rare) · periodontal disease (late) · hearing loss

**Why** — **mandible suspends the tongue anteriorly** → fracture + supine → **tongue falls back** · soft-tissue swelling · worst with **multiple mandible** or combined maxillary-mandibular-nasal fractures

**Airway** — intubation often impossible → **surgical airway**

### Dental avulsion — milk storage & replantation

**Avulsed tooth** — **medical emergency** · survival depends on **time out of socket** + **periodontal ligament** damage

**Handling** — hold by **crown, never root** · **rinse** debris with water / saline · **never brush or scrub**\
✗ Wrong options: toothbrush · hydrogen peroxide

**Replant immediately** — **immediate repositioning / replantation** → bite on towel → dentist / ER\
✗ Wrong options: wait for dentist · emergency root canal

**If not replanted** — keep **moist**: **milk (whole)**, saline, **saliva** / buccal fold, buffered solution kits · **rinse with saline → milk** = usual answer\
✗ Wrong options: ice · water · carbonated drink

**Children** — replanting or carrying in mouth → **aspiration risk**

**Dentist** — as soon as possible, **preferably within 1 hour**

![Avulsed incisors](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3120-4-moved.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Auricular haematoma** | **Septal haematoma** |
|---|---|---|
| First step | **Otoscopy** | Penlight inspection |
| Treatment | **Aspiration + compressive wrap** | **Urgent I&D under LA + packing** |
| Complication | **Cauliflower ear** | **Saddle nose**, septal abscess |

## How it's asked

**"Several front teeth knocked out — initial steps?"** → **rinse in saline, place in milk**

**"Emergent management of tooth luxation?"** → **immediate repositioning or replantation**

**"Three teeth out (figure) — next step besides calling dentist?"** → **pour saline on teeth, then place in milk**

**"Unstable jaw, malocclusion — most serious acute complication?"** → **airway obstruction**

**"Hyphema, ↓ acuity + colour vision, field cut?"** → **emergent CT**

**"Purple bulging septum — true?"** → **septal avascular necrosis is a potential complication**

**"I&D of septal haematoma prevents?"** → **chondral necrosis**

**"Auricular haematoma — best next step in ED?"** → **otoscopic evaluation**

# Exercise Science

*Knee & Sports · 12 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Contraction types – isokinetic, isotonic, isometric & eccentric

| Type | Definition | Example | Fact asked |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Isotonic** | **Constant muscle tension (force)** as length changes · improves motor performance | Free-weight biceps curls · leg extensions + curls | **Concentric + eccentric are isotonic** · force–velocity (force constant) |
| **Isometric** | **Constant length**, tension ∝ external load · causes hypertrophy (Orthobullets) | Push against an immovable object · plantarflex against a wall 15 s | **Length–tension relationship** |
| **Concentric** | Muscle **shortens**, tension ∝ load | Curl with elbow flexing · slow single-leg heel rise | — |
| **Eccentric** | Muscle **lengthens** under constant force | Curl with elbow extending · "negative" of a lift | **Most efficient strengthening** · **highest muscle forces** |
| **Isokinetic** | **Constant velocity, variable resistance** (✗ Wrong options: constant effort at variable speed) | **Cybex / dynamometer** · bike at constant pedal velocity, varied resistance | Objective strength testing in rehab · force–velocity (velocity constant) |
| **Plyometric** | **Rapid eccentric → concentric** (stretch-shortening) | **Box jumps**, bounding, hopping | Power sports · late rehab |
| Open chain | Distal end **moves freely** | Seated leg extensions + curls | — |
| Closed chain | Distal end **fixed** | Squat with planted foot, leg press | — |

**Eccentric = injury contraction** — **muscle strain at myotendinous junction** · **DOMS** · **distal biceps rupture** (forced eccentric load, + hook test)\
✗ Wrong options: isometric · concentric · isokinetic · plyometric

**Achilles tendinitis rehab after rest + CAM boot** — **eccentric strengthening**: rise on both legs on a 1-inch board, **lower slowly on the injured leg only** + gastrocnemius-soleus stretching · avoid steroid injection\
✗ Wrong options: box jumps (plyometric) · wall push (isometric) · slow single-leg rise (concentric) · delay rehab

**Overstretched muscle** — sarcomeres generate little active tension → **< 30% maximal force**

**Isokinetic in tennis (TO)** — **slow + fast speed isokinetic** → maximal resistance at every point of ROM, uniform strength, concentric + eccentric · add **core strengthening (lower back + hip)** → kinetic-chain power transfer\
✗ Wrong options: ballistic · plyometric (power, added once baseline strength exists) · fartlek (trains energy systems, not muscle)

### Open vs closed kinetic chain exercise

| Characteristic | **Closed chain** | **Open chain** |
|---|---|---|
| Distal segment | **Fixed** against considerable resistance | **Free** in space |
| Stress pattern (TO) | **Linear** (axial loading) | **Rotary** |
| Joint axes (TO) | Multiple | **One primary** |
| Joint segments (TO) | **Both move simultaneously** | One stationary, other mobile |
| Moving joints (TO) | **Multiple** | Isolated joint |
| Planes (TO) | Multiple (triplanar) | One |
| Muscles (TO) | **Significant co-contraction** → predictable movement | **Isolated** group, minimal co-contraction |
| Pattern (TO) | Functional | Often non-functional |
| Loads (OB) | **Compressive** → ↓ shear, translation, distraction | ↑ shear |
| Use (OB) | **Early rehab**; more often **lower limb** | Later; maximal single-muscle strengthening |
| Examples | Squat, leg press, bicycle, push-up | **Seated leg extension**, hamstring curl, waving a hand, swing-phase foot |

**Closed-chain limits (OB)** — individual muscles **not maximally strengthened**, not all joint motion normalised · joint compression **↑**\
✗ Wrong options: distal end free · mainly upper limb · ↓ compression · single joint

**After ACL reconstruction** — **closed chain** (quads + hamstrings co-contract, protects graft) · **open-chain leg extension → strong anterior tibial pull → graft stress** · one EMG study: **more hamstring co-activation with isokinetic** than closed chain

**Push-up on a weight plate (TO)** — **closed chain dynamic** (distal fixed + up-down movement; instability adds core work) · static push-up = plank (isometric)

![Push-up on weight plate — closed chain dynamic](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17136380-1.png)

| Shoulder rehab stage (OB) | Exercise |
|---|---|
| **Early** | **Closed chain** — axial load → **glenohumeral compression ↓ rotator cuff demand** · **co-contraction of periscapular + rotator cuff** |
| Early–mid | **Facilitated active motion** — proximal segment motion, diagonal patterns |
| Later | **Resistive active motion** — **open chain** with extrinsic loads (weights, tubing) |
| Late (return to sport) | **Plyometrics** — slow = stabilisation; fast = stretch-shortening |

### Muscle fibre types

| | **Type I** | **Type IIA** | **Type IIB** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Name | **Slow oxidative** ("slow red ox") | Fast oxidative-glycolytic | **Fast glycolytic** |
| Metabolism | Aerobic (O2 needed) | Aerobic + anaerobic | **Primarily anaerobic** |
| Recruitment | **First** (smallest units) | Intermediate | **Last** |
| Speed / force | Slow, less forceful | Intermediate | **Fastest, most powerful** |
| Fatigue | **Resistant** | Intermediate | **Most fatigue-prone** |
| Training | ↑ % with endurance (OB) | ↑ % with endurance (Orthobullets) · ↑ with resistance (OB) | ↓ with resistance training · **DOMS mainly here** |

### Energy systems & nutrition (ATP-CP, glycogen, fatty acids)

| | **ATP-CP (alactic)** | **Glycolytic (lactic acid)** | **Aerobic** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fuel | **ATP + creatine phosphate** | Muscle glycogen → lactate | **Glycogen + fatty acids** (Krebs, oxidative phosphorylation) |
| Duration | **< 10 s** high intensity | **2–3 min** (Orthobullets) · **1–4 min** glycogen + lactic acid (OB) | Endurance · **> 4 min** (OB) · predominates from **\~60 s** (TO) |
| Fibre | Type II | Type II | **Type I** |
| O2 | Not needed | Not needed | Required |
| ATP yield | Immediate, tiny stores | **Low**; lactic acidosis after several min | **High** |

**Energy continuum at maximal effort (TO)** — **20 s: PCr 40% · glycolysis 50% · aerobic 10%** · **40 s: 5% · 80% · 15%**

**Marathon** — **muscle glycogen early (< 90 min)** → **free fatty acids after \~90 min** (final hour = FFA) · liver glycogen early, secondary · lactate = intense exercise in low glucose\
✗ Wrong options: creatine phosphate · muscle / liver glycogen late

![Energy substrates over time](../../MCQ/attachments/q210183-illa-complete.jpg)

**Substrate curve labels (OB)** — A = ATP + CP · B = muscle glycogen (anaerobic) · **C = plasma FFA + adipose triglycerides** · **D = muscle glycogen, plasma glucose, liver glycogen**

**Glycogen stores (TO)** — CHO = **1–2%** of energy stores · **80% muscle (300–400 g)**, 14% liver (70–100 g), 6% blood glucose

| Glycogen depletion curve (TO) | Exercise | Fuel |
|---|---|---|
| **A — flat** | **Light** (walk, warm-up) | Plasma FFA oxidation, low steady glycogen use |
| **B — gradual fall** | **Moderate** (jogging) | ↑ glycolysis supplementing aerobic |
| **C — steep fall** | **Heavy** (sprints, HIIT) | **Anaerobic glycolysis** + PCr |

![Glycogen depletion: light, moderate, heavy](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17136260-1.jpg)

| RER = VCO2 / VO2 (TO, respiratory gas analyser) | Fuel |
|---|---|
| **0.7–0.8** | **Lipids** |
| **1.0** | **Carbohydrate** |
| **> 1.0** | Significant anaerobic contribution |
| ↓ RER with | Exercise duration, dietary fat, age, VO2max, % type I fibres |
| ↑ RER with | Intensity, dietary CHO, male sex |

**Crossover point (TO)** — fat → CHO predominance at **70% CHO / 30% fat** · **below it → mainly fat oxidation** · **training shifts it right** (more fat) · **sympathetic activity shifts it left**\
✗ Wrong options: protein above it · same for everyone · fat ↑ beyond it

![Crossover concept](../../MCQ/attachments/TO21733244-2.png)

| Caffeine (TO) — methylxanthine | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Ergogenic** | **↑ FFA + glycerol**, **↑ catecholamines**, **↑ muscle fat oxidation (glycogen sparing)** · not **↑ carbohydrate oxidation** |
| Muscle | ↑ SR Ca2+ release, ↑ troponin / myosin Ca2+ sensitivity, ↓ SR reuptake · ↑ Na+/K+-ATPase (K+ uptake, Na+ efflux) |
| WADA | **Removed January 2004** · old urine limit **12 µg/ml** ≈ 10 mg/kg (\~700 mg / 70 kg) |
| Dose | Drinks 30–200 mg / serve · tablets 100–200 mg · **250–300 mg mild diuresis** · **\~500 mg (7 mg/kg) → dehydration** |

**Carbohydrate loading** — ↑ CHO **3 days** before + ↓ activity → **maximises muscle glycogen** for events **> 90 min** · best technique = **maintain normal diet** (Orthobullets)\
✗ Wrong options: liver glycogen / 60 min · fructose (makes liver, not muscle, glycogen) · diuresis

**Fluids** — core temperature + HR rise **∝ water debt** at onset · replace to **maintain pre-practice weight** · **low-osmolarity (< 10%)** CHO-electrolyte fluid → ↑ gut absorption · **glucose polymers ↓ osmolarity**

### Training adaptations – capillary density, fibre types, protein synthesis, periodisation

| | **Endurance (aerobic)** | **Strength (resistance)** |
|---|---|---|
| **Key adaptation** | **↑ capillary density (most important)** | **↑ cross-sectional area (hypertrophy)**; hyperplasia unlikely |
| Mitochondria | ↑ size, number, density · ↑ respiratory capacity → ↑ VO2max | ↑ (Orthobullets weight training) · not aerobic only (OB) |
| Enzymes | ↑ Krebs, fatty acid, respiratory chain · **no change in glycolytic enzymes** | — |
| Fuel | Fatty acids > glycogen over time | Immediate sources |
| Fibres | ↑ oxidative capacity I, IIA, IIB | ↑ IIA, ↓ IIB (OB) |
| Early gains | — | **↑ motor unit recruitment** ± synchronisation; **hypertrophy after 4–6 wk** |
| Force | — | Max force **∝ physiologic CSA** |
| Load | Dynamic exercise ↑ cardiac output via **↑ stroke volume** | High load, low reps |

**Weight training (Orthobullets)** — ↑ CSA, strength, mitochondria, capillary density, thickened connective tissue · **adult gains = hypertrophy**

**Marathoner vs powerlifter (OB)** — **marathoner: ↑ capillary density**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ CSA in marathoner · ↑ mitochondria / fat use / type I in powerlifter

**Myofibrillar protein synthesis (OB)** — **resistance exercise above 60% 1-repetition maximum (1-RM)** (anabolic ceiling; sigmoidal dose response)\
✗ Wrong options: aerobic (↑ oxidative capacity only) · 30% 1-RM high reps · GH / testosterone (no acute MPS effect; driven by autocrine / paracrine + mechanotransduction)

**Periodisation** — **planned variation in intensity + duration over a predefined time** (Orthobullets, OB) · **planned manipulation of training variables (load, sets, reps) to maximise adaptation** + prevent overtraining (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: rest interval · gradual increase / decline

**Cycles (OB)** — **macro = training year** · **meso = 3–6 months** · **micro = 3–6 weeks** · goal = peak performance via progressive overload

**Models (TO)** — **linear** (one attribute, e.g. 4 wk strength) vs **non-linear** (strength + power + endurance together)

| Training term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Progressive overload** (OB) | Stress above what the body is used to → adaptation |
| **Stacking** (OB) | Multiple performance-enhancing drugs / supplements for synergy |
| Static progressive (OB) | Splinting for contracted joints, not exercise |
| **Interval** (TO) | Near-maximal work (\~**90% max HR**) alternating with recovery; **work : rest \~1 : 1** |
| **Fartlek** (TO) | Continuous steady pace + sprints → aerobic + anaerobic; distance athletes; up to 45 min |
| **Circuit** (TO) | ≥ **6 stations** in sequence |

| State (TO) | Performance | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| **Supercompensation** | **Balanced training** → dip → rebound **above baseline** | — |
| Functional overreaching | Temporary decline → supercompensation | Short |
| Non-functional overreaching | Short-term decrement ± maladaptation | **Days–weeks** |
| **Overtraining syndrome** | **Long-term** decrement (unbalanced training / non-training stress / rest) | **Weeks–months** |

### Strength training in children

**Safety** — age-appropriate, supervised **weight training + plyometrics are safe** · no ↑ injury, **no growth-plate damage**, no ↑ OCD

**Mechanism** — gains from **↑ neuromuscular activation + coordination** / efficiency of muscle action ("muscle memory") · **not hypertrophy** (insufficient testosterone) · gains **reversible** if stopped

### Biomechanics of racing & ball sports (drag, force-time, force platform, cycling posture)

| Drag (TO) | Cause | Reduce by |
|---|---|---|
| **Surface** (friction / skin) | Fluid friction at the boundary layer | Smooth suits, shaving, waxing skis / surfboards |
| **Pressure** (form) | High pressure in front, low-pressure wake behind | **↓ frontal area + streamlining**, **drafting** |
| **Wave** | Surface waves (open-water swimming) | Lane markers + gutters in pools |

**Flow (TO)** — laminar at low velocity · **turbulent boundary layer → later separation → ↓ pressure drag** · "drag crisis" · **dimples / scuffing** → low drag at lower velocity

**Not useful to ↓ drag (TO)** — **increasing frontal area**\
✗ Wrong options: streamlining · drafting · smooth suits

**Drafting (TO)** — **cycling**, car racing (also swimming) · leader uses more energy · running **1 m behind → \~7% ↓ metabolic cost**\
✗ Wrong options: shooting · baseball · ice hockey

**Force–time principle (TO)** — Newton's 2nd law → **impulse–momentum** · longer force application → safer deceleration · **catch with arm / glove extended (A)** · land with legs extended to flex through range\
✗ Wrong options: catching close to body (B)

**Cycling posture (TO)** — **anterior pelvic tilt** flattens lumbar spine → **↓ frontal area** (wind = main retarding force) · **low stack-height cleat** (pedal spindle → cleat top) · 1st MT base over pedal axis · narrow hips → cleats outside, wide hips → inside · toe-out → rotate cleat in

**Force platform (TO)** — measures **ground reaction force** (gait biomechanics)\
✗ Wrong options: ball spin · jump force · shoe friction

### Sports clothing, footwear & equipment (TO)

**Tight body suits** — ↓ surface drag, **↓ turbulence** (bonded seams follow contour), **↑ buoyancy**, ↑ speed (✗ Wrong options: **decreased buoyancy**)

**Children in Delhi summer** — **moisture-wicking garments** (capillary action; polyester, polypropylene, nylon, merino wool)\
✗ Wrong options: cotton (absorbs **up to 7%** of own weight, damp, hinders cooling) · lightweight dry · dark heavy

| Footwear (TO) | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Pitch (heel drop)** | **Heel height − forefoot height**; range 0–14 mm · lower = closer to barefoot forefoot strike |
| **\~6 mm** | **Midfoot strike** |
| **\~12 mm** | **Heel strike** (false: "pitch > 12 mm gives marathoners a midfoot strike") |
| **Achilles problems** | **Slightly ↑ pitch** (↓ stretch load) + flexibility + eccentric strength |
| **Sole flexion point** | **At 1st MTP** · too proximal → ↓ stability · too distal → ↓ sagittal 1st MTP motion |

## 2 Mechanism

### Muscle injury healing – TGF-β & early mobilisation

| Injury | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Soreness (DOMS)** | **Oedema + inflammation in connective tissue** → ↑ intramuscular pressure · **type IIB** · **eccentric** · starts at MTJ, spreads · cytoskeletal disruption · **peaks 24–72 h** (Orthobullets) |
| **Strain** | **Myotendinous junction**, **eccentric** contraction · inflammation → fibrosis |
| **Contusion** | Blunt → haematoma · ECM within 2 days, peaks 5–21 days · **myositis ossificans most apparent at 4 wk** |
| **Laceration** | Near MTJ, abnormal contour · **dense scar via myofibroblasts ← TGF-β** · regeneration + reinnervation unpredictable, incomplete |

**Scar cytokine** — **TGF-β** (myofibroblast differentiation + proliferation) · **regeneration from 7–10 days ← IGF-1, FGF, NGF**\
✗ Wrong options: VEGF

**Immobilisation after muscle injury** — **≤ 3–5 days**, then progressive strengthening + stretching · **immediate motion → large dense scar**, poor fibre penetration · **2 wk → atrophy limiting outcome**, ↑ fatty infiltration, less scar, less regeneration, disorganised fibres

**Immobilisation (Orthobullets)** — atrophy, ↓ tension, **↑ fatigability**, fatty infiltration · faster in **single-joint** muscles · **non-linear** (most in first days; myofibril loss) · worse **without tension** → **quadriceps > hamstrings** when knee held in extension

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Fact |
|---|---|
| **CK after eccentric exercise** | ↑ **24–48 h**, peak **3–6 days** (OB) |
| CPK normal (TO) | **40–350 U/L** |
| **CK > 5,000 U/L** (TO) | Serious muscle disturbance |
| **CPK > 10,000 U/L** (TO) | Clinically significant exertional rhabdomyolysis threshold |
| Rhabdomyolysis urine | Dipstick + for blood but **no microhaematuria** (myoglobinuria) |
| **TGF-β** | Scar after laceration |
| IGF-1, FGF, NGF | Muscle regeneration |

### Diagnostic criteria

| Condition | Criterion |
|---|---|
| **Exertional rhabdomyolysis** (TO) | **CPK > 10,000 U/L + signs of muscle damage** |
| **Haematuria needing work-up** | **Persists > 7 days** after stopping exercise or **age > 50** (Orthobullets) · **persists > 72 h** (OB) |
| **EILO** | **Negative BDR + negative bronchoprovocation, positive CLE** |
| **High-altitude illness** (TO) | Rapid ascent **> 8000 ft (2500 m)** · HAPE > 2500–3000 m |

### Medical conditions in athletes (laryngeal obstruction, haematuria)

**EILO** — mistaken for exercise-induced bronchoconstriction · **females** · dyspnoea + wheeze · includes exercise-induced laryngomalacia + vocal cord dysfunction · differentials: EIB, hyperventilation, cardiac

| Test | **EIB (exercise asthma)** | **EILO** |
|---|---|---|
| Bronchodilator reversibility (β2-agonist) | **Positive** | **Negative** |
| Bronchoprovocation (methacholine, mannitol, EVH) | **Positive** | **Negative** |
| **Continuous laryngoscopy during exercise (CLE)** | — | **Positive — gold standard** |

**EILO level** — **supraglottic** (arytenoids, closure above glottis) vs **glottic** (**vocal cord closure**) · optimal treatment under study

![Supraglottic vs glottic EILO](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3118-supraglottic-and-glottic-level-eilo.jpg)

**Exercise-induced haematuria** — microscopic **16.7–46.7%**, macroscopic **3.1–19.2%** · risk: intensity, posture, age, heat, altitude, renal disease · bladder / kidney contusion, renal vasospasm · asymptomatic · stop exercise, **repeat UA at 48–72 h**

**Persistent haematuria differential** — **sickle cell trait → papillary necrosis**, UTI / STI (pyuria, dysuria), kidney contusion (flank blow), stone (flank pain)\
✗ Wrong options: pre-workout (only ↑ susceptibility) · mononucleosis · rhabdomyolysis (myoglobin, no RBCs)

### Cardiorespiratory fitness testing (VO2 max, ventilatory threshold, Bruce protocol) (TO)

**VO2 max** — max O2 taken in + used at maximal exercise · **best indicator of cardiovascular fitness** · units **ml/kg/min** · rises with fat loss\
✗ Wrong options: l/kg/min · ml/g/min · ml/kg/hr

**Measurement** — direct (treadmill, ergometer, step test; accurate, costly) vs indirect (Astrand charts, HR, RPE)

| Threshold | Definition |
|---|---|
| Aerobic threshold (Orthobullets) | Effort where anaerobic pathways become significant |
| **Anaerobic (lactate) threshold** (Orthobullets) | **Lactate production > removal** |
| Lactate threshold (TO) | Substantial ↑ blood lactate · **predicts endurance better than VO2max** |
| **Ventilatory threshold** (TO) | **Ventilation rises disproportionately to O2 uptake** · **55–75%** of max aerobic capacity · H+ buffered by HCO3− → extra CO2 · synonyms: lactate threshold, OBLA, HR deflection point, maximal lactate steady state |
| Low intensity 30–50% (TO) | Ventilation coupled to O2 (depth, diaphragm) · ageing blunts maximal ventilation |

| Test (TO) | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Bruce protocol** | **Most common treadmill test** (Robert A Bruce) · speed + incline ↑ **every 3 min** · **7 stages** · stop at **85% max HR** or HR > 115 for two stages |
| Modified Bruce | Starts flat: **1.7 mph 0% → 1.7 mph 5%**, then stage 1 of standard · elderly / sedentary |
| Bruce VO2max | 14.76 − 1.379T + 0.451T² − 0.012T³ (T = minutes) |
| Astrand | Ergometer 6 min to HR > 120, then +25 W / min · **Borg RPE 6–20** |

### Body composition – bulking/cutting & bioimpedance (TO)

**Body mass analyser** — **electrical impedance measurement**\
✗ Wrong options: body fat analysis · dry mass · electron spectroscopy

**BIA** — weak alternating current; Ohm's law R = V/I · current passes faster through **hydrated fat-free tissue** · **more muscle → more water → ↓ impedance** · phase angle read at 50 kHz

**BIA current** — **800 µA at 50 kHz** (answer key; explanation text writes "800 mA") · source electrodes dorsal foot + wrist, detectors at wrist styloids + between malleoli · Siri equation · 5 mm girth or 1.5 cm height error → up to 2% change

**Bulking vs cutting** — never simultaneous · bulking = high CHO + protein · cutting = calorie restriction preserving muscle · **ratio 3 : 1**, bulking 8–12 wk → **gain 12 wk before, cut 4 wk after competition**

## 5 Management

```
Choosing the exercise
├─ Protect healing graft / joint → closed chain
├─ Achilles tendinitis after rest → eccentric + calf stretch
├─ Shoulder: early → closed chain → resistive → plyometrics
└─ Tennis player muscle training (TO) → slow + fast isokinetic
```

### Therapeutic modalities – iontophoresis & aquatic exercise

**Goals** — ↓ inflammation, ↑ local blood flow, ↑ tissue compliance · cryo / heat, massage, ultrasound, electrical stimulation, iontophoresis

**Iontophoresis** — **direct (galvanic) current drives charged medication through skin** · steroids, LA, salicylates, NSAIDs · tendinitis · deep delivery evidence conflicting · shoulder tendinitis **< 45 yr** responds to steroid iontophoresis\
✗ Wrong options: phonophoresis = ultrasound · TENS · EMG · laser · magnetic fields

**Contraindications** — **cardiac pacemaker** (current-sensitive), drug allergy

| Iontophoresis indication | Drug |
|---|---|
| Hyperhidrosis | Tap water, glycopyrrolate |
| Muscle spasm | Magnesium sulphate, calcium chloride |
| Oedema | **Hyaluronidase** |
| Adhesive conditions | Iodine |
| Inflammation | Dexamethasone, hydrocortisone, prednisone, lidocaine, salicylates |
| **Calcific tendinitis + myositis ossificans** | **Acetic acid** |
| Open wounds | Zinc oxide, tolazoline |

**Aquatic exercise** — **buoyancy ↓ vertical ground reaction force** → ↓ joint stress · prevents secondary lower-limb injury · **less abrupt HR rise** (lower max HR) · **↑ O2 consumption** vs land\
✗ Wrong options: ↑

### Stretching (PNF, gastrocnemius) (TO)

**PNF = stretching** · muscle **contracted maximally → relaxes → stretched** actively / passively by partner · Golgi tendon organ response relaxes the muscle · OB wording: muscle stretch used to augment contraction\
✗ Wrong options: strength · resistance · injury-prevention technique

**Gastrocnemius vs soleus (TO)** — gastrocnemius plantarflexes ankle + assists knee flexion → stretch with **knee straight** · soleus acts at ankle only → stretch with **knee bent**

| Calf stretch (TO) | Position | Hold |
|---|---|---|
| **Straight-leg gastrocnemius** | Hands on wall, **back knee straight**, heel down, lean in | **20–30 s** |
| Achilles / soleus lunge | **Rear knee bent**, weight on rear leg | 10–30 s × 3 |
| Soleus | **Both knees slightly bent**, heels down | 30–60 s × 2, 3 / day |

### Exertional rhabdomyolysis (TO)

**Warning signs** — swelling + soreness beyond expected · screen with **urinalysis**: scanty, **dark brown "Coca-Cola"** urine, protein + blood · **definitive = CPK**

**Treatment** — **fluids + maintain urine output** (renal protection) · fasciotomy only for severe compartment syndrome

**Biceps swollen after workout, normal urine, CPK 8500, transaminases 1.5–2×** — **admit to ward for IV hydration**\
✗ Wrong options: muscle biopsy · oral hydration + recheck 12–24 h · ICU fasciotomy

### High-altitude illness (TO)

**HAI** — acute mountain sickness, **HACE**, **HAPE**

**HAPE** — non-cardiogenic oedema; **exaggerated hypoxic pulmonary vasoconstriction**, capillary "stress failure" · **2–5 days** after arrival · dry cough, exertional dyspnoea → rest dyspnoea, **pink frothy sputum** (advanced) · cyanosis, tachycardia, tachypnoea, fever ≤ 38.5 °C, rales mid-zones · patchy opacities, often **right middle lobe** · rare after **1 wk acclimatisation**

**First step** — **oxygen** (TO answer) · **immediate descent = definitive** · remote, no care → **descent first priority** · portable hyperbaric chamber · **nifedipine 30 mg SR 12-hourly**\
✗ Wrong options: hypoxia chamber · oral nifedipine first

**Prevention** — nifedipine, tadalafil, dexamethasone, **acetazolamide** 500–1000 mg/day 8–12-hourly or 125 mg BD (start **24 h before ascent**)

### Low back pain prevention – core strengthening (TO)

**LBP prevention in athletes** — answer key not provided by source · options: core strengthening, hamstring stretching alone, foot posture correction, intermittent NSAIDs · module filed as **core muscle strengthening**

## X vs Y

| Constant | **Isotonic** | **Isometric** | **Isokinetic** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Held constant | **Tension / force** | **Length** | **Velocity** |
| Relationship shown | Force–velocity | **Length–tension** | Force–velocity |

| | **Iontophoresis** | **Phonophoresis** |
|---|---|---|
| Driver | **Electrical (direct) current** | **Ultrasound** |
