<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hamstring Injuries

*Knee & Sports · 17 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **30% of new lower-extremity injuries in athletes** · ↑ 4% / yr in soccer over \~15 yr

**Sports** — rapid acceleration: **soccer, track and field, football** · sprinters, gymnasts (TO)

**Most common muscle injury in athletes (TO)** — **biceps femoris**\
✗ Wrong options: adductor longus · gastrocnemius · rectus femoris

**Two-joint muscles (TO)** — exposed to acceleration / deceleration forces → **hamstrings, quadriceps, gastrocnemius** most often injured

**Which hamstring (TO)** — **biceps femoris \~83%** > semimembranosus \~12% > semitendinosus \~5%

**Site — adult** — **myotendinous junction (MTJ)** = most common rupture site, sprinting

**Site — skeletally immature** — **ischial tuberosity avulsion** · also **water skiers** · **10% of pelvic avulsion fractures** in the skeletally immature (Orthobullets) · ischial tuberosity **\~50%** of pelvic apophyseal avulsions (OB, Rossi — see AIIS / ASIS chapters) — read the options

### Risk factors

**Previous hamstring injury** — **×6 reinjury risk** (weak scar) → **greatest predictor** of proximal avulsion

**Strength** — **hamstring : quadriceps ratio < 0.6** · side-to-side difference **> 10–15%**

**Other** — inadequate warm-up · **↓ hip extension** (Orthobullets) · ↓ baseline hip flexion (OB) · leg-length difference (shorter leg = tighter hamstrings) · ↑ age, high training demand, ↑ BMI, **tight hip flexors** (OB)

### Anatomy

**4 muscles** — **semimembranosus, semitendinosus, biceps femoris long head + short head** · **gracilis is NOT a hamstring** (pubic origin) (OB) · TO text lists "semitendinosus and gracilis medially, biceps femoris laterally" — read the options

**Origin** — **all from ischial tuberosity except short head** (**linea aspera**)

**Footprint** — **conjoint tendon (ST + BF long head) medial** (inferomedial / posteromedial), **BF more proximal than ST** · **SM = separate, most lateral** (superolateral / anterolateral), slightly proximal · **SM closest to sciatic nerve**

![Ischial tuberosity footprint: BF long head proximal-medial, ST distal-medial, SM lateral](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3102-tuberosity-hamstring-insertion.jpg)

| Muscle | Insertion | Nerve |
|---|---|---|
| Semimembranosus | Posterior **medial tibial condyle** | **Tibial** division of sciatic |
| Semitendinosus | Superomedial tibia — **pes anserine** | **Tibial** division |
| BF long head | **Fibular head** | **Tibial** division |
| **BF short head** | Fibular head, BF long head, lateral capsule | **Common peroneal** division |

**Blood supply** — **inferior gluteal + profunda femoris** arteries

**Distances** — origin **\~6 cm proximal** to inferior border of gluteus maximus · **sciatic nerve 1.2 cm lateral** to origin · **pudendal nerve 2–3 cm superomedial**

**Biomechanics** — cross **hip + knee** (except short head — knee only) · **BF long head + SM** most active in **hip extension** · **ST + BF short head** most active in **knee flexion**

### Muscle regeneration — satellite cells

**Satellite cells** — dormant in muscle until injury → **regenerate skeletal muscle** + new satellite cells\
✗ Wrong options: osteoblast signalling · periosteum · chemotherapy binding · **sonic hedgehog = limb bud**

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanism & origin anatomy

**Hip flexion + knee extension** — **eccentric** contraction at **end of swing phase** (maximal fibre elongation) → **biceps femoris**\
✗ Wrong options: sartorius · rectus femoris · adductor magnus · TFL

**Intramuscular / MTJ injury** — **sudden take-off phase of running** · acute injuries usually **BF**

**Chronic repetitive stretch** — hip flexion + knee extension → **proximal semimembranosus** (OB)

**Older patients** — same hyperflexion in **slips and falls**, water-skiing

**Adolescent hurdler** — ischial tuberosity avulsion = **"hurdler's fracture"** · same mechanism in adult → **MTJ rupture**\
✗ Wrong options: muscle belly · tendinous insertion · ramus fracture · sports hernia

| Hamstring strain (TO) | Type I | Type II |
|---|---|---|
| Mechanism | **High-speed running** | **Slow stretch** — hip flexed + knee extended (**high kicking, splits**) |
| Site | **BF long head, proximal MTJ** | **Semimembranosus near ischial tuberosity** |
| Acute loss of function | **More marked** | Less dramatic |
| Rehabilitation | **Shorter** | **Longer** |

**Tennis leg (TO)** — **medial head of gastrocnemius** torn at muscle belly–aponeurosis junction

## 3 Presentation

**History** — sudden posterior thigh pain running / kicking / jumping ± **"pop"** · **pain with sitting** = proximal avulsion

**Ecchymosis** — posterior thigh → proximal avulsion or high-grade MTJ tear · **latent ecchymosis** appears **\~1 week** after injury (haematoma tracking)

**Palpable mass** — middle ⅓ posterior thigh → **MTJ rupture**

**Gait** — **"stiff-legged"** (avoids hip + knee flexion)

**↑ Popliteal angle** — hip 90°, knee 90° → extend knee slowly → angle at pain vs other side

**Strength** — prone knee flexion at 90° weak · **resisted knee flexion prone painful** (TO)

**Neuro** — may have peroneal weakness (foot drop)

| Provocative test (pain = +) | Method | Sens / spec |
|---|---|---|
| **Puranen-Orava** | Heel on raised surface, reach for toes | 0.76 / 0.82 |
| **Bent-knee stretch** | Supine, hip + knee max flexed, **slow** passive knee extension | 0.84 / 0.87 |
| **Modified bent-knee stretch** | Same, **rapid** full extension | **0.89 / 0.91 — best** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| X-ray: **AP pelvis, AP + lateral femur** | **Bony avulsion of ischial tuberosity** |
| **MRI — gold standard** | **Number of tendons + retraction**, sciatic nerve position (chronic) |
| MRI T1 | ↑ signal = **tendinopathy** |
| MRI T2 | ↑ signal = **partial tear** |
| MRI partial avulsion | **"Sickle sign"** at hamstring origin |
| MRI complete rupture | **Ischial tuberosity denuded of tendon**, haematoma, retraction (e.g. **> 5 cm**) · e.g. BF long head + ST (conjoint) torn on axial |
| US + MRI (TO) | Support diagnosis · **MRI better** for oedema, haemorrhage, tear; BAMIC grading |
| CT | **No indication** (✗ Wrong options: urgent CT) |

![Bony avulsion of the ischial tuberosity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3102-hamstring-avulsion-fracture.jpg)

### Hamstring tear MRI classification

| Grade | MRI |
|---|---|
| 1 | T2 ↑ signal about tendon / muscle, **no fibre disruption** |
| 2 | T2 ↑ signal + fibre disruption **< ½ width** |
| 3 | Fibre disruption **> ½ width** |

### British Athletics Muscle Injury Classification (BAMIC) (TO)

| Location (TO) | Site |
|---|---|
| **a** | **Myofascial** (surface / fascia) |
| **b** | **Muscle–tendon junction** |
| **c** | **Intratendinous** |
| **d** | **Tendon avulsion** |

| Grade (TO) | Injury | Severity |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | No structural damage, soreness | Mild |
| 1 | Small tear, minimal fibre disruption | Mild |
| 2 | Moderate tear, partial fibre disruption | Moderate |
| 3 | Extensive tear | Severe |
| 4 | **Full-thickness, complete rupture** | Very severe |

### Prognosis for return to play (TO)

| Predictor (TO) | Effect |
|---|---|
| **MRI-negative** strain | **Early RTP** |
| **More proximal** (near ischial tuberosity) | **Prolonged RTP** |
| ↑ **craniocaudal length** on MRI | Longer RTP |
| Pain-free walking > 1 day | **4× more likely RTP > 3 weeks** |
| Days to pain-free jogging | **1–2 d → < 2 wk · 3–5 d → > 2 wk · > 5 d → > 4 wk** |
| **Intramuscular conjoint tendon** injury | RTP delayed **3–4 months** |
| Pain on passive SLR, longer tender length | Longer RTP |
| Stopped within 5 min, ↑ max pain | Longer RTP |

## 5 Management

```
Hamstring injury (MRI: tendons + retraction)
├─ MTJ / single tendon / 2 tendons < 2 cm → NONOPERATIVE
├─ 2 tendons > 2 cm, young active → OPEN REPAIR
├─ 3-tendon / complete proximal avulsion → OPEN REPAIR (acute)
├─ Partial avulsion, symptoms > 6 months → REPAIR TO ISCHIAL TUBEROSITY
└─ Bony avulsion → by displacement
       ├─ < 2 cm → PROTECTED WB 4 wk → PT
       └─ > 2 cm or chronic symptomatic → ORIF
```

### Nonoperative care

**Indications** — **most injuries** · **all single-tendon tears** · **2 tendons < 2 cm retraction** · **MTJ rupture** · less active / comorbid

**Regimen** — **rest, ice, NSAIDs, protected WB 4 wk** → stretching + strengthening · heals in **up to 6 wk** · OB: WBAT, gentle stretching, over 4–6 wk\
✗ Wrong options: NWB 6 wk

**Single tendon < 2 cm** — ruptured tendon **scars to intact tendons**

**Return** — strength **≥ 90% of contralateral**

**Modalities** — massage, ultrasound, electrical stimulation

**PRP** — acute strains, **high-level athletes** · **within 24–48 h**, US-guided · RTP **3–5 days earlier** (NFL, low-level data)

### Operative criteria

| Tendon repair | ORIF |
|---|---|
| **Proximal avulsion ruptures** | **Bony avulsion > 2 cm displaced** (fragment big enough for hardware) |
| **Partial avulsion, failed nonop 6 months** | **Chronic symptomatic bony avulsion** |
| **2 tendons > 2 cm retraction, young (< 50 y) active** | |
| **3-tendon tears** (all avulsed) | |

**Complete tears** — nonop → poorer outcomes → **repair** · **acute complete** > chronic partial for improvement after surgery

**Arthroscopy** — no intra-articular pathology · endoscopic repair = case reports only

### Proximal avulsion — surgical indications & repair

**22-yr sprinter, complete proximal avulsion retracted > 5 cm** — **surgical repair first-line**\
✗ Wrong options: ATFL signal = low ankle sprain → rest, ice, early rehab · PCL avulsion → nonop, quadriceps strengthening · partial cuff tear → NSAIDs + PT · compression-sided femoral neck stress fracture < 50% width → activity restriction + NWB

**Hurdler, 3 tendons avulsed (BF, SM, ST)** — **open surgical repair of all tendons to the ischial tuberosity**\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs + NWB 6 wk · stretching · CT · hip arthroscopy

**42-yr, acute 3-tendon tear, massive retraction, no bony fragment** — **primary suture-anchor repair + 4–6 wk PWB ± brace** · bracing optional\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF · Achilles allograft + sciatic neurolysis · PRP + NWB · crutches 6 wk

**Partial avulsion, ischial pain at 6 months** — **repair to ischial tuberosity**\
✗ Wrong options: sciatic decompression · labral repair · repair to intertrochanteric crest / femoral shaft (nonanatomic)

**Approach** — **prone**, knee free to flex · **transverse incision in gluteal crease** → **"T" extension** distally for large retracted / chronic tears · fascia usually intact; vertical fascial incision → haematoma

**Footprint** — scrape with **elevator / curette, avoid burr** (sciatic) · **4–6 suture anchors**, knee flexed

**Chronic, unable to re-approximate** — **Achilles allograft** bridge (≈ acute results, small studies)

**Post-op** — **PWB 4–6 wk, knee flexed 40°** ± knee / hip brace

**ORIF technique** — direct reduction, **partially / fully threaded screws + washers** ± suture anchors / interference screws

**Outcomes** — **80% RTS at 6 months** · complications **up to 23%** (23.17%) · **chronic > acute (< 6 wk)** complications · 91% return of strength after operative repair (OB)

### Surgical nerve risk (sciatic, pudendal)

| Nerve | Position vs ischial tuberosity | Fact |
|---|---|---|
| **Sciatic** | **1.2 cm lateral** | **Most commonly injured** in repair · neurolysis just lateral to hamstring sheath · transient palsy (retraction) |
| **Pudendal** | **2–3 cm superomedial** | **Aggressive Hohmann superomedially** → sexual dysfunction, dyspareunia, impotence, pain sitting / defecating → use glenoid retractors |
| Posterior femoral cutaneous | Lateral, beside sciatic | With inferior gluteal artery deep to gluteus max · transient posterior thigh numbness |
| Inferior gluteal | Below piriformis, under gluteus max | Retracted with gluteus max · 5 cm cephalad to its inferior border |
| Superior gluteal | Far superolateral | Exits **above** piriformis (OB) · "inferior to piriformis, then superior" (other OB) — not in field |
| Nerve to obturator internus | Medial, with pudendal | Rarely encountered |

![Pudendal nerve superomedial and sciatic / PFCN lateral to the hamstring footprint](../../MCQ/attachments/q220108-illb-figure-b.jpg)

### Rehabilitation & recurrence prevention (eccentric training)

| Phase (TO) | Exercise |
|---|---|
| **Acute** (painful) | **Isometric** (no joint movement), ice, compression, elevation, NSAIDs as needed |
| **Subacute** (pain-free walking) | **Isotonic** = concentric + eccentric → **emphasis on eccentric**: **Askling L-protocol + Nordic hamstring curls** |
| Adjunct | **Lumbopelvic stability (balance board)**, adductor + glute strengthening |

**Recurrent hamstring injury, 15-yr footballer (TO)** — **eccentric training**\
✗ Wrong options: balance board · isotonic · isometric

**Nordic hamstring exercise** — kneel, heels held, lean forward to prone + return · **↓ injuries 50–70%**

![Nordic hamstring exercise](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3102-nordic-hamstring-exercise.jpg)

**Prevention** — add abdominal, hip + quadriceps strengthening · **correct hamstring–quadriceps imbalance**

**Recurrent sprinter returning — advise (TO)** — work on **hamstring strength** · assess **hip position** while running · **lumbopelvic control** · false: **"avoid high-speed training"** — sprinter must train at high intensity

**Terminal swing (TO)** — hip max flexion **\~30°** → controlled by **hamstrings + glutes**; hamstrings slow the extending knee before heel strike → **eccentric** load = injury phase · hip adductors switch eccentric → concentric

**Anterior pelvic tilt (TO)** — strains lumbar spine + hamstrings, impairs gluteal function → lumbopelvic control prevents recurrence

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Recurrence** | **Most common — 12–31%** · weakness, H:Q imbalance, premature return |
| Peroneal nerve injury | **Distal non-insertional** injuries · usually self-resolves |
| **Sciatic nerve injury** | **8% of surgical cases** · chronic scarring → **nerve exploration** |
| **Hamstring syndrome** | Buttock / ischial pain after **nonop avulsion**, worse sitting, stretching, sprinting, hurdling, kicking · hamstrings scarred to sciatic → **surgical release + sciatic decompression** |
| **Ischial tuberosity nonunion** | **Bony avulsion > 2 cm treated nonop** → **ORIF ± bone graft** |

**Prognosis** — unpredictable · **84% regain strength, 89% endurance** · poor: **severely retracted**, **chronic scarred to sciatic nerve**

## X vs Y

| | **Adolescent** | **Adult** |
|---|---|---|
| Same hurdling mechanism | **Ischial tuberosity avulsion (hurdler's fracture)** | **MTJ rupture** |
| Surgery | **> 2 cm displacement** | **2 tendons > 2 cm (young) or 3 tendons** |

| | **Sciatic nerve** | **Pudendal nerve** |
|---|---|---|
| Position | **1.2 cm lateral** | **2–3 cm superomedial** |
| Injured by | Lateral retraction, burr, chronic scar | **Superomedial Hohmann** |

## How it's asked

**"Most common muscle injury in athletes (TO)?"** → **biceps femoris**

**"Adolescent hurdler avulsion — adult equivalent?"** → **hamstring myotendinous junction rupture**

**"Hip flexion + knee extension injures?"** → **biceps femoris**

**"Ischial footprint labels 1-2-3 (figure)?"** → **1 = biceps femoris, 2 = semitendinosus, 3 = semimembranosus**

**"Hamstring with a separate ischial attachment?"** → **semimembranosus**

**"Hurdler, MRI 3 tendons avulsed — next step?"** → **open surgical repair**

**"Partial avulsion, ischial pain at 6 months — surgery?"** → **repair to the ischial tuberosity**

**"Which patient gets surgery first-line?"** → **22-yr sprinter with complete proximal hamstring tear**

**"Acute 3-tendon tear, massive retraction?"** → **primary suture anchor repair, PWB 4–6 weeks ± brace**

**"Retractor superomedial to ischial tuberosity — nerve?"** → **pudendal nerve**

**"Nerve most at risk lateral to ischial tuberosity?"** → **sciatic nerve**

**"Role of satellite cells?"** → **regenerate skeletal muscle after injury**

**"Recurrent hamstring injury — training (TO)?"** → **eccentric**

**"Recurrent sprinter — which advice is NOT given (TO)?"** → **avoid training at higher speeds**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hip Arthroscopy

*Knee & Sports · 18 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Overview & indications

**Why hard** — deep joint, **high congruity** (vs knee / shoulder) → steep learning curve

**Growth** — **250% ↑** in US 2007–2011 (Orthobullets) · **88-fold ↑** since late 1990s (OB)

**Advantage** — lower morbidity than open arthrotomy, faster early recovery

| Intra-articular indications | Extra-articular indications |
|---|---|
| FAI lesions, labral tears | **AIIS subspine impingement** |
| AVN — diagnosis + staging | Capsular tears / instability |
| **Loose bodies**, **chondral defects** | **Iliopsoas tendon** pathology |
| **Ligamentum teres** injury | **Gluteal tears**, trochanteric bursal debridement |
| **Synovial disease**, **septic arthritis** | **Ischiofemoral impingement**, **deep gluteal syndrome** |
| Impinging osteophytes, unexplained mechanical symptoms | **Piriformis release**, **proximal hamstring tears** |

**Contraindications (Orthobullets)** — **advanced DJD**, **hip ankylosis**, **acetabular dysplasia**, **joint contracture**, **severe osteoporotic bone**, significant protrusio acetabuli

### Compartments

| Compartment | Traction? | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| **Central** | **Yes** | Acetabular fossa, head articular surfaces, ligamentum teres, rim + labrum, AIIS + capsule |
| **Peripheral** (lateral to labrum) | No | **Head-neck junction**, **iliopsoas tendon**, **zona orbicularis**, medial synovial fold |
| Lateral (peritrochanteric) | No | IT band + bursa, gluteus medius / minimus tendons |
| Deep gluteal space | No | Piriformis + **sciatic nerve**, hamstring origin (ischial tuberosity) |

### Arthroscopic landmarks – zona orbicularis

**Zona orbicularis** — circular ring of capsule · **landmark for iliopsoas** tendon lying **immediately deep** to it\
✗ Wrong options: pectineus · sartorius · adductor brevis · rectus femoris

**Use** — pericapsular **iliopsoas release** for **internal snapping hip** (tendon over iliopectineal eminence or femoral head) · alternative: release at lesser trochanter

**Stability** — proximal–middle capsule incl. zona orbicularis → greatest influence on stability during distraction (Ito)

![Zona orbicularis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3100-zona-orbicularis.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

### Clinical sign of intra-articular pathology (C sign)

**Byrd C sign (TO)** — **index finger over anterior hip + thumb over posterior trochanteric region** → **intra-articular hip pathology**\
✗ Wrong options: knee · ankle · shoulder

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings (pre-arthroscopy screen)

| Measurement | Normal | Implication |
|---|---|---|
| **Tönnis grade** (OA) | 0–1 | **Grade 2–3 → contraindication** (failure, conversion to THA) |
| **LCEA** | **> 25°** (28° normal) | **< 20° → dysplasia** (e.g. 15°) |
| **ACEA** | — | **< 20° → dysplasia** |
| **Tönnis (roof) angle** | **0–10°** (5°, 8° normal) | **> 10° → dysplasia** (e.g. 15°) |
| **Alpha angle** | **≤ 42°** (40° normal) | **> 42° → cam** → treatable, not a contraindication |
| **Head-neck offset ratio** | **≥ 0.17** (0.2 normal) | **< 0.17 → cam** → treatable |

## 5 Management

### Setup, positioning & joint access

**Kit** — **70° arthroscope**, long cannulas + guides, flexible instruments · fluoroscopy + arthroscopy towers both visible

**Position** — **supine or lateral decubitus**, fracture / dedicated table · **well-padded perineal post**

**Traction** — **~50 lb** to distract · Trendelenburg ↓ force needed

**Access** — spinal needle at **AL portal** into central compartment under **fluoroscopy** (avoid head + labrum) → saline distension or venting (air arthrogram) → guidewire, cannula, scope → later portals under direct vision

### Portals & nerve injury (LFCN, sciatic)

| Portal | Location | Use | At risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Anterolateral (AL)** | **2 cm anterior + 2 cm superior** to anterosuperior GT (Orthobullets) · **1 cm proximal + 1 cm anterior** to GT tip (TO) | **First** (fluoroscopy, only blind portal) · **primary viewing** | **Superior gluteal nerve** (> 4 cm proximal, TO) · **chondrolabral injury = biggest risk (TO)** |
| **Anterior (AP)** | Line down from **ASIS** × line across top of GT, moved lateral · ~**6 cm distal to ASIS** through sartorius + rectus femoris (OB) | Central compartment (**4:30–8:30 o'clock**) | **LFCN** + **lateral femoral circumflex artery** · femoral NV bundle if medial |
| **Distal anterolateral (DALA)** | **3–5 cm distal to AL** · traction off, neutral or **45° flexion** | Peripheral compartment (neck) | **Ascending branch LFCA** |
| Mid-anterior (MAP) | Between anterior and AL | As anterior | — |
| **Posterolateral (PL)** | **1 cm posterior + 1 cm proximal** to posterosuperior GT tip (Orthobullets, TO) · **2–3 cm posterior** to GT tip (OB) · **neutral rotation** | Posterior joint | **Sciatic nerve** (2.9 cm) · deep branch **MFCA** (> 1 cm) (TO) |

![Hip arthroscopy portals — AP, ALP, PLP relative to ASIS and greater trochanter](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3100-portals.jpg)

**LFCN distance from anterior portal** — **within 3 mm** (TO) · **15.4 mm** average (OB, Robertson) — read the options

**LFCA distance (TO)** — femoral nerve + ascending LFCA ~**4 cm** · terminal LFCA branches **as close as 2 mm** · transverse LFCA branch **10.1 mm** (OB)

**LFCN at portal level** — divided into **≥ 3 branches** · runs from inguinal ligament along sartorius, variable branching

**Protect LFCN** — **"nick and spread"**: skin-only incision + blunt dissection · stay **lateral to vertical line from ASIS** (femoral artery / vein safe)

**Medialised anterior portal** — no better view · ↑ risk to **femoral vessels + LFCN** (Thorey) · too medial → **femoral nerve**

**Anterolateral thigh numbness** — **LFCN from anterior portal** · AL / distal lateral portals can also hurt LFCN but anterior = greatest risk\
✗ Wrong options: AL portal → SGN · traction → pudendal · fluid extravasation · orthosis

**Sciatic nerve + rotation** — **ER brings sciatic closer** to PL portal → never ER during entry; IR moves it away (OB, Orthobullets) · ↑ risk with **IR or ER** (TO) — read the options

**Standard central portals** — AL, anterior, PL (Byrd) · reference for all others

### Traction-related complications (pudendal nerve)

**Complications mostly due to** — **traction**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral positioning · supine positioning · DVT · HO

**Pudendal nerve** — **commonest neurovascular complication** · compression by **perineal post** → **numb inner thigh + hemiscrotum / groin**\
✗ Wrong options: obturator (no scrotal supply) · LFCN (anterolateral thigh) · pudendal from DALA portal

**Pudendal rates** — **1.8%** (62/3405), **4.3% with post** + longer traction · all resolve 6 wk–3 mo (Habib) · **2%** sensory neuralgia, resolve 3 wk–6 mo (Pailhe)

**Prevent pudendal** — limit traction force + time, padded post **> 8–10 cm** diameter, **post-less** surgery

**Peroneal nerve** — traction or ill-fitting boot → **dorsum of foot** · prevent: **intermittent traction release**, **total traction < 2 h**, **adequate muscle paralysis**

**Overall rates** — complications **3.3%**, nerve injury **0.9%** → **pudendal + LFCN** commonest (Nakano) · Byrd 10-yr series: only 2 complications

**Obturator nerve** — medial thigh sensation + adductors · deep + medial to AL portal → not at risk

### Operative criteria — contraindications (arthritis, dysplasia, age)

| Factor | Contraindication / poor prognosis | Not a contraindication |
|---|---|---|
| Arthritis | **Tönnis grade 2–3** | Acetabular chondrosis (treat at scope) |
| Dysplasia | **LCEA < 20°**, **ACEA < 20°**, **Tönnis angle > 10°** | LCEA 28°, Tönnis angle 5–8° |
| Cam | — | **Alpha 60°**, offset ratio 0.2 (treat it) |
| Age | > 60 → conversion to THA | **< 40 → positive** prognostic (27, 25 yr) |
| Smoking | ↑ poor outcome | **Not** a contraindication |
| Tear site | — | **Anterosuperior** (commonest) |

**Dysplasia + isolated labral repair** — fails (primary cause unaddressed) → **PAO ± arthroscopic labral repair**

**Dysplasia outcomes** — failure **29% vs 2.5%** FAI-only (Vahedi) · **32.2%** failure (Larson) → avoid even mild–moderate dysplasia

**Failed arthroscopy (Bogunovic)** — **37% OA, 43% under-treated FAI, 15% dysplasia** (one OB) · **residual FAI 68%, dysplasia 24%** (another OB) — all visible on preop imaging

**ANCHOR / Clohisy** — standardised radiographs, always read with history + exam

### Conversion to THA after arthroscopy

| Risk factor (Kester) | Odds ratio |
|---|---|
| **Obesity (BMI > 30)** | **5.6** |
| **Age > 60** | **3.4** — most predictive in a 64-yr-old (✗ Wrong options: BMI 27) |
| Hip OA | 2.4 |
| Surgeon **< 40 cases / yr** | 1.9 |
| Tobacco | 1.9 |
| Female | 1.8 |

**Kester** — conversion **5.9%** at 14.7 mo · ipsilateral knee OA not a risk factor

**Degen** — revision **13.2%** at 1.7 yr · THA **9.6%** · OA HR 2.72, **age > 50** HR 2.09, loose body removal HR 1.61 · protective: non-white HR 0.74, surgeon **> 164 cases / yr** HR 0.42

**Age threshold** — **> 50–60** depending on study · protective: male, non-white, high-volume surgeon

### Rehabilitation

**Protocol** — preop PT · foot-flat partial WB → full WB · **early ROM** · strengthening after full ROM · **iliopsoas tendinitis common**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Cause / risk | Prevention / Rx |
|---|---|---|
| **Iatrogenic chondrolabral injury** | **Commonest reported complication** · scope / instruments (AL portal blind) | Instruments under direct vision |
| **Instability** | Capsular insufficiency · imprecise capsulotomy, **no capsular closure** | Careful capsulotomy, **closure / plication** |
| **Traction neurapraxia** | **Pudendal** (post), **peroneal** (boot / traction) | Post-less, < 2 h, intermittent release |
| **Portal nerve injury** | SGN (AL), **LFCN (anterior)**, **sciatic (PL, ER)**, ascending LFCA (DALA) | Lateral portals, nick and spread |
| **Fluid extravasation** | Long surgery, high pump pressure · rare **abdominal compartment syndrome** | Low pressure · **iliopsoas tenotomy last** |
| **Heterotopic ossification** | — | **Routine postop NSAIDs** |
| Conversion to THA | Obesity, age > 60, female, tobacco, low-volume surgeon, OA | Patient selection |

| "Most common" (read the stem) | Answer |
|---|---|
| Reported complication overall | **Iatrogenic chondral injury** (Orthobullets, OB) |
| Complications related to | **Traction** (OB) |
| Neurovascular complication | **Pudendal nerve** (traction / post) |
| Nerve injuries (Nakano) | **Pudendal + LFCN** |
| Portal-related nerve | **LFCN** (anterior portal) |
| Postop dislocation | **Anterior** |

### Postoperative dislocation

**Pattern** — **anterior** dislocation commonest after arthroscopy · hip in **extension + ER** (Duplantier)

**Management** — **immediate closed reduction → restrict extension + ER**\
✗ Wrong options: emergent ORIF (only irreducible / fracture) · restrict flexion + IR (posterior precautions) · 24 h traction first · vascular surgery consult

**Sequel** — progressive arthritis possible (Wylie)

## X vs Y

| Numb area after hip scope | Nerve | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| **Anterolateral thigh** | **LFCN** | **Anterior portal** |
| **Groin / perineum / hemiscrotum / inner thigh** | **Pudendal** | **Perineal post** compression |
| Dorsum of foot | Peroneal | Traction / boot |
| Abductor weakness | Superior gluteal | AL portal |
| Posterior thigh / foot | Sciatic | PL portal in ER |
| Medial thigh (no scrotum) | Obturator | Not portal-related |

## How it's asked

**"Lateral upper-thigh numbness after hip scope — which portal (TO)?"** → **anterior**

**"Anterior portal — 2 structures at maximum risk (TO)?"** → **LFCN + lateral femoral circumflex artery**

**"Sciatic nerve most at risk with?"** → **posterior peritrochanteric portal with limb in external rotation**

**"Numbness in yellow zone after 3 portals?"** → **LFCN from portal A (anterior)**

**"Anterior portal — structure at greatest risk?"** → **lateral femoral cutaneous nerve**

**"Anterolateral thigh numbness — which part of surgery?"** → **use of the AP (anterior) portal**

**"Hip arthroscopy complications mostly related to?"** → **use of traction**

**"Numb inner thigh + hemiscrotum after scope with post?"** → **pudendal nerve, direct compression**

**"General contraindication to hip arthroscopy?"** → **Tönnis grade 3**

**"64-yr-old converted to THA — strongest preop predictor?"** → **age > 60**

**"Contraindication to isolated arthroscopic labral repair?"** → **LCEA 15°**

**"Dysplasia sign contraindicating isolated repair?"** → **Tönnis angle 15°**

**"Zona orbicularis is the landmark for?"** → **iliopsoas**

**"Dislocation in recovery, hip extended + ER?"** → **immediate closed reduction, restrict extension + ER**

**"Byrd C sign (TO)?"** → **intra-articular hip pathology**

# Hip Labral Tear

*Knee & Sports · 19 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & etiology

**Commonest association** — **acetabular dysplasia = highest incidence** (Orthobullets, OB) · **FAI = most common cause** of labral pathology (another OB) — read the options

**Who** — all ages · commonly **active females**

**Site** — **anterosuperior** (chondrolabral junction) — low collagen content + fibres parallel to chondrolabral junction · FAI tears 10–2 o'clock

**Asymptomatic** — **69%** of asymptomatic adults have a labral tear on MRI · 73% some hip pathology (Register) → a tear alone ≠ indication for surgery

**Causes** — **FAI**, **dysplasia** (floppy / redundant / **enlarged** labrum), **trauma** (dislocation / subluxation), **capsular laxity** (↑ translation), **degeneration** (edge loading)

**Effect** — tear ↓ resistance to motion → **initially relative hip instability** ✓ · commonest associated lesion: **chondral injury**

**Traps** — tears **are** related to DJD (✗ Wrong options: unrelated) · occur **with or without** abnormal morphology (✗ Wrong options: only with)\
✗ Wrong options: caused by ligamentum teres lesions · posteroinferior quadrant

**Dysplasia vs FAI labrum (Leunig)** — both tear **anterosuperior** · labrum **enlarged** in 12 dysplastic hips, none with FAI → anterosuperior rim = initial fatigue site

**Consequence** — ↑ friction, cartilage consolidation + strain → accelerated degeneration (Safran)

### Anatomy

**Shape** — horseshoe, **continuous with transverse acetabular ligament**

**Two parts** — articular = **fibrocartilage** · capsular = dense connective tissue

**Vascularity** — from capsule + synovium at acetabular margin → **only peripheral ⅓ vascularised**

**Innervation** — **highly innervated** (mechanoreceptors + nociceptors) · **nerve to quadratus femoris** + **obturator nerve**

**Function** — adds **22% surface area** + **33% volume** · **negative-pressure suction seal** → retains synovial fluid, frictionless motion

![Acetabular labrum and transverse acetabular ligament](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3097-labrum-body.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — mechanical **pain + snapping** (internal snapping), **locking**, vague groin pain · pain with sitting (anterior pathology)

| Provocative test | Manoeuvre |
|---|---|
| **Anterior labral tear** | **Flexed + ER + abducted → extension + IR + adduction** (painful catch) |
| **Posterior labral tear** | **Flexed + adducted + IR → abduction + ER + extension** |
| FADIR | Positive in **AS chondrolabral tear** and cam FAI |

**Intra-articular steroid relief** — diagnostic + therapeutic · confirms intra-articular source\
✗ Wrong options: sacroiliitis

**Distractors** — FNSF: no mechanical symptoms, axial-load pain, not linked to dysplasia · trochanteric bursitis: lateral GT tenderness · AVN: risk factors

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| Radiographs | Exclude other pathology · **dysplasia**, arthritis, acetabular cysts |
| ANCHOR views (Clohisy) | AP pelvis, cross-table lateral, **45° + 90° Dunn**, frog-leg, false profile |
| **MR arthrogram** | **Study of choice / gold standard** · **92% sensitive** (Orthobullets) · **> 90%** (OB) |
| MRA finding | **Dye extravasation into labrum** · fluid deep to anterior chondrolabral junction |
| MRA + injection | Intra-articular **lidocaine + steroid** → diagnostic + therapeutic |

**FAI triad on MRA** — ↓ head-neck concavity → **↑ alpha angle + chondrolabral tear** · impingement + no crossover → **abnormal alpha angle + chondrolabral tear**\
✗ Wrong options: acetabular retroversion · HO · AS · coxa vara

**Normal values in stems** — **alpha 40° normal** (> 42° cam) · **head-neck offset ratio 0.20 normal** (< 0.17 cam)

![MR arthrogram — labral tear](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3097-mri-arthrogram.jpg)

### Konan classification (acetabular chondral lesions)

| Element | Grading |
|---|---|
| Cartilage grade | **0** normal → **4** full-thickness defect, exposed bone |
| Site A | **< ⅓** of rim-to-cotyloid-fossa distance |
| Site B | **⅓–⅔** |
| Site C | **> ⅔** |
| Reliability | Intra-observer **0.81** · inter-observer **0.88** |

## 5 Management

```
Hip labral tear
├─ All patients first → rest, NSAIDs, PT, steroid injection
└─ Failed nonop → hip arthroscopy (+ treat FAI)
       ├─ full-thickness tear at labral-chondral junction → labral repair
       ├─ tear not amenable to repair → labral debridement
       └─ irreparable labrum → labral reconstruction
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **Nonoperative** | **Initial treatment for all** | No long-term data |
| **Arthroscopic repair** | Failed nonop + **full-thickness tear at labral-chondral junction** | Better healing + function than partial labrectomy |
| Arthroscopic debridement | Failed nonop + tear **not amenable to repair** | **70–85%** short-term relief |
| Labral reconstruction | Failed nonop + **irreparable** labrum | **Allograft ≈ autograft** PROMs |

### Treatment – arthroscopic repair

**Failed rest, PT, NSAIDs + 1-day injection relief** — **arthroscopic treatment of labral tear ± FAI**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat injection · pinning (no fracture) · loose body removal · PAO (no dysplasia)

**Address cause** — both labrum and FAI bone must be treated for symptomatic gain · AP pelvis may show retroversion

**Repair technique** — **knotless suture anchors** preserve labral anatomy

**Debridement** — remove unstable labrum + synovitis · **limited WB × 4 wk** · **flexion + abduction limited 4–6 wk**

**Approach (Matsuda)** — open dislocation, mini-open and arthroscopy all improve pain + function · open → **highest complication rate** · mini-open → **LFCN injury** · all-arthroscopic ≥ others, fewest major complications

## X vs Y

| | **Dysplasia labrum** | **FAI labrum** |
|---|---|---|
| Labrum | **Hypertrophic / floppy / enlarged** | Normal size, crushed / separated |
| Site | Anterosuperior | Anterosuperior |
| Bony fix | **PAO** | Osteoplasty |

## How it's asked

**"Avid runner, MRA dye in labrum?"** → **labral tear**

**"Impingement test +, no crossover — MRI shows?"** → **abnormal alpha angle + chondrolabral tear**

**"True statement about hip labral tears?"** → **initially lead to relative hip instability**

**"Dysplasia, snapping, pain flexed-ER-abducted → extended-IR-adducted?"** → **labral tear**

**"FADIR pain, no axial-load pain — which image?"** → **MRA with anterior labral tear (fluid deep to chondrolabral junction)**

**"Failed PT, NSAIDs, rest, brief injection relief?"** → **arthroscopic treatment of labral tear and possible FAI**

# History and Physical Exam of the Knee

*Knee & Sports · 20 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Fabella anatomy

**Fabella (TO)** — accessory sesamoid ossicle **almost always in lateral head of gastrocnemius**, rarely medial head (can be in both) · **\~20% (10–30%)** of population · **bony or cartilaginous**

**Articulation (TO)** — **often articulates directly with lateral femoral condyle** → "no bony attachment" is the FALSE statement

**Function (TO)** — stabilisation of the medial femoral condyle (as stated in source) + **fabella complex**: plantaris, gastrocnemius, arcuate, **fabellofibular**, fabellopopliteal, oblique popliteal ligaments

### Normal values

**ROM** — **10° recurvatum → 130° flexion** · rotation minimal in extension · **at 90°: 45° ER, 30° IR** · ab / adduction 0° in extension, few degrees at 30°

**Quadriceps girth** — measured at **10 cm (VMO)** + **15 cm (quadriceps)**

**Tibial step-off** — medial plateau **\~1 cm anterior** to MFC at 90°

## 3 Presentation

### History & exam by lesion

| Lesion | History | Exam |
|---|---|---|
| **ACL** | Non-contact, awkward landing, **"pop"**, **immediate swelling** | **Lachman**, pivot shift, large haemarthrosis |
| **PCL** | **Dashboard**, fall on **plantarflexed** foot, posterior pain | **Sag**, **posterior drawer (90°)**, quad active |
| MCL | Blow to outside of knee, medial pain | Valgus instability |
| LCL | Varus injury, lateral pain | Varus instability |
| **PLC** | Lateral + posterior pain, usually combined | **Dial + at 30°** |
| **Meniscus** | **Mechanical** (catching, locking), joint line pain, **delayed swelling** | **Joint line tenderness**, McMurray |
| **Patella** | Fall on **dorsiflexed** foot, **2 "pops"**, anterior pain, stairs | Apprehension, **MPFL tenderness**, effusion, crepitus, ↑ Q angle |

**Inspection / palpation** — scars, swelling, quadriceps atrophy, gait (antalgia, stride), standing alignment · joint line, pes bursa, patellar tendon, ITB · effusion: ballot, milking

### Neurovascular exam

| Nerve | Sensation | Motor |
|---|---|---|
| Obturator | Medial thigh | Thigh adduction |
| Femoral | Anterior thigh | Knee extension |
| Sciatic | Posterolateral calf | Knee flexion |
| **Peroneal** | **Dorsal foot** | **Toe extension** |
| Tibial | Plantar foot | Toe flexion |

**Vascular** — popliteal, DP, PT pulses · **ABI < 0.9 abnormal**

### ACL tests

**Lachman** — **most sensitive** test · **PCL tear → "false" Lachman** (posterior subluxation)

| Lachman grade | Translation |
|---|---|
| **1** | **< 5 mm** |
| **2 A / B** | **5–10 mm** |
| **3 A / B** | **> 10 mm** |
| A / B | **A = firm endpoint**, **B = no endpoint** |

**Pivot shift** — **extension → flexion**, reduces at **20–30°** · needs full relaxation (easier under anaesthesia) · mimics giving way

**KT-1000** — quantifies anterior laxity · slight flexion, **10–30° ER**

**Gait** — **quadriceps avoidance** (doesn't actively extend)

### PCL tests

**Posterior drawer (90°)** — **most accurate** for PCL

**Posterior sag** — hips + knees 90°, ankles supported → tibia sags vs other side

**Quadriceps active test** — quad contraction at 90° → **anterior reduction of tibia** = +

### Collateral tests

| Stress | 30° only | 0° + 30° |
|---|---|---|
| **Valgus** | **Isolated MCL** | **MCL + ACL and / or PCL** |
| **Varus** | **Isolated LCL** | **LCL + ACL and / or PCL** |

**Varus opening + ↑ ER at 30°** — **LCL + PLC**

**MCL grade (opening)** — **I 0–5 mm · II 6–10 mm · III 11–15 mm** (this topic) · 1–4 / 5–9 / ≥ 10 mm (MCL topic exam) — read the options

**Anterior drawer, tibia in ER** — positive → associated injury · **grade III MCL often with ACL + posteromedial corner** tears

### Posterolateral corner – dial test

| Test | Technique | Positive means |
|---|---|---|
| **Dial test** | ER asymmetry at 30° + 90° | **> 10° at 30° only → isolated PLC** · **30° + 90° → PLC + PCL** |
| Varus stress | 0° vs 30° | 0° → LCL + cruciate · 30° → LCL |
| **Posterolateral drawer** | Hip 45°, knee 80°, foot **ER 15°**, posterior drawer + ER | ↑ posterolateral translation |
| **Reverse pivot shift** | Knee **90°**, ER + valgus, **extend** | Tibia reduces with clunk at **\~20°** (ITB flexor → extensor) |
| **ER recurvatum** | Suspend leg by toes, supine | Leg falls into ER + recurvatum |
| Gait | — | **Varus thrust** or hyperextension thrust |
| Peroneal nerve | — | ↓ dorsal foot sensation, weak dorsiflexion |

**Dial threshold** — **> 10°** (Orthobullets) · 10–15° (OB) · 15° (TO) — see PCL / PLC chapters

**Reverse pivot direction** — 90° → extension (Orthobullets) · extension → flexion (TO, PLC chapter) — read the options

![External rotation recurvatum test](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3003-hughston-test.jpg)

**Isolated PLC (OB)** — blow from medial side, lateral tenderness, Lachman **1A**, normal posterior drawer, **20° ↑ ER at 30°**, symmetric at 90° → **PLC reconstruction** (repair or reconstruction in high-level athlete)\
✗ Wrong options: BTB ACL · ACL + PLC · PCL · PCL + PLC

**Veltri (OB)** — isolated PCL **< 10 mm** posterior laxity → **nonop** · **> 10 mm or combined** → reconstruct

### Meniscus tests

**Findings** — **joint line tenderness** · effusion

**McMurray** — flex knee, hand on medial side, **externally rotate**, extend → palpable pop / click → **medial meniscus** tear

### Patella tests

**Haemarthrosis** — large · **absent swelling → ligamentous laxity / habitual** dislocation

**Tenderness** — **medial (MPFL)**

**Passive translation** — quadrants (midline = 0) vs other side · normal **< 2 quadrants** · medial border to lateral trochlear edge = **2 = abnormal**

**J sign** — excessive lateral translation in extension, "pops" into groove in early flexion → **patella alta**

**Also** — apprehension · ↑ **Q angle**

![Q angle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3003-q-angle-illustration-aafp-moved.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"ER 20° ↑ at 30°, symmetric at 90°, normal drawer and Lachman — surgery?"** → **posterolateral corner reconstruction**

**"NOT true about fabella (TO)?"** → **no bony attachment (it articulates with lateral femoral condyle)**

**"Fabella usual site (TO)?"** → **lateral head of gastrocnemius (rarely medial, can be both)**

**"Fabella composition (TO)?"** → **bony or cartilaginous**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Idiopathic Chondromalacia Patellae

*Knee & Sports · 21 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & pathophysiology

**Who** — **adolescents + young adults** · **F > M** · PF pain = **most common condition affecting adolescents** (OB) · mainly adolescent females

**Pain generator** — unclear, **multifactorial** · roughened / damaged **undersurface cartilage of patella** · biologic, mechanical + emotional causes (OB)

**Proposed factors** — limb malalignment, muscle weakness, chondral lesions, **maltracking** (narrow contact points → **↑ PF contact pressure → pain**) · **dynamic valgus** = excessive medial movement of limb in weight bearing (OB)

**Pain receptors** — **anterior fat pad + joint capsule = highest potential** · subchondral bone weak

**Miserable malalignment** — **femoral anteversion + genu valgum + external tibial torsion / pronated feet** → ↑ Q-angle · assess tibial + femoral rotation

### Associations & aetiology (TO)

**Associated with chondromalacia (TO)** — **rheumatoid arthritis**, **alkaptonuria**, **recurrent haemarthrosis**

**EXCEPT (TO)** — **ankylosing spondylitis**

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **diffuse peripatellar / retropatellar**, insidious, vague · always weigh physical, mental + social elements

**Aggravated by** — **climbing or descending stairs** · **theatre sign** (prolonged sitting, knee bent — long car rides, back seat) · **squatting / kneeling** · rising from a deep seat

**Exam** — **pain on patellar compression** with ROM or **resisted (open-chain) knee extension** · crepitus · quads atrophy · full ROM, **no effusion**, stable ligaments

**Maltracking signs** — ↑ femoral anteversion / tibial ER, lateral subluxation or **loss of medial mobility**, positive apprehension

**Differential** — quadriceps or patellar tendinitis, **saphenous neuroma**, post-operative neuroma

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| X-ray (AP, lateral, notch ± Merchant) | Often **normal** · chondrosis, shallow sulcus, patella alta / baja, **lateral tilt** |
| **Merchant view** | Knee **45° flexion**, beam **30°** · lateral tilt = possible contributor, **not specific** for PF pain |
| CT | PF alignment, fracture, trochlear geometry, **TT-TG**, limb torsion · TT-TG CT is for **recurrent dislocation** |
| **MRI** | **Best for articular cartilage** · **T2** best · abnormal cartilage = **high signal** · only if intra-articular / intra-osseous lesion suspected |

### Outerbridge MRI classification

| Grade | Cartilage |
|---|---|
| 0 | Normal |
| **I** | Surface intact, heterogeneous, **high signal** |
| **II** | Fissures + fragmentation extending down to articular surface |
| **III** | **Partial-thickness defect**, focal ulceration |
| **IV** | **Exposed subchondral bone** |

![Chondromalacia of the patella on axial MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3022-patella-chondro-grade-ii.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Chondromalacia / PF pain
├─ All patients → rest, NSAIDs, quads (VMO) + core + hip PT, ≥ 1 yr
├─ Failed rehab + Outerbridge 2–3 → arthroscopic debridement
└─ Tight lateral retinaculum + lateral tilt → lateral retinacular release
       (severe, failed extensive PT → realignment osteotomy / MPFLR)
```

### Physical therapy – quadriceps, core & hip strengthening

**Mainstay** — rest, rehab, NSAIDs for **minimum 1 year** · **NSAIDs > steroids** · activity modification

**Rehab focus** — **VMO**, **core**, **closed-chain short-arc quads**, **hip external rotators** · low-impact aerobic exercise

**15-yr-old runner, 5 wk, normal X-rays** — **PT focused on quadriceps and core strengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: FAI hip series · CT for TT-TG · Graston / ASTYM / iontophoresis (chronic tendinopathy) · chest CT + oncology

**Failed 4 wk closed-chain PT** — **continued PT with addition of hip exercises**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid injection · MRI · diagnostic arthroscopy · patellar taping (adjuvant only)

**Exercise that ↑ pain (TO)** — **leg extension** = open chain against resistance in functional range → ↑ contact pressure\
✗ Wrong options: leg press · squat · lateral step

**Best quads exercises (TO)** — **closed kinetic chain** (mini-squat, lateral step, high-saddle cycling) in **0–30°** (minimal pressure) · **leg press tolerated** · mini-squat + **hip adduction (ball)** favours VMO

**Least PF stress (TO)** — **CKC restricted to 0–45° + OKC restricted to 90–45°**\
✗ Wrong options: OKC full 90–0° · CKC 60–90° + OKC 30–0° · deep squats 0–90°

### Operative options

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Arthroscopic debridement** (mechanical, radiofrequency) | **Outerbridge grade 2–3** chondromalacia |
| **Lateral retinacular release** (open or arthroscopic) | **Tight lateral retinaculum, loose medial capsule, lateral tilt** |
| **Maquet** (anterior tubercle elevation) | Elevate **≤ 1 cm** → else **skin necrosis** |
| **Fulkerson** (anteromedialisation) | ↑ Q-angle, instability (controversial) · not with **superior medial arthrosis** (scope first) or skeletal immaturity |
| Elmslie-Trillat, MPFL reconstruction | Severe symptoms after failed extensive PT |

## X vs Y

| | **Closed kinetic chain** | **Open kinetic chain** |
|---|---|---|
| Examples | Leg press, mini-squat, lateral step | **Leg extension** |
| Safe PF arc | **0–45°** (best 0–30°) | **90–45°** |
| PF pain | Tolerated | **↑ pain** (full-range resisted extension) |

## How it's asked

**"Exercise likely to increase PF pain (TO)?"** → **leg extension**

**"PFJ-sparing quads prescription (TO)?"** → **CKC 0–45° + OKC 90–45°**

**"Associated with chondromalacia EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **ankylosing spondylitis**

**"Adolescent, theatre sign, normal X-rays — next?"** → **PT: quadriceps + core strengthening**

**"Failed closed-chain PT, normal sunrise views?"** → **continue PT, add hip exercises**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Iliac Crest Contusion (Hip Pointer)

*Knee & Sports · 22 / 61*

## 1 Basics

**Hip pointer** — **contusion / traumatic haematoma over the iliac wing** · direct blow or crush in **contact sports** (hockey player checked into the boards)

**Haematoma** — around iliac wing → may bleed into **hip abductor muscles**

**Muscles from the crest (any can be involved)** — sartorius, TFL, **gluteus medius**, abdominals (transversus, obliques)

**Adolescent** — **rule out iliac apophysis avulsion** (see ASIS chapter)

## 3 Presentation

**Exam** — **point tenderness over iliac crest** · **contusion + haematoma** · hip weakness · **↓ ROM**

![Hip pointer: bruising and haematoma over the iliac crest](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3090-hip-1-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray + MRI** — usually **unremarkable** · MRI may show a **large haematoma**

## 5 Management

**Nonoperative (mainstay)** — **ice, compression, rest** (extended, until symptoms settle), NSAIDs ± muscle relaxants

**Therapy** — stretch muscles about the crest · **leg on maximum stretch (extension)**\
✗ Wrong options: flexion (that is quadriceps contusion)

**Corticosteroid injection** — near the crest, **some advocate**

**Return to play** — **extra padding** after acute phase

## How it's asked

**"Hockey check, point tender iliac crest, bruise (photo)?"** → **hip pointer**\
✗ Wrong options: hip flexor strain · apophysitis · piriformis syndrome · trochanteric bursitis

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Iliotibial Band Friction Syndrome

*Knee & Sports · 23 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **2–15%** of knee overuse injuries · **most common cause of lateral knee pain in runners + cyclists** (TO)

**Who** — **runners, cyclists**, repetitive flexion–extension · first seen in **US Marine recruits (1975)**; skiers, hockey, basketball, soccer (TO) · **slightly more common in women** (TO)

### Anatomy

**Origin** — continuation of **tensor fascia lata** (Orthobullets) · distal fascial continuation of **TFL + gluteus medius + gluteus maximus** (TO)

**Insertion** — **Gerdy tubercle** (lateral tibial plateau) + partly **lateral femoral supracondylar ridge** (TO) · runs **superficial to vastus lateralis**

**Iliopatellar band (TO)** — anterior extension to lateral patella → **prevents medial patellar translation**

**Innervation** — **superior gluteal nerve (L4, L5, S1)** · synergists = **hip abductors**

**Function (TO)** — **knee extensor < 30° flexion**, **flexor > 30°**; moves **posterior** to lateral epicondyle with flexion · anterolateral stabiliser (OB)

![Iliotibial band over the lateral femoral condyle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3019-itband4.jpg)

### Pathophysiology

**Mechanism** — ITB shifts forward + back over **lateral femoral condyle / epicondyle** → friction, tension, inflammation · also compression + bursal inflammation (OB)

**Impingement zone** — **30° of knee flexion** = knee angle at **foot strike** (TO)

**Pathoanatomy** — compression / irritation of tissue **deep to ITB** (TO: highly innervated **fat pad** under ITB) · cysts or **bursitis of lateral synovial recess** · condylar bone oedema · **ITB itself rarely abnormal**

**Associated** — **patellofemoral syndrome** (tight ITB) · **medial compartment OA** (↓ medial joint space → varus) · **greater trochanteric pain syndrome**

## 2 Mechanism

### Presentation & running risk factors

| Risk factors | Orthobullets | TO |
|---|---|---|
| **Training** | **Sudden ↑ intensity / mileage**, poor shoe support | Tilted surfaces, hills, abrupt training change, **downhill running** |
| **Anatomy** | **Genu varum / recurvatum**, LLD, **excessive foot pronation**, **weak hip abductors**, **tight ITB** | Tight ITB + weak hip abductors |
| **Biomechanics** | Quads–hamstring imbalance, ↑ landing force, **↑ knee flexion at heel strike** | **↑ hip adduction**, **↑ knee IR** + IR velocity, **↑ knee flexion at heel strike**, **↑ maximal foot inversion** |

**Foot conflict** — **excessive pronation** (Orthobullets) vs **↑ maximal foot inversion** (TO) — read the options

**Hockey player, lateral knee pain on running (TO)** — **increased knee flexion at heel strike**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ foot eversion · ↑ knee external rotation · ↑ hip abduction

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **activity-related lateral knee pain** in endurance athletes (e.g. **10 min into a run**) · worse with terrain / mileage change, **downhill** · relieved by rest

**Site** — **lateral femoral condyle / epicondyle** · tenderness **proximal to lateral joint line** (TO) · can be **insertional at Gerdy tubercle** (OB)

**26 yr woman, no trauma, pain after downhill running, normal exam (TO)** — **ITB syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral meniscus tear · LCL tear · lateral hamstring strain

**Exam** — swelling over ITB, **foot + knee malalignment**, crepitus, ↓ hip / knee motion, **weak hip abduction**, **pain on single-leg squat**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis — provocative tests

| Test | Technique | Positive |
|---|---|---|
| **Ober** (Orthobullets) | **Side-lying, symptomatic side up, knee 90°**; hip from **flexion + abduction → extension + adduction** | **Pain, tightness or clicking** over ITB |
| Ober (OB) | Lying on unaffected side; leg **abducted + extended** | **Can't adduct** the leg |
| Ober (TO) | Lateral recumbent, knees 90°; examiner abducts + extends hip, then adducts | **Restriction + lateral knee pain** |
| **Noble compression** (TO) | Supine, **knee 90°**; press **lateral epicondyle** proximal to joint line; extend knee (+ hip) to 0° | **Pain at \~30° flexion** — **specific for ITBFS** |

![Ober test positioning](../../MCQ/attachments/q217836-illa-ober-test.jpg)

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| X-ray (AP, lateral ± oblique / skyline) | **Usually normal**; may show **medial JSN**, patellar malalignment, fracture |
| **MRI** | Rule out **lateral meniscus tear, LCL injury**; signal in **lateral synovial recess**, ITB or periosteum · **↑ T2 signal deep to ITB at lateral epicondyle + ITB thickening** · **negative MRI does not exclude ITBS** |
| US / MRI (TO) | **ITB thickening** |

### Differential — other lateral knee tests

| Finding | Means |
|---|---|
| **Flexion/abduction → extension/adduction pain (Ober)** | **ITB syndrome** |
| Pop on rotation + extension (McMurray) | Meniscal tear |
| Varus laxity at 30° | **Isolated LCL** (LCL inserts on **fibula**, not Gerdy) |
| ER asymmetry **> 10° at 30°** (dial) | **PLC** (LCL, popliteus, popliteofibular ligament) |
| **"Clunk"**, IR + valgus, extension → flexion (pivot shift) | **ACL**; reduction at **20–30°** |

## 5 Management

```
ITB friction syndrome
├─ Acute → REST, ICE, NSAIDs (oral / topical), activity modification
│      └─ fails → CORTICOSTEROID INJECTION (ITB bursa)
├─ Then → PT: ITB / gluteal stretching, HIP ABDUCTOR strengthening, foam roller
└─ Refractory > 6 months → SURGERY (release, excision, Z-plasty, bursectomy)
```

### Diagnosis (Ober test) & stretching

**Initial treatment** — **home stretching program + cross training**\
✗ Wrong options: bursectomy · Z-lengthening · partial ITB excision

**PT** — stretch **ITB, lateral fascia, gluteals** · deep transverse friction massage · **hip abductor strengthening** · proprioception · **myofascial release / foam roller** (TO) · strengthening **after** inflammation settles

**Training changes** — **change shoes every 300–500 miles** · avoid sudden mileage ↑

**Return to running (TO)** — wk 1: alternate days, **flat surface** → daily fast-paced, **no downhill** → after **3–4 wk** ↑ distance / frequency → hills + cambered surfaces only if pain-free · relapse → rest + restart

**Prognosis** — **50–90% improve in 4–8 wk** nonop (Orthobullets) · most fully relieved, return in **6–8 wk** (TO)

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication / detail |
|---|---|
| **Surgery overall** | **Refractory > 6 months** nonop (TO) |
| Excision of cyst, bursa or **lateral synovial recess** | Failed nonop + soft-tissue pathology **without ITB signal change**; arthroscopic or open; may cause **chronic effusion + pain** |
| **Elliptical ITB excision** | Chronic, failed nonop · open, lateral distal femur incision · **2 × 4 cm ellipse of posterior band** over lateral epicondyle |
| **Resection of posterior half of ITB** (TO) | Resistant cases |
| **Z-plasty** lengthening | **Refractory only** |
| Others (TO) | Percutaneous / open release (triangle over LFC), **bursectomy** (bursa + fat pad), arthroscopic debridement · modern focus: **resect lateral synovial recess** + innervated synovial fat |

![Z-plasty lengthening of the iliotibial band](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3019-zplasty.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Ober test** | **Noble test** |
|---|---|---|
| Position | **Side-lying**, knee 90° | **Supine**, knee 90° |
| Manoeuvre | Hip abduct + extend → **adduct** | **Press lateral epicondyle**, extend knee |
| Positive | Tight ITB, pain, can't adduct | **Pain at 30°** |

## How it's asked

**"Runner, lateral epicondyle pain, Ober + — initial?"** → **home stretching program and cross training**

**"Cyclist, pain at Gerdy tubercle after ↑ training — exam finding?"** → **pain with hip brought from flexion/abduction to extension/adduction while side-lying**\
✗ Wrong options: McMurray · varus at 30° (LCL) · dial (PLC) · pivot shift (ACL)

**"Hockey player, lateral knee pain — running risk factor (TO)?"** → **increased knee flexion at heel strike**

**"Lateral knee pain after downhill running, normal exam (TO)?"** → **iliotibial band syndrome**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Infectious Diseases in Athletes

*Knee & Sports · 24 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Overview

**Skin infections** — **10–15% of time-loss injuries** in collegiate athletes (OB, Johnson) · NCAA + NFHS return-to-play rules · mandatory **team skin checks**

| Infection | Organism | Spread | Typical sport |
|---|---|---|---|
| **CA-MRSA** | S. aureus (PVL +) | **Direct skin contact**; **abrasions ("turf burns")**; shared razors, towels, soap | Contact sports, wrestling |
| **Herpes gladiatorum** | **HSV-1** (80%, OB) | **Skin-to-skin**; blister fluid, saliva, mucosa | **Wrestling (2–7%)**, rugby |
| **Tinea** | Dermatophytes | Direct contact, broken skin; survives off host in warm, moist places | **Wrestling** |
| **Impetigo** | **S. pyogenes, S. aureus** | Highly contagious | Wrestling |
| Acne mechanica | Friction, heat, occlusion | — (not infectious) | **Padded sports** (hockey, football) |
| Pitted keratolysis | **Corynebacterium** | Moist feet | Runners |
| **Mononucleosis** | **EBV** (herpesvirus) | **Saliva** — kissing, shared cups / utensils, sneeze / cough | Any |
| HIV | CD4 (T-helper) cells | ↑ haemophiliacs, IV drug users, MSM | Any |

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Panton-Valentine leukocidin (PVL)** | **Cytotoxin that defines CA-MRSA** (not HA-MRSA) → lyses WBCs, tissue necrosis → rapid abscess, "spider bite" |
| **mecA → PBP2a** | Low β-lactam affinity → **methicillin resistance** (all MRSA) |
| **SCCmec type IV** | **CA-MRSA**; lacks extra elements → **less multidrug resistance** (HA-MRSA SCCmec carries them) |
| β-lactamase | In most S. aureus; methicillin resists it |
| **D-zone test** | Erythromycin-resistant isolate → clindamycin + erythromycin discs → **D-shaped zone = inducible clindamycin resistance** |
| **Heterophile Ab (Monospot)** | **87% sensitive, 91% specific**; may stay positive |
| **VCA IgG + IgM** | **97% sensitive, 94% specific** |
| CBC | Absolute + relative **lymphocytosis, > 10% atypical lymphocytes** |
| LFTs (TO) | Mildly ↑ in **\~90%** of IM |
| **KOH prep** | **Hyphae → tinea** |
| **Wood's lamp red fluorescence** | **Corynebacterium** (pitted keratolysis; erythrasma too) |
| EBV (TO) | **Double-stranded DNA gamma herpesvirus**; isolated **1964 from Burkitt lymphoma** |
| AIDS | HIV + **CD4 < 200** or opportunistic infection |

## 2 Mechanism

**MRSA risk** — **exposed skin from abrasions** ↑ risk significantly · sharing hygiene products · prevention: cover compromised skin + good hygiene

**HSV** — incubation **2–14 days** (Orthobullets) · up to 14 days (OB) · **5–10 days** (OB) · head, neck, shoulders · eye contact → **herpetic conjunctivitis**

**EBV** — incubation **30–50 days** · **transmissibility low → isolation unnecessary** · transmission to teammates possible in acute phase (OB)\
✗ Wrong options: blood-borne spread more severe

**IM epidemiology** — annual incidence **1–3% of college freshmen / students** (OB)

## 3 Presentation

### CA-MRSA – presentation

**CA-MRSA** — **community-acquired methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus**; ↑ in sport

**Lesion** — boil / pimple · **"spider bite"** · **pustules on an erythematous base**

**Wrestler, 6-cm elbow lesion "spider bite"** — **CA-MRSA**\
✗ Wrong options: psoriasis · tinea · HSV

**Abscess → amoxicillin → recurs with fever + pain** — **CA-MRSA** · up to **70%** of players hospitalised\
✗ Wrong options: group A strep (amoxicillin covers it) · tinea · HSV

**7-day ankle swelling after slide tackle + malaise, normal X-ray** — **MRSA abscess**

![CA-MRSA abscess](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3124-mrsa.jpg)

### Herpes gladiatorum – presentation

**Lesion** — **clusters of fluid-filled vesicles on an erythematous base** · grouped **1–2 mm** clear vesicles → rupture → moist ulcers / crusted plaques

**Primary infection** — constitutional symptoms; burning, tingling, stinging · bacterial infections = larger pustules

**DDx** — impetigo, tinea corporis, eczema

![Herpes gladiatorum vesicles on the neck](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3124-herpesgladiatorum.jpg)

### Other skin infections – impetigo, tinea, pitted keratolysis

**Tinea types** — pedis, corporis, capitis, cruris (by site) · **corporis** excludes feet, groin, scalp, bearded face, nails

**Tinea corporis ("ringworm")** — **scaly red circular patches** · well-demarcated plaque, **raised border, central hypopigmentation** · hypo- / hyperpigmented dry, dusty oval patches · highly contagious → minor epidemics

![Tinea corporis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3124-tineacorporis.jpg)

**Impetigo** — fluid-filled blisters → **crusting after a few days** · **golden-brown crusts around nose + mouth** (OB)

**Acne mechanica / folliculitis** — **red papules**, inflamed follicles under padding

**Pitted keratolysis** — **shallow crater-like pits on weight-bearing sole** (palms less often), thickened skin, **foul odour**, sweating · **red fluorescence on Wood's lamp** (Corynebacterium)

### Infectious mononucleosis – presentation

**Prodrome (3–5 days)** — **malaise, myalgia, nausea, headache**

**Hoagland's triad** — **fever, pharyngitis, lymphadenopathy** (Orthobullets, OB) · TO "triad": fatigue + pharyngitis (chief complaint), **posterior cervical >> axillary / inguinal nodes**, athlete with only fatigue / ↓ performance

**Pharyngitis** — 30% (Orthobullets, GAS) · **exudative** (white / grey pseudomembrane) in 50% · exudative pharyngitis + **tonsillar enlargement** classic (TO)

**Lymphadenopathy** — **posterior cervical chain**, lasts 2–3 wk

**Rash** — **after ampicillin / amoxicillin**; petechial / maculopapular / urticarial · **10–40%** (TO)

**Other (TO)** — **posterior palatine petechiae (⅓)**, **jaundice 10–15%**

**Splenomegaly** — palpable only **50%** (OB) · lymphocytic infiltration pushes spleen beyond rib cage · resolves **4–6 wk** (TO)

| IM symptom (TO) | Frequency |
|---|---|
| **Sore throat** | **95%** |
| Cervical lymphadenopathy | 80% |
| Fatigue | 70% |
| Upper respiratory symptoms | 65% |
| Headache · ↓ appetite | 50% each |
| Fever | 47% |
| Myalgia | 45% |

**Course** — symptoms resolve **4–8 wk** (Orthobullets) · acute phase **7 days to 2–3 wk**, recovery up to **2–3 months** (TO) · most findings ≤ 10 days; **fatigue + cervical nodes ≥ 3 wk** (TO)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| IM imaging | **Generally unnecessary** |
| **Ultrasound** | **First choice if imaging** — noninvasive, reliable, no radiation · confirms **normal spleen size**; baseline variability limits RTP use (TO) |
| **CT** | **Exclude splenic rupture** (stable patient) |
| FAST | Free fluid + unstable → **laparotomy** |

![Splenic rupture after mononucleosis (CT)](../../MCQ/attachments/q218194-illa-splenic-rupture1.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria

| Condition | Criteria |
|---|---|
| **AIDS** | **HIV positive + CD4 < 200** or **opportunistic infection** |
| **Tinea** | KOH scraping → **hyphae** |
| **Pitted keratolysis** | Crater-like pits + odour + **red Wood's fluorescence** |
| **IM** | Heterophile Ab (Monospot); VCA IgG / IgM; lymphocytosis > 10% atypical |

## 5 Management

### Return-to-play criteria

| Infection | Return to play |
|---|---|
| **CA-MRSA** | After **72 h on antibiotics** (e.g. TMP-SMX) (OB) · **cover lesions** · NCAA bacterial: **no new lesions 48 h** + **72 h antibiotics** + **no exudative lesions** (OB) |
| **Herpes gladiatorum** | **No new lesions 72 h** + **≥ 5 days (120 h) antivirals** + **all lesions dry / crusted** + no systemic symptoms (NCAA) |
| **Tinea corporis** | **≥ 72 h topical antifungal** + lesions **covered** + **screened before competition** |
| **Tinea capitis (scalp)** | **14 days** antifungal (NCAA, OB) |
| **Molluscum contagiosum** (pox virus, painless papule) | Lesions **removed (curetted)** + **covered with Tegaderm** before play (NCAA, OB) |
| **Impetigo** | **All lesions clear of crusting** |
| **Mononucleosis** | **No contact sport 3–5 wk** (up to 3 months) **until splenomegaly resolves** — see sources below |
| **HIV** | **HIV alone does not bar competition** · universal precautions · out until **bleeding stops** |

### CA-MRSA – treatment & prevention

| Lesion | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Small** | **Topical mupirocin** |
| **Larger** | **I&D + oral TMP-SMX + rifampin** |
| **Severe** | **I&D + IV antibiotics** |

**Abscess** — **incise, drain, culture** first; antibiotics = necessary adjunct · empiric anti-MRSA cover until sensitivities\
✗ Wrong options: topical antibiotics + occlusive dressing · training-room debridement + oral antibiotics · ice only

**Superficial, no systemic illness** — **oral** antibiotics\
✗ Wrong options: IV

**Erythromycin-resistant + D-zone positive** — **switch clindamycin → oral doxycycline**\
✗ Wrong options: continue clindamycin · IV vancomycin · IV cefazolin · **rifampin alone** (rapid resistance)

**Mupirocin small + I&D / TMP-SMX large** — = **MRSA boil**\
✗ Wrong options: impetigo · molluscum contagiosum · ringworm · HSV

**Most effective team prevention** — **cover lesions with occlusive dressing during sport**\
✗ Wrong options: separate locker room · prophylactic antibiotics for teammates · ban from team events

**Hygiene** — showering, hand washing, breathable clothing, shower sandals, **no shared towels / equipment**, daily skin surveillance, disinfect equipment, restrict contact

```
Suspected CA-MRSA skin lesion
├─ Small → topical mupirocin
├─ Abscess / larger → I&D + culture + oral TMP-SMX
│      └─ D-zone positive → oral doxycycline (not clindamycin)
└─ Severe → I&D + IV antibiotics
```

### Herpes gladiatorum – antivirals & return to play

**Treatment** — **acyclovir, valacyclovir, famciclovir** for any active lesion → **inhibit viral DNA polymerase / inactivate viral DNA synthesis** ✓

**Duration** — **5 days regardless of agent**\
✗ Wrong options: famciclovir 3 days

**Traps** — ✗ Wrong options: TMP-SMX (MRSA) · unroof / debride · RTP 48 h after starting · streptococci on culture (impetigo) · wrestle if covered · out 2 weeks · keflex 48 h

**Wrong-drug distractors (OB)** — clotrimazole → **ringworm** · mupirocin → **impetigo** · erythromycin → **erysipelas**

### Tinea – treatment

**Topical antifungal** — **tinea corporis, cruris, pedis** · **topical ketoconazole 14 days, RTP after 72 h** ✓ · imidazoles (clotrimazole, ketoconazole, miconazole) or allylamines (terbinafine, naftifine)

**Systemic antifungal** — **tinea capitis** (oral itraconazole; griseofulvin first line) + severe cases · **oral fluconazole** → widespread, failed topical, immunocompromised\
✗ Wrong options: for an isolated lesion

**✗ Wrong option: Bactrim** — antibiotic, no role in tinea

**No sport** — while infection active

### Impetigo, acne mechanica, pitted keratolysis – treatment

**Impetigo** — **erythromycin, topical Bactroban (mupirocin)** first line · no sport while active

**Acne mechanica** — **observation first** (resolves after season) · **tretinoin** (keratolytic) if severe · wash immediately after play, moisture-wicking clothing

**Pitted keratolysis** — keep feet dry, **topical clindamycin / erythromycin / mupirocin**, antiperspirants; oral antibiotics if severe · clindamycin 1%–benzoyl peroxide 5% gel effective (OB)

### Infectious mononucleosis – splenomegaly, splenic rupture & return to play

**Supportive** — fluids, acetaminophen, rest · **no isolation** · **valacyclovir not needed**

**Strep pharyngitis** — **IM penicillin once** or **oral penicillin 10 days**; erythromycin if allergic · **NO amoxicillin** (rash)

**Corticosteroids** — shrink tonsils if dysphagia / dehydration (Orthobullets) · **no role for antivirals or corticosteroids** (OB) — read the options

**Other** — airway management if respiratory distress · **stool softener** (↓ Valsalva straining)

**Rest (TO)** — rest **3 weeks**, then light activity · no strenuous activity, weights, contact, roughhousing first **21 days** · spleen still palpable at 3 wk → light activity until **28 days**

**RTP prerequisites (TO)** — **afebrile, hydrated, asymptomatic, no palpable liver or spleen** · individualise (contact vs noncontact, in- vs off-season) · explain splenic risk — **full recovery may take months**

**Not RTP markers** — negative heterophile test (stays positive) · fatigue / sore throat better (may resolve before spleen) · lymphocytosis resolved · 48 h after antibiotics

| Mono RTP rule | Source |
|---|---|
| **3 weeks** — contact sport after 3 wk of illness if no ongoing signs | **TO** |
| **3 wk after symptoms resolve**, no splenomegaly | OB |
| **4 wk after onset** (seen at 1 wk → 3–4 wk more) | OB |
| **4 wk after onset AND normal-sized spleen** | OB |
| **3–5 wk** off until splenomegaly resolves | OB, Orthobullets |
| No exertion 3 wk, then **light non-contact activity** | OB (consensus) |

**Diagnosed 2 wk ago, asymptomatic 1 wk** — **RTP in 2 weeks if no splenomegaly**\
✗ Wrong options: now · valacyclovir course

## 6 Complications

### Splenic rupture

**Rate** — **0.1–0.5%** (Orthobullets, OB) · 0.1–0.2% (OB) · **< 1%** (TO) — most feared complication

**Timing** — **most in first 3 weeks** (21 days) (Orthobullets, OB, TO) · within a month (OB)

**Mechanism** — **sudden ↑ portal venous pressure** · **50% atraumatic Valsalva** (rowing, weightlifting) + 50% external trauma (Orthobullets) · **up to 86% atraumatic** (OB) — read the options

**Normal-sized spleens** — rupture reported in nonathletes + normal spleens too

**Returned at 2 wk, tackled, severe left abdominal pain** — **splenic rupture**\
✗ Wrong options: liver contusion · sports hernia · iliac / ASIS avulsion

**Signs** — LUQ pain, **left shoulder pain (Kehr's sign)**, fatigue

**Unstable + FAST positive** — **exploratory laparotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: CT · IV fluids alone · angiography · Monospot

### Other complications of mononucleosis

**Long-term performance (TO)** — **chronic fatigue** (> 6 months) ✓\
✗ Wrong options: thrombocytopenia · myocarditis · hepatitis

**Rare, \~1% (TO)** — airway obstruction, strep pharyngitis, meningoencephalitis, haemolytic anaemia, thrombocytopenia

**Orthobullets list** — aplastic anaemia, Guillain-Barré, meningitis / encephalitis, neuritis, lymphoma, HUS, DIC

**Malignancy link (TO)** — **Burkitt, Hodgkin lymphoma, nasopharyngeal carcinoma**

**HIV wound care** — **compressive dressing**; participation stops until all bleeding ceases

## X vs Y

| Appearance | Diagnosis | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Pustule / boil, "spider bite", abscess** | **CA-MRSA** | Mupirocin · I&D + TMP-SMX |
| **Grouped vesicles on erythematous base** | **HSV** | Acyclovir / valacyclovir 5 days |
| **Ring, raised scaly border, central clearing** | **Tinea corporis** | Topical antifungal ≥ 72 h |
| **Golden-brown crusts, nose / mouth** | **Impetigo** | Erythromycin, mupirocin |
| Red papules under pads | Acne mechanica | Observe · tretinoin |
| Well-demarcated raised erythema, face | Erysipelas | Erythromycin |
| **Sole pits + odour + red Wood's** | **Pitted keratolysis** | Topical clindamycin |
| Reddish-brown patches (Corynebacterium) | Erythrasma | — |
| Thick red skin, silver-white flaky patches | Psoriasis | — |
| Keratin plugs in follicles, no odour | Keratosis pilaris | — |
| Scaling + itch, no pits, no fluorescence | Tinea pedis | Topical antifungal |

| | **CA-MRSA** | **HA-MRSA** |
|---|---|---|
| Defining element | **PVL cytotoxin** | — |
| SCCmec | **Type IV** | Multidrug elements |
| Drug resistance | **Less multidrug** | Multidrug resistant |
| Common in | Healthy athletes, close contact | Healthcare settings |

## How it's asked

**"Safe return after mononucleosis (TO)?"** → **3 weeks**

**"Sore throat + cervical nodes — complication hurting long-term performance (TO)?"** → **chronic fatigue syndrome**

**"Rash, tonsillar exudate, LUQ mass — true?"** → **RTP 3 weeks after asymptomatic**

**"Mono 1 wk, splenomegaly, wants to play tomorrow?"** → **safe after 3–4 weeks**

**"Lacrosse player with mono — RTP?"** → **4 weeks after onset AND normal-sized spleen**

**"Mono 2 wk ago, asymptomatic 1 wk?"** → **RTP in 2 weeks if no splenomegaly**

**"Splenomegaly + lymphocytosis — RTP?"** → **extended rest + resolution of splenomegaly**

**"Mono, tackled at 2 wk, left abdominal pain?"** → **splenic rupture**

**"LUQ + left shoulder pain, unstable, FAST +?"** → **exploratory laparotomy**

**"MRSA, D-zone positive — definitive antibiotic?"** → **oral doxycycline**

**"Abscess recurs after amoxicillin?"** → **CA-MRSA**

**"Best prevention of MRSA spread?"** → **occlusive dressing over lesions during sport**

**"Fluctuant ankle lesion, malaise — treatment?"** → **incision and drainage**

**"Wrestler's 'spider bite'?"** → **CA-MRSA**

**"Genetic element defining CA-MRSA?"** → **Panton-Valentine leukocidin**

**"Vesicles on chest wall before NCAA final?"** → **out until scabbed + no new lesions 72 h**

**"HSV — true?"** → **treatment inactivates viral DNA synthesis**

**"Facial vesicle cluster — RTP?"** → **after 5 days on valacyclovir**

**"Neck vesicles — treatment?"** → **acyclovir, RTP after 5 days + crusting**

**"Mupirocin small, I&D + TMP-SMX large — figure?"** → **MRSA boil**

**"Ringworm on back — treatment + RTP?"** → **topical ketoconazole 14 days, RTP after 72 h**

**"Sole craters, odour, red Wood's lamp?"** → **pitted keratolysis**

**"MRSA return to play?"** → **after 72 h of antibiotics**

**"Impetigo return to play?"** → **all lesions clear of crusting**

**"HIV-positive athlete?"** → **may compete; universal precautions**
