# Meniscus

*Knee & Sports · 31 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Function

**Load transmission** — carries **50% of load in extension, 85% in flexion** · ↑ congruency → ↑ contact area → ↓ point loading

**Shock absorption** — meniscus **more elastic than articular cartilage**

**Hoop stress** — vertical compressive load → **circumferential "hoop" tension** in the circumferential fibres → load spread, ↓ femorotibial contact stress

**Stability** — deepens the tibial surface · **secondary stabiliser** · **posterior horn MM = main secondary stabiliser to anterior translation** · menisci become **primary stabilisers in the ACL-deficient knee**

**Lateral meniscus** — less stabilising · **2× the excursion of the MM**

**Other roles (OB)** — proprioception, lubrication, nutrition of the joint, cartilage protection / OA prevention

### Composition & collagen

**Tissue** — **fibroelastic cartilage (fibrocartilage)**: collagen, proteoglycan, glycoproteins, cells

**Water** — **65–75%** (Orthobullets) · **72% water + 22% collagen** (OB, TO)

**Collagen share** — **type I = 90%** of meniscal collagen (Orthobullets, OB) · **type I = 75% of ECM dry weight** (TO) · + II, III, V, VI · collagen gives **tensile strength**

**Predominant type (TO)** — **type I**\
✗ Wrong options: type II · "type II in red-red + type I elsewhere" · "type I in red-red + type II elsewhere"

**Zonal collagen (TO)** — **red-red: type I** (like ligament / tendon) · **white-white: more type II** (like hyaline cartilage) (false: "type II predominates in red-red")

| Collagen | Tissues |
|---|---|
| **Type I** | **Meniscus (fibrocartilage)**, **ACL**, **annulus fibrosus**, **bone**, **tendon** |
| **Type II** | **Articular (hyaline) cartilage** (✗ Wrong options: same as meniscus) |

| Fibre layer (OB) | Orientation | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Superficial network + lamellar layer | **Radial** | Structural integrity; tie fibres together |
| Central main layer | **Circumferential (longitudinal)**, parallel to periphery | **Converts compression → hoop stress**; captured by **vertical mattress** sutures |

![Collagen layers of the meniscus — radial superficial, circumferential central](../../MCQ/attachments/q8834-illb-meniscus.jpg)

**Horizontal cleavage tear (OB)** — usually excised (avascular inner zone, degeneration) · Ahn: all-inside **vertical mattress** + **notch drilling (marrow)** → **91% healing**

### Gross anatomy

| | **Medial meniscus** | **Lateral meniscus** |
|---|---|---|
| Shape | **C-shaped**, semicircular, triangular cross-section | **Near circular** — horns close together, next to ACL |
| Length (TO) | **40–45 mm — longer / "larger"**; ~27 mm wide | **32–35 mm — shorter** |
| Width (Orthobullets) | 9–10 mm | 10–12 mm |
| Thickness | 3–5 mm | 4–5 mm |
| Plateau covered (TO) | **51–74% — less** | **75–93% — more** |
| Horns (TO) | **Posterior horn ≫ anterior** | Horns equal |
| Fixation | **Rigid peripheral (coronary) fixation → less mobile** | **Fewer capsular attachments → more mobile** |
| AP displacement (OB) | 0.43 mm | 0.66 mm |
| Artery (Orthobullets) | **Medial inferior genicular** → peripheral **20–30%** | **Lateral inferior genicular** → peripheral **10–25%** |

**Kinematics** — both menisci move **anterior with extension, posterior with flexion** · **MM: less anterior translation in extension + less posterior movement in flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: more translation · fewer capsular attachments

**TO "not true" trap** — "MM larger than LM" ✓ (longer) · "LM covers more surface" ✓ · "PH MM larger than anterior horn" ✓ · **"type II predominates in red-red"** (false)

### Attachments

**Transverse (intermeniscal) ligament** — joins the **anterior horns** of MM + LM

**Transverse ligament on MRI** — its junction with the **anterior horn LM mimics a tear** on sagittal images → follow it over successive images\
✗ Wrong options: LM / MM tear · anterior meniscofemoral ligament (a posterior structure) · ligamentum mucosum

**Ligamentum mucosum (infrapatellar plica)** — best on sagittal · **intercondylar notch → anterior fat pad**

**Coronary (meniscotibial) ligaments** — fix the meniscus **peripherally** to the tibia

**Meniscofemoral ligaments** — from the **posterior horn LM** into the **PCL / MFC near the PCL insertion** · **Humphrey = anterior** to PCL · **Wrisberg = posterior** · **both in 46%, at least one in 100%** (TO)

![Meniscal anatomy — transverse ligament, ligament of Wrisberg](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9019-meniscus-anatomy-jpg-moved.jpg)

### Blood supply & healing

**Middle genicular artery** — **posterior horns** (+ posterior capsule, ACL, PCL) → **primary supply to PH MM**\
✗ Wrong options: medial inferior / superior genicular · lateral geniculates

**Inferior geniculates** — medial inferior → peripheral 20–30% MM · lateral inferior → peripheral 10–25% LM · anastomose anteriorly → fat pad, synovium, patellar tendon · **lateral superior + inferior → lateral retinaculum**

**Perimeniscal capillary plexus (TO)** — from superior + inferior medial / lateral geniculates · menisci lose vessels after birth → **only peripheral 10–25% perfused at maturity** (TO; meniscal-tears TO: 10–30%)

**Central 75%** — nourished by **diffusion** from synovial fluid · limited / no intrinsic healing

**Peripheral 25% (red zone)** — heals by **fibrocartilage scar** · **fibrochondrocyte** = healing cell · **tears < 4 mm** from periphery heal best

**Innervation** — peripheral two-thirds: **type I + II nerve endings** · **posterior horns: highest mechanoreceptor density**

## 5 Management

### Meniscal root tear — tibial fixation

**Root tear** — biomechanically **= total meniscectomy** (loss of hoop tension → ↑ peak contact pressure) → chronic pain, instability, **rapid OA** · an independent tear subtype

**52 y, PH MM root tear, no arthritis on X-ray** — **fixation to the tibia (root repair)** = gold standard\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs + PT · partial meniscectomy · total meniscectomy (Fairbank changes) · UKA (only if arthritis)

**Nonop (Krych)** — poor outcomes, worsening OA, **> 33% arthroplasty at mean 32 months**

**Partial meniscectomy vs repair (Chung)** — repair better clinically + radiologically · **lower arthroplasty at 5 y**

**Age (LaPrade)** — transtibial double-tunnel pull-out improves **< 50 and > 50 y equally**, medial or lateral

**Pre-existing / rapid arthritis** — **UKA / TKA**

**TKA after unrepaired root tear — cross-check** — > 33% at 32 months (OB, here) · ~30% at 5 y · up to 28% at 3.2 y (OB, Meniscal Tears chapter) — read the options

## X vs Y

| | **Red zone (peripheral 25%)** | **White zone (central 75%)** |
|---|---|---|
| Blood supply | Geniculate capillary plexus | **Diffusion** only |
| Collagen (TO) | **Type I** | More **type II** |
| Healing | **Fibrocartilage scar** (fibrochondrocyte) | Little or none |

## How it's asked

**"Predominant collagen in meniscus (TO)?"** → **type I**

**"NOT true of the menisci (TO)?"** → **type 2 collagen predominates in red-red zone**

**"Medial vs lateral meniscus — true?"** → **MM: less anterior translation in extension, less posterior in flexion**

**"Primary blood supply to PH MM?"** → **middle genicular artery**

**"Structure joining anterior horns on MRI?"** → **transverse meniscal ligament**

**"Torn meniscus — same collagen as?"** → **ACL and annulus fibrosus (type I)**

**"Root tear, no arthritis — definitive treatment?"** → **fixation to the tibia**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Neck Injuries in Athletes

*Knee & Sports · 32 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Spectrum & epidemiology

**Spectrum** — **ligament sprain** → **burner / stinger** → **spear tackler's spine** → **cervical fracture** → **transient quadriplegia** → **quadriplegia**

**Who** — contact sports, **football + rugby** · better protective gear → ↓ incidence · **9–15%** of \~12,000 new spinal cord injuries / yr from contact / collision sports

**Catastrophic** — \~**6 quadriplegic events / yr** (US high school + college football; more at college level)

**Highest rate of cord injury** — **bilateral facet dislocation**

### Predisposing conditions (↑ severity, may bar play)

**Underlying conditions** — **previous C-spine trauma** (fracture, ligament injury), **cervical stenosis**, **congenital odontoid hypoplasia**, **os odontoideum**, **Klippel-Feil** anomalies

## 2 Mechanism

### Spear tackling & axial loading

**Mechanism** — **axial load (compression) on a flexed neck** · most injuries during **tackling**

**Spear tackling** (leading with crown of helmet) — **most common mechanism of neck injury in football** → gradual **cervical stenosis + loss of lordosis**

**Catastrophic injury** — **axial loading of the subaxial spine during spear tackling** → fracture, dislocation, ligament disruption\
✗ Wrong options: arm-tackle traction root avulsion (neurotmesis, most severe "stinger") · lateral bending · flexion-distraction / whiplash cut block · face-mask rotation

**Boden series (196 catastrophic injuries, 13 yr)** — **subaxial 95**, C1-C2 9, combined 7, unknown 41 · **43 cord neurapraxia**, 1 root avulsion · **defensive backs + spear tackling** = greatest risk

| # | Spear tackler's spine — all 4 criteria |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Developmental cervical canal stenosis** |
| 2 | **Persistent straightening / reversal of lordosis** |
| 3 | Post-traumatic radiographic abnormalities |
| 4 | **Documented spear-tackling technique** |

**Spear tackler's spine** — **contraindication to contact sports**

![Spear tackling — straightened cervical spine takes the axial load](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3114-spear-tackler.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

### On-field management of suspected cervical spine injury

**Assume spinal injury** — any athlete with **lost or altered consciousness**

**Most important prehospital step** — **maintain neutral cervical alignment**\
✗ Wrong options: assess off field first · high-dose steroids (weak evidence, surgeon's discretion)

```
Prone player, can't move limbs
├─ 1 → log-roll SUPINE with C-spine stabilised
├─ 2 → remove FACEMASK (airway)
├─ 3 → remove helmet + shoulder pads together (if safe)
└─ 4 → hard collar, controlled backboard (log-roll)
       (equipment kept: towel rolls taped beside helmet)
```

(✗ Wrong options: **Traps** — **hard collar with helmet still on**) · keeping player **prone** · **helmet off before facemask**

**Helmet + pads** — **NATA now allows on-field removal of both** by trained personnel if neutral alignment kept (Orthobullets, Mills consensus) · older advice: **remove facemask, retain helmet** (Schroeder & Vaccaro) · cadaver C5-6: **helmet alone → kyphosis, pads alone → lordosis, both retained → neutral** — remove or retain **together**

**Exam** — inspect deformity · palpate spinous processes (**step-off**, pain) · motor, sensation + reflexes in **all 4 limbs**

### Transient quadriplegia (cervical cord neurapraxia)

**Features** — neurapraxia of the cord → **bilateral upper + lower limb** pain, paresthesia, weakness → resolves **minutes–hours** (Orthobullets) · **10 min–48 h** (OB, stinger chapter)

**Mechanism** — hyperextension, hyperflexion or axial load · 3 of 16 NFL disc herniations presented with transient paresis

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Fact |
|---|---|
| Radiographs — indications | **Recurrent stingers** · neurologic symptoms / **transient quadriplegia** · C-spine trauma series |
| **Canal diameter (SAC)** | Normal **\~17 mm** · **< 13 mm = stenosis** · **< 10 mm = absolute stenosis** (OB) |
| **Torg-Pavlov ratio** | **Canal ÷ vertebral body** (sagittal) · normal **1.0** · stenosis **< 0.8** (Orthobullets) · **< 0.82** (Pavlov + Torg original) |
| Torg ratio limits | **Technique dependent**, **low PPV / not predictive**, **inaccurate in large athletes** (big bodies skew ratio) |
| Torg ratio < 0.8 prevalence | **49% of asymptomatic pro football players** |
| **MRI** | **Bilateral neurologic symptoms** → stenosis, **loss of CSF around cord**, cord signal change |
| "Functional" stenosis | **Loss of CSF / cord deformation on MRI or CT myelogram** — bone ratios alone can't define it |

**Torg original data** — canal **14 mm** in symptomatic athletes vs **18.9 mm** controls

**SAC at injury level vs deficit** — **10.5 mm complete** · 13.1 mm incomplete · 15.9 mm root · 16.7 mm none

![Lateral C-spine — a = vertebral body, b = canal (SAC); Torg-Pavlov ratio = b ÷ a](../../MCQ/attachments/q219418-illa-ptrat.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Return-to-play contraindications (cord signal change, fusion, transient quadriparesis, Torg ratio)

| ✓ May return to play | Condition |
|---|---|
| Stinger | **Complete resolution**, normal strength + ROM |
| Transient quadriplegia | **Normal MRI** |
| **Congenital stenosis (Torg < 0.8), no instability** | ✓ — counsel risks (✗ Wrong options: absolute contraindication) |
| **Solid 1- or 2-level ACDF** | Asymptomatic, **no T2 signal change** |
| Transient paralysis, **resolved T2 change, SAC 14 mm** | ✓ RTP |

| Case-by-case | Condition |
|---|---|
| Solid **1-level ACDF, continued T2 change** | Individual decision |
| Transient paralysis, **never had T2 change** (e.g. SAC 9 mm) | Individual decision |
| Transient paralysis, **resolved T2 change, SAC ≥ 10 mm** (e.g. 11 mm) | Individual decision |
| **Torg type 2 Klippel-Feil below C3**, asymptomatic | **Relative** contraindication |

| No return to play | Condition |
|---|---|
| **Cord signal change on MRI** | **Absolute** · multiple transient quadriparesis episodes + MRI cord injury → avoid contact |
| Transient quadriplegia + **severe / functional stenosis** | No RTP |
| **Spear tackler's spine** | No RTP |
| Cord neurapraxia + **ligamentous instability** | No RTP |
| **C0–C2 anomalies** | **Odontoid hypoplasia, os odontoideum, atlanto-occipital fusion** — absolute |
| **Torg type 1 Klippel-Feil** (long fusion mass) | Absolute |
| Torg type 2 Klippel-Feil (1–2 segments) | With limited motion, degeneration or instability |
| **3-level ACDF** | Even if solid, asymptomatic, **no T2 change** |
| Continued T2 change after **2- or 3-level ACDF** | No RTP |
| **Resolved T2 change + SAC < 10 mm** after transient paralysis | No RTP |
| Cervical myelopathy history | No RTP |

**Bilateral UE numbness > 15 min, Torg 0.80** — **no RTP until advanced imaging (MRI)**\
✗ Wrong options: Torg has high PPV · "low PPV → return now" · only useful in pros · congenital stenosis = absolute bar

**Transient quadriplegia + residual paresthesia, Torg 0.7, C5-6 disc compressing cord, canal 9 mm, lost lordosis** — **disqualify from further contact play**\
✗ Wrong options: waiver · graduated RTP · PT + epidural · decompression + fusion then RTP

**Disc herniation in athletes** — commonest = **radiculopathy after one event** · disc abutting cord → nonop allowed but **no RTS until symptoms improve + repeat imaging shows no cord compression** · usual **6-week conservative trial**

**Operative** — as for any traumatic spinal injury

## X vs Y

| | **Stinger** | **Transient quadriparesis** |
|---|---|---|
| Lesion | Root / brachial plexus | **Spinal cord** |
| Limbs | **Unilateral** arm | **Bilateral**, arms ± legs |
| Imaging | Radiographs if recurrent | **MRI** |
| RTP | Same day if fully resolved | Only if **MRI normal**, no instability / severe stenosis |

| | **Torg-Pavlov ratio** | **MRI "functional" stenosis** |
|---|---|---|
| Measures | Bony canal ÷ body width | **CSF reserve around the cord** |
| Predicts injury | **Poorly** (low PPV) | Better — bars contact sport |

## How it's asked

**"Sequence for prone player who can't move limbs?"** → **log-roll supine, facemask off, helmet + pads off, hard collar, backboard**

**"Most important prehospital consideration?"** → **maintain neutral cervical spine alignment**

**"Commonest mechanism of catastrophic neck injury in football?"** → **axial loading of the subaxial spine with spear tackling**

**"Absolute contraindication after transient arm paralysis?"** → **cord signal change on MRI**

**"Bilateral numbness > 15 min, Torg 0.80?"** → **no RTP until advanced imaging obtained**

**"Absolute contraindication to collision sports?"** → **asymptomatic, no T2 change after solid 3-level ACDF**

**"Transient quadriplegia, Torg 0.7, disc on cord, canal 9 mm?"** → **disqualify from further play**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Osgood Schlatter's Disease (Tibial Tubercle Apophysitis)

*Knee & Sports · 33 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & pathophysiology

**Definition** — **osteochondrosis / traction apophysitis of the tibial tubercle** → paediatric anterior knee pain

**Who** — **boys > girls** · **bilateral 20–30%** · **jumpers (basketball, volleyball)**, sprinters, soccer, gymnastics, track · non-athletes too

**Age** — boys **12–15 yr** (Orthobullets, OB) · boys **5–12 yr** (OB) · girls **8–12 yr** — read the options

**Cause** — repetitive pull of **extensor mechanism / patellar tendon** · **growth spurt**: bone lengthening outpaces muscle–tendon flexibility → ↑ tension on apophysis

**Patellar tendon strain ratio** — **↓ in OSD** (elastography, Enomoto: 18 vs 42 legs) → **less compliant tendon** → force reaches tibial tubercle **faster**

### Anatomy — tibial tubercle ossification

| Age | Tubercle |
|---|---|
| **< 11 yr** | **Cartilaginous** |
| **11–14 yr** | **Apophysis forms** (secondary ossification centre) |
| 14–18 yr | Apophysis **fuses with tibial epiphysis** |
| **> 18 yr** | Epiphysis + apophysis **fused** to tibia |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — dull **anterior knee pain**, worse with **kneeling**, jumping, squatting, stairs · often **bilateral**

**Exam** — **enlarged, tender tibial tubercle** · **pain on resisted knee extension** (most painful) · no effusion, no limp, stable knee

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Lateral knee X-ray** | **Irregularity + fragmentation of tibial tubercle** · early: apophysis **separation** · late: fragmentation + **intratendinous ossicles** · may be normal |
| X-ray role | Diagnosis is **clinical**; films rule out other causes |
| MRI | **Not essential** · soft-tissue swelling, **thickened oedematous inferior patellar tendon**, fragmented ossification centre |
| Mature patient | **Persistent ossicle** after tibial epiphysis fuses |

![Lateral radiograph — fragmentation of the tibial tubercle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3029-lat-xr.jpg)

### Diagnosis & differential (Sinding-Larsen-Johansson, sleeve fracture)

| Condition | Distinguishing fact |
|---|---|
| **Osgood-Schlatter** | **Tibial tubercle** tenderness, chronic, walks normally |
| **Sinding-Larsen-Johansson** | Chronic **apophysitis / minor avulsion of inferior patellar pole**; **10–14 yr**, esp. **cerebral palsy** |
| **Patellar sleeve avulsion** | **Inferior pole** tenderness, cortical elevation of inferior patella, **can't walk** |
| **Tibial tubercle avulsion fracture** | **Acute**, ambulation difficulty |
| Tibial spine avulsion | Acute, can't bear weight, big effusion, **Lachman +** |
| **Bipartite patella** | Normal variant (failed fusion of 2 ossification centres); **painless, incidental** |
| Others | Jumper's knee, **proximal tibial osteochondroma**, Lyme (isolated effusion), plica, fat-pad impingement, infection, malignancy |

![Sinding-Larsen-Johansson — inferior patellar pole](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3029-xr-slj.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Osgood-Schlatter
├─ First line → NSAIDs, rest, ice, activity modification, strapping, quads stretching
│      └─ severe, not responding → CAST 6 WEEKS
└─ Skeletally mature + persistent symptoms (~10%) → OSSICLE EXCISION ± TUBERCLEPLASTY
```

**Nonop outcome** — **90% complete resolution** · **self-limiting** but **resolves only when growth stops** (physeal closure)

**Persistent pain** — **60.5%** still had knee pain at median **3.75 yr** (Guldhammer) → "self-limited" dogma questioned

**Cast complication** — **quadriceps wasting**

### Persistent symptomatic ossicle — excision

**Surgery indications** — pain localised to tubercle, limits activity, **failed nonop**, **persists into skeletal maturity**

**Procedure** — **ossicle excision + tubercleplasty** (resect prominence) → **relieves symptoms, return to sport expected**\
✗ Wrong options: never indicated · prednisolone injection · hamstring lengthening · RTS unlikely

| Surgical outcome (Pihlajamäki) | Result |
|---|---|
| 178 military recruits, 10 yr | **85% no pain** at operative site · **75% back to full activity** |
| 107 patients (117 knees), median 10 yr | Good Kujala / VAS, no long-term harm · but **39% still pain on kneeling** |
| Mital (16 knees) | Only **1** did not return to sport → combine excision + tubercleplasty |

## X vs Y

| | **Osgood-Schlatter** | **Sinding-Larsen-Johansson** | **Tibial tubercle fracture** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Site | **Tibial tubercle** | **Inferior patellar pole** | Tibial tubercle |
| Onset | Chronic, activity-related | Chronic | **Acute** |
| Walking | Normal | Normal | **Difficult** |
| X-ray | Fragmentation, ossicles | Inferior pole fragmentation | Displaced fracture |

## How it's asked

**"13 yr, bilateral tibial tubercle pain on jumping / stairs?"** → **Osgood-Schlatter disease**\
✗ Wrong options: Lyme · plateau stress fracture · patellar tendinopathy · SLJ

**"11 yr, growth spurt, pain on resisted extension — radiograph?"** → **Figure C: tubercle irregularity / intratendinous ossification**\
✗ Wrong options: A tubercle avulsion · B sleeve fracture · D bipartite patella · E tibial spine

**"Decreased patellar tendon strain ratio — radiograph?"** → **Figure B: fragmented tibial tubercle apophysis**\
✗ Wrong options: normal · tubercle fracture · sleeve · loose body

**"18 yr, OSD since 13, failed nonop — surgery?"** → **ossicle excision and tubercleplasty will relieve symptoms and return to sport is expected**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Osteitis Pubis

*Knee & Sports · 34 / 61*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **inflammation of the pubic symphysis** from **repetitive microtrauma** · excessive strain on pubic bone

**Sports** — **repetitive kicking** or repetitive **hip adduction / abduction** · soccer, hockey, football, running · most common in **young football players** (OB)

### Anatomy — pubic symphysis

**Joint** — anterior articulation of the hemipelves · cartilage-covered rami + **fibrocartilage disc** · **very stable**

**Attachments** — adductors (magnus, brevis, **longus**, gracilis), **rectus abdominis**, pectineus

**Ligaments** — superior, inferior, anterior, posterior pubic ligaments

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **vague, ill-defined anterior pelvic / groin pain**, dull ache · sharp stabbing pain running, kicking, changing direction · worse with hip adduction / abduction · ± adductor spasm · loss of groin flexibility

**Exam** — **localised tenderness directly over the symphysis** · pain on **resisted rectus abdominis testing**

**Sports hernia contrast** — tender in the **abdomen / inguinal region, not the pubis** (comparison table in Athletic Pubalgia chapter)

**No fever** — excludes osteomyelitis\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration + antibiotics

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **AP pelvis** | **Bony erosions + irregularity**, osteolytic pubis, **symphysis widening (diastasis)**, degenerative change · may be **negative early** |
| **MRI** | **Bone marrow oedema** (early) |
| Bone scan | ↑ uptake at symphysis |

![Osteitis pubis: erosions, irregularity and early widening of the symphysis](../../MCQ/attachments/q3175-c-osteitis-pubis.jpg)

![MRI: bone marrow oedema either side of the symphysis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3093-osteitis.jpg)

### Differential diagnosis

| Image in the options | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| **Erosions + irregularity + early widening** | **Osteitis pubis** |
| Widening, **no underlying bony abnormality** | Pubic symphysis diastasis |
| Femoral neck, compression side | Compression-type femoral neck stress fracture |
| Inferior pubic ramus | Pubic ramus stress fracture |
| Pelvic bony lesion | Pelvic osteochondroma |

**Differential** — athletic pubalgia, pubic ramus stress fracture, femoral neck stress fracture, inguinal hernia, oncologic disease (rare)

## 5 Management

**Nonoperative (vast majority)** — **rest, NSAIDs, rehabilitation, activity modification, gradual return to play**

**Soccer player, symphysis tenderness, negative X-rays** — **rest, NSAIDs, rehab, gradual RTP**\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration + antibiotics · hernia referral · symphyseal plating (no instability) · MRI

**Steroid injection** — into symphysis **controversial**

**Course** — **self-limiting**, may take **several months**

## How it's asked

**"Which image is osteitis pubis?"** → **Figure C — erosions, irregularity, early symphysis widening**

**"Symphysis tenderness, pain on resisted rectus testing, no fever — next?"** → **rest, NSAIDs, rehabilitation, gradual return to play**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Osteochondritis Dissecans

*Knee & Sports · 35 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & location (lateral aspect of MFC)

**Definition** — acquired, potentially reversible idiopathic lesion of **subchondral bone** → delamination + sequestration ± articular cartilage involvement + instability

**Juvenile form** — **open physes**, age **10–15** (median **13.1** Orthobullets · 13 OB) · **adult form** = skeletally mature

**Typical patient** — **13-year-old male**, lesion on **lateral aspect of medial femoral condyle** · **M:F 5–6:1**

**Sites** — **knee (most common)**, capitellum, talus · knee osteochondrosis on **0.002–0.003%** of knee radiographs

| Knee location | Share |
|---|---|
| **Posterolateral MFC (lateral aspect of MFC) — "classic"** | **70%** (Orthobullets, OB) · **60–70%** (OB) |
| **Inferior-central lateral femoral condyle** | **15–20%** (\~15%) |
| Femoral trochlea | **< 1%** |
| Patella, trochlea | Infrequent |
| **Baseball catchers** | **Posterior femoral condyle** (hyperflexion loading — OB, cartilage chapter) |

### Etiology & pathoanatomy

**Causes** — **hereditary, traumatic, vascular** (multifactorial) · **adult form → vascular** · microtrauma · mechanical (**discoid meniscus**, **tibial spine impingement**) (OB)

**Cascade** — softening of cartilage with **intact surface** → early cartilage separation → **partial detachment** → **osteochondral separation + loose bodies**

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **vague, poorly localised, activity-related**

**Mechanical symptoms** — catching, locking → **advanced disease** / unstable flap or loose body

**Other** — **recurrent effusions**, localised tenderness, stiffness, swelling, antalgic gait

**Wilson test** — **pain on internal rotation of tibia during extension between 90° and 30°**, **relieved by tibial external rotation**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| Weight-bearing AP + lateral | Lesion (**incongruence of lateral aspect of MFC**), **physeal status**; may be **read as normal** → MRI if pain persists |
| **Tunnel (notch) view** | **Knee flexed 30–50°** — shows posterior condyle lesion |
| X-ray sign | Well-circumscribed **sclerotic subchondral bone + radiolucent halo**; **sclerosis = poor prognosis** |
| **MRI** | **Size**, subchondral bone + cartilage status, **signal around lesion**, **loose bodies** → **stability** |
| MRI unstable | **Fluid (high T2) behind / circumferential to fragment** (**rim sign**), fluid **breaching cartilage** / breaks in cartilage + oedema; cysts deep to lesion = subchondral involvement |
| Bone scan | **Open physes + activity behind lesion → 100% healed**; **closing physes → none** (Paletta) |
| **Arthroscopy** | **Gold standard for instability** — break in cartilage or **mobile flap on probing** |

![Tunnel (notch) view showing an OCD lesion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3028-tunnel-notch-view.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria — stable vs unstable lesion

| | **Stable** | **Unstable** |
|---|---|---|
| MRI T2 | **No ↑ T2 signal deep to fragment** | **High-signal fluid line behind / circumferential** (DeSmet → less likely to heal) |
| Subchondral plate | Intact | **Articular fracture through subchondral plate** |
| Cyst | None | **≥ 5 mm fluid-filled cyst deep to lesion** |
| Cartilage | **Intact cap**, no fragmentation | **Focal osteochondral defect**, fluid through cartilage |
| Arthroscopy | Firm on probing | **Hinged, ballotable**, mobile flap, detached |

**De Smet instability signs (TO, cartilage chapter)** — T2 fluid line **≥ 5 mm** · cysts **≥ 5 mm** · focal cartilage defect **≥ 5 mm** · fluid through cartilage into bone

![MRI — fluid signal behind an OCD fragment](../../MCQ/attachments/q2189-illa-unstable.jpg)

### Clanton classification (Clanton & DeLee)

| Type | Finding |
|---|---|
| I | **Depressed** osteochondral fracture |
| II | Fragment attached by **osseous bridge** |
| III | **Detached, non-displaced** fragment |
| IV | **Displaced** fragment |

**ICRS OCD** — **types II + III = unstable** → often surgery (OB)

### Prognosis — open physes & lesion stability

| Good prognosis (nonop) | Poor prognosis |
|---|---|
| **Open distal femoral physes — best predictor** | **Closing / closed physes**, **adult form** (symptomatic → **DJD** if untreated) |
| **Younger age** | Older adolescent |
| **Classic site: lateral aspect of MFC** | **Lateral femoral condyle, patella** |
| **Size < 2 cm** (e.g. 1.5 cm) | > 2 cm |
| **Intact surface, no fragmentation on MRI** | **Synovial fluid behind lesion** on MRI |
| **No sclerosis** on X-ray | **Sclerosis** on plain radiographs |

**No proven effect** — weight / BMI, gender, trauma history, ethnicity, family history, degree of sport, **concomitant tibial apophysitis**, cartilage thickness

**Location conflict** — classic MFC site = **good prognosis** (Orthobullets, OB) vs **typical location not predictive of healing** (OB, DeSmet / Paletta question) — read the options

**Adult OCD** — rarely heals spontaneously; **very low nonop healing rate**

## 5 Management

```
OCD — first decision
├─ Open physes + stable MRI → RESTRICTED WB / ACTIVITY MODIFICATION ± brace
│      └─ fails 3–6 mo / approaching physeal closure → SUBCHONDRAL DRILLING
├─ Unstable / hinged, salvageable → ARTHROSCOPIC OR OPEN FIXATION
├─ Unsalvageable, large / bone loss → OCA or MACI (± bone graft)
└─ Asymptomatic adult → NONOP
```

```
Arthroscopic finding (OB algorithm)
├─ Stable → TRANSCHONDRAL DRILLING
├─ Unstable, reducible → FIXATION ± GRAFT
├─ Unstable + fragmentation / osteolysis → FIXATION + GRAFT
└─ Unstable + chondral damage → FIXATION + GRAFTS, CHONDROCYTE TRANSPLANT or OSTEOCHONDRAL GRAFT
```

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Source |
|---|---|
| **Impending physeal closure** | Orthobullets, Cahill |
| **Clinical signs of instability** / detachment during treatment | Orthobullets, Cahill |
| **Expanding lesion** on plain films | Orthobullets |
| **Failed nonop** (persistent symptoms in compliant patient) | Orthobullets, Cahill |
| Persistently ↑ or worsening **bone-scan activity** | Cahill |
| Mechanical locking (loose body), displaced fragment | OB |
| **Closed physes**, unstable lesion | Orthobullets, OB |

**Cahill series** — **204 JOCD**: **50% needed surgery** · fragment detachment **34%**, persistent symptoms / combined **26%** of failures

| Procedure | Indication | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **Subchondral drilling** (K-wire / drill) | **Stable** lesion on arthroscopy; **transchondral (antegrade) or retrograde** | **Fibrocartilage**; better if immature; radiographic healing **\~4.4 months** |
| **Fixation** | **Acute unstable** lesion on arthroscopy or MRI, **> 2 cm** | **85% healing** in JOCD |
| **Chondral resurfacing** | **> 2 × 2 cm** | — |
| Osteochondral grafting | **Arthroscopic < 3 cm** · **arthrotomy > 3 cm** | Allograft plugs, autograft OATS |
| Knee arthroplasty | **> 60 yr** | — |

### Stable juvenile lesions — activity modification & restricted weight bearing

**Stable, open physes** — **restricted WB + bracing** / **activity modification** · **50–75% heal without fragmentation** · also **asymptomatic adults**

**Duration** — **NWB 6–8 wk** (OB) · activity restriction **3–9 months** (OB) · cease sport 4–6 months (OB, osteonecrosis chapter)

**11 yr, intact cartilage, Wilson +** — **non-weight bearing for 6–8 weeks**\
✗ Wrong options: removal · ORIF · microfracture · full WB

**11 yr catcher, 14 × 14 mm, no sclerosis / cysts** — **stable, period of modified weightbearing**\
✗ Wrong options: fixation · OCA (larger lesions)

**10 yr, 1 × 1 cm LFC lesion, open physes** — **activity modification**\
✗ Wrong options: microfracture · antegrade drilling · fixation · excision

**13 yr, stable LFC lesion, no prior treatment** — **restricted weight bearing**\
✗ Wrong options: **aggressive physical therapy** (worsens) · excision · drilling yet · OCA

**Immature distal femur OCD** — outcomes **good**, usually heal without surgery (Schenck & Goodnight)

### Stable lesion failing nonoperative care — drilling

**16 yr, stable 1.5 × 1 cm LFC, 6 months failed restriction** — **arthroscopic drilling of the subchondral bone** · near maturity → unlikely to heal nonop; drilling **high success**\
✗ Wrong options: 6 more months · aggressive PT · screw fixation · OATS

**Drilling only works** — **stable** lesions **without circumferential fluid** on MRI

### Unstable lesions — fragment fixation or salvage

**Principle** — **salvage the fragment + restore native surface whenever possible** · stabilise **arthroscopically or open**

**Fixation options** — **cannulated screws, Herbert / headless compression screws**, bone pegs, K-wires, bioabsorbable screws / darts, metal or biocomposite · may need **hardware removal**

**Unstable OCD, 15 yr** — **arthroscopic or open reduction and internal fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: loose-body removal · 9 months restriction · drilling

**Most indicated for fixation** — **16-year-old boy, closed physis, 2.5-cm hinging OCD lesion from the medial femoral condyle**\
✗ Wrong options: intact cap lesions → drill if failed · 5.2 cm → OCA

**Catcher, failed 8 wk NWB + 4-month RTP, MRI unchanged** — **attempted salvage of the osteochondral fragment** (drilling vs screw fixation) · **immature → better salvage**\
✗ Wrong options: play on NSAIDs · more PT / restriction · MACI first

**20 yr, acute unstable 1.5 × 3 cm, no chronic bone change** — **arthroscopic assisted internal fixation of lesion**\
✗ Wrong options: PT + steroid · abrasion · MACI (needs supportive bone bed) · OATS (< 2 × 2 cm unsalvageable)

**Chronic / poor tissue / no bone on fragment** — **loose-body removal + debridement**, later cartilage restoration

**16 yr lineman, \~2.5 × 2 cm unstable lateral-MFC lesion, circumferential fluid** — **arthroscopic fixation of the lesion with headless compression screws** · microfracture **out of favour for large lesions** · same OB item calls him **skeletally mature** (explanation) and **skeletally immature** (figure legend) — fixation either way\
✗ Wrong options: bracing · MACI stage 1 · OATS · retrograde drilling

**Cartilage flap** — lets **subchondral fluid accumulate** behind the fragment

**Microfracture technique** — awl to **1–1.5 cm** below surface · **NWB 4–6 wk + CPM**

### Unsalvageable / failed lesions — osteochondral allograft

**OCA after failed fixation + microfracture** — **reliably high rate of patient satisfaction** · **survivorship > 90% at 10 yr** (✗ Wrong options: failure > 30% at 5 yr) · incorporation by **creeping substitution** (✗ Wrong options: cutting cone) · not replaced by fibrocartilage · **higher BMI → failure to return to sport** (✗ Wrong options: low BMI)

**OCA over MACI** — **subchondral bone loss > 1cm** · MACI needs minimal bone loss; add **bone graft if > 1 cm**\
✗ Wrong options: age · bipolar (worse with any procedure) · location · > 2 cm² (fits both)

| Defect size (OB) | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **< 2 cm²** | Chondroplasty, microfracture, **OATS** |
| **> 2 cm²** | **OCA or MACI**; **OCA** if bone loss / revision |
| **Chronic > 2 up to 4–6 cm²** | **OATS** (limited by harvest sites) |
| **> 6 cm²** | **OCA** (Treatment E) or MACI |

**31 yr, 2.25 × 2.75 cm untreated OCD, MPTA 87°, Mikulicz line 4 mm medial to lateral tibial spine** — normal alignment → **OCA (Treatment E)**\
✗ Wrong options: HTO · OATS · microfracture · screw

**Figure D key** — A **OATS** · B **microfracture** · C **HTO** · D **headless compression screw** · E **OCA**

## X vs Y

| | **Juvenile OCD** | **Adult OCD** |
|---|---|---|
| Physes | **Open** (10–15 yr) | **Closed** |
| Cause | Multifactorial | **Vascular** |
| Nonop healing | **Good** (50–75%) | **Very low** |
| First line | **Restricted WB** if stable | Surgery if symptomatic / unstable; nonop if asymptomatic |
| Prognosis | Better | **Worse → DJD** |

| | **Drilling** | **Fixation** | **OCA** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lesion | **Stable**, intact cartilage | **Unstable / hinged**, salvageable | **Unsalvageable**, large, bone loss |
| Example | Failed nonop, near maturity | Acute, bone on fragment | Failed fixation, > 6 cm² |

## How it's asked

**"Most likely location of JOCD?"** → **lateral aspect of the medial femoral condyle**

**"Most common presentation?"** → **13-year-old male with a lesion on the lateral aspect of medial femoral condyle**

**"Most likely site on figure (15 yr, soccer)?"** → **posterolateral medial femoral condyle (D)**

**"Most important determinant of nonop success?"** → **presence of open physes**

**"Best predictor of nonop success?"** → **open femoral physis**\
✗ Wrong options: high signal behind lesion (unstable)

**"Good prognosis EXCEPT?"** → **sclerosis of the lesion on plain radiographs**

**"Greatest impact on prognosis?"** → **skeletal immaturity**\
✗ Wrong options: tibial apophysitis · sport level · ethnicity · family history

**"Warrants initial nonop management?"** → **absence of increased T2 signal deep to lesion with open physes**

**"11 yr, Wilson test +, intact lesion?"** → **non-weight bearing for 6–8 weeks**

**"Catcher, 14 × 14 mm lesion — describe + next step?"** → **stable, period of modified weightbearing**

**"10 yr, sclerotic lesion with lucent halo, open physes?"** → **activity modification**

**"13 yr, stable LFC lesion, untreated?"** → **restricted weight bearing**

**"16 yr, stable LFC lesion, 6 months failed?"** → **arthroscopic drilling of the subchondral bone**

**"15 yr, unstable lesion on MRI?"** → **arthroscopic or open reduction and internal fixation**

**"Which vignette most needs reduction + fixation?"** → **16-year-old boy, closed physis, 2.5-cm hinging lesion of the MFC**

**"Failed nonop, MRI unchanged — next?"** → **attempted salvage of the osteochondral fragment**

**"Acute unstable 1.5 × 3 cm lesion, 20 yr?"** → **arthroscopic assisted internal fixation of lesion**

**"Mature, unstable lesion on MRI?"** → **arthroscopic fixation with headless compression screws**

**"OCA for failed OCD — true?"** → **results in a reliably high rate of patient satisfaction**

**"OCA over MACI alone?"** → **subchondral bone loss > 1 cm**

**"Untreated OCD > 6 cm², normal alignment?"** → **osteochondral allograft (Treatment E)**

# Osteonecrosis of the Knee

*Knee & Sports · 36 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Who** — **women : men 3 : 1** · **women < 55 yr with risk factors** · found **after knee arthroscopy in middle-aged women**

**Distribution** — usually **> 1 compartment** or even **metaphysis** · **80% bilateral** · **multifocal** common

**Prognosis** — **self-limiting** condition (Orthobullets)

### Risk factors & pathophysiology

| Group | Risk factors |
|---|---|
| **Classic** | **Alcoholism**, **steroids** (endogenous or exogenous), **sickle cell**, **hypercoagulable states**, **trauma** |
| Dysbaric | **Decompression sickness ("the bends")** |
| Marrow-replacing | **Gaucher disease** |
| Systemic | **SLE**, inflammatory bowel disease, transplant patient |
| Infective / drugs | Viruses (**CMV, hepatitis, HIV**, rubella, rubeola, varicella), **protease inhibitors** (HIV) |

**Mechanism** — ↓ blood flow to bone secondary to risk factors

**Spontaneous osteonecrosis (SONK)** — likely a **subchondral insufficiency fracture** · also linked to **meniscal root tear**

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **with weight bearing**, especially **sitting → standing**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| X-ray (first line) | AP, lateral, Merchant · **wedge-shaped lesion** · diagnostic only in **advanced** disease |
| **MRI** | **Most useful** · **highest sensitivity + specificity** · needed for **early / subclinical** disease |
| MRI signal | **T1 dark** · **T2 bright (marrow oedema)** |

![MRI of osteonecrosis of the distal femur](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3025-mri-colorado-moved.jpg)

### Differential diagnosis

| Condition | Distinguishing fact |
|---|---|
| **Osteochondritis dissecans** | **Lateral aspect of MFC**, males **15–20 yr** (this note) · **10–15 yr**, median 13 (OCD chapter) — read the options |
| **Transient osteoporosis** | **Young–middle-aged men**; **multiple joints in 40%** (transient migratory osteoporosis) |
| Occult fracture / bone bruise | Trauma, weak bone, overuse |
| **SONK** | Subchondral insufficiency fracture; meniscal root tear |

## 5 Management

```
Osteonecrosis of the knee
├─ All → NONOP FIRST (NSAIDs, limited WB, quads, activity modification)
├─ Small unstable fragments → ARTHROSCOPIC REMOVAL
├─ Extra-articular lesion → CORE DECOMPRESSION
├─ Large symptomatic lesion, young, failed nonop → OSTEOCHONDRAL ALLOGRAFT
└─ Large area / collapse / multiple compartments → TKA
```

**Nonop outcome** — favourable, but **less so than nonop management of SONK**

**Overall** — observation + **treat the underlying systemic condition** · surgery for **advanced disease with severe subchondral collapse**

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| Diagnostic arthroscopy | **Remove small, unstable fragments** |
| **Core decompression** | **Extra-articular lesions** |
| **Osteochondral allograft** | **Large symptomatic lesions, younger patients, failed nonop** |
| **TKA** | **Large area of involvement**, **collapse**, **multiple compartments** |

### Juvenile OCD — activity modification

**12½ yr, stable small JOCD, untreated** — **activity modification** + limited WB until pain resolves\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic stability check · transarticular drilling with 0.045 K-wire · excision + microfracture · mosaicplasty

**Sport cessation** — **4–6 months** may allow healing

**Surgery for JOCD** — **unstable** lesions · no radiographic healing + **still symptomatic after 6 months** nonop · **approaching skeletal maturity** · loose body → fixation, drilling or regenerative procedure (depends on subchondral sclerosis)

**Stable in situ lesion failing nonop** — **transarticular or retroarticular drilling** → good results

**Excision alone** — **poor at 5 yr**; microfracture benefit unclear · symptomatic after excision + microfracture → **mosaicplasty**

**JOCD vs adult** — better healing potential, but **up to 50% fail to heal nonop** (Wall et al.) · 50–75% heal without fragmentation (Orthobullets, OCD chapter)

## X vs Y

| | **Osteonecrosis (risk-factor)** | **SONK** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | **Risk factors** (steroids, alcohol, sickle cell, hypercoagulable) | **Subchondral insufficiency fracture** ± meniscal root tear |
| Extent | **Multiple compartments, metaphysis, 80% bilateral, multifocal** | — |
| Nonop result | Favourable | **Better** |

## How it's asked

**"Stable small JOCD, 12½ yr, untreated — initial?"** → **activity modification**\
✗ Wrong options: drilling · excision · mosaicplasty

**"Most sensitive / specific imaging?"** → **MRI (T1 dark, T2 bright)**

**"Extra-articular lesion — surgery?"** → **core decompression**

**"Collapse or multicompartment disease?"** → **TKA**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Patellar Instability

*Knee & Sports · 37 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **2–3% of all knee injuries** (Orthobullets; 2–3% of knee presentations, TO) · first-time dislocation **5.8 / 100,000 / yr** · recurrence risk 3.8 / 100,000

**Who** — **2nd–3rd decade** · usually 10–25 yr (OB) · **10–17 yr** high risk (TO) · young active females

**Hemarthrosis** — acute dislocation = **2nd most common cause of traumatic knee hemarthrosis**

**Recurrence after 1st dislocation** — **15–60%** (Orthobullets) · **17–49%** → **up to 70% after a 2nd dislocation** (TO) · **\~1/3** (OB) · 15–50% at 2–5 yr (OB) — read the options

### Risk factors for first & recurrent dislocation (age, prior episode, trochlear dysplasia, laxity)

| Risk factor | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Previous instability event** | **Strongest predictor** of persistent instability (✗ Wrong options: younger age · ↑ Q-angle · male · lateral tilt) |
| **Age < 20 at 1st dislocation** | ↑ recurrence · **age > 20 = protective** |
| Recurrence predictors (OB) | **Age, open physes, patella alta, trochlear dysplasia, ↑ TT-TG** |
| NOT recurrence predictors (OB) | **MPFL rupture**, **female sex**, CD 1.1 (normal), **TT-TG < 12 mm** |
| Contralateral knee | High + lateral patella, lateral hypermobility, dysplastic VMO → ↑ risk (congenital) |
| **Miserable malalignment** | **Femoral anteversion + genu valgum + external tibial torsion / pronated feet → ↑ Q-angle** |
| Osseous | Patella alta, trochlear dysplasia, lateral tilt (in extension), **LFC hypoplasia** |
| Muscle | **VMO weakness** · overpull of **ITB + vastus lateralis** |
| Systemic | Laxity — **Ehlers-Danlos**, **Marfan**, family history |
| Anatomic cut-offs (TO) | **CDI ≥ 1.2**, **patellar tilt ≥ 20°**, **TT-TG ≥ 20 mm**, trochlear dysplasia |

**Predisposing EXCEPT** — **insufficiency of the vastus lateralis** · VMO insufficiency + tight lateral retinaculum = predisposing\
✗ Wrong options: excess femoral IR · external tibial rotation · LFC hypoplasia · ↑ Q-angle

**Beighton 6/9, hip IR 70°, genu valgum, patella alta** — **patellar instability**\
✗ Wrong options: ACL rupture · patellar tendon rupture · MFC osteochondral lesion (dislocation gives **lateral** condyle lesions) · lateral bucket-handle

**Sex** — females ↑ **incidence** (↑ Q-angle) but female sex **not** a predictor of recurrence (OB)

### MPFL anatomy & function (Schöttle point, restraint 0–30°)

**Most important static stabiliser (TO)** — **MPFL** in a structurally normal knee\
✗ Wrong options: VMO · Q-angle · TT:TG ratio

| Stabiliser (TO) | Structures |
|---|---|
| **Static** | Trochlear shape (lateral edge elevated, contains patella after 30°), patellar shape / position, medial retinaculum, **MPFL** |
| **Dynamic** | **Vastus medialis (VMO)** — attaches to MPFL; stabilises in early flexion |
| **Deeper flexion (> 30°)** | **Bony** — trochlear morphology, patellar height, tracking |

**MPFL size** — 4.5–6.4 cm × 1.9 cm · **208 N** tensile strength

**Femoral origin** — **between medial epicondyle and adductor tubercle** ("saddle") · **proximal to sMCL** · proximal + posterior to medial epicondyle · **anterior + distal to adductor tubercle**\
✗ Wrong options: on adductor tubercle = adductor magnus · distal to sMCL · lateral wall of MFC = PCL

**sMCL femoral attachment** — \~3.2 mm proximal, 4.8 mm posterior to medial epicondyle (OB)

**Patellar insertion** — **junction of proximal + middle thirds of medial border** (Orthobullets) · **proximal half** of medial border (OB) · undersurface of VMO

**Function** — **primary passive restraint to lateral patellar translation 0–30°** (Orthobullets, TO, OB) · **0–20°** (other OB, PF-joint note) — read the options · lax in deeper flexion

**Function traps** — valgus at 30° = **MCL** · ER + posterior tibial translation = **POL** (AMRI) · knee flexion = **gastrocnemius** · hip adduction = **adductor magnus**

**Schöttle point (lateral X-ray)** — **1 mm anterior to line extended from posterior cortex of shaft** · **2.5 mm distal** to **adductor tubercle** (Orthobullets) · to **posterior origin of MFC** (OB) · **proximal to posterior point of Blumensaat's line** · true lateral with **both posterior condyles superimposed** · = **isometric point** of the graft (OB)

![Schöttle point — MPFL femoral attachment](../../MCQ/attachments/q3611-illa-schottle-lines.jpg)

**Schöttle traps** — ACL femoral = posterior + distal to Blumensaat (in notch) · PCL = lateral wall MFC, posterior to Blumensaat · LCL = lateral femoral condyle

**Paediatric** — femoral fixation **more anterior / distal** to Schöttle point (physis)

### Patellofemoral mechanics – contact, sulcus angle, Q-angle (TO)

**Maximum PF contact (TO)** — **90°** · contact area ↑ 30° → 90° · **> 90°** quadriceps tendon contacts trochlea · **90–135° odd facet** contacts MFC · **45°** in the Orthobullets PF-joint note — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: 30° · 60° · 120°

**PFJRF (TO, this topic)** — **level walking < stair ascent < stair descent < running < squat < deep squat < jumping** · **descent > ascent** · maximal in deep squat + jumping · the PF-joint topic's TO places **jumping just after walking** — read the options

**Q-angle** — **ASIS → centre of patella → tibial tubercle** · **14° M / 17° F** (TO) · 14° M / 18° F (OB) · 13° M / 18° F (Orthobullets PF joint) · **measure in slight flexion** (full extension = falsely normal, patella unengaged)

**↑ Q-angle (TO)** — **↑ femoral anteversion**, **external tibial torsion**, **lateral tibial tubercle**, **genu valgum**\
✗ Wrong options: medialised tuberosity · internal tibial torsion · genu varum

**Standing alignment (TO)** — < 11° normal · **≥ 20° = risk factor needing correction**

### Patellofemoral pain risk factors (TO)

**Risk factors (TO)** — flat / pronated feet, **gastrocnemius tightness**, **patellar instability without laxity**, excessive femoral anteversion + tibia vara

**EXCEPT (TO)** — **ligamentous laxity without patellar instability** (not necessarily painful)

## 2 Mechanism

**Commonest** — **noncontact twisting, knee extended, foot externally rotated** (external tibial rotation, foot fixed)

**Reduction** — reflex **quadriceps contraction** reduces patella → **osteochondral fracture as patella relocates**

**Less common** — **direct blow to medial knee** (knee-to-knee, helmet)

**Impact** — **medial patellar facet** strikes **lateral trochlear ridge / LFC** → bone bruises at both

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — instability, anterior knee pain, painful **"pop" / "clunk"**, often **spontaneous reduction**

**Acute signs** — **large hemarthrosis** · **medial tenderness over MPFL** / medial facet / medial epicondyle · no swelling → laxity / habitual

**Patellar translation** — quadrants (midline = 0) vs other side · **normal < 2** · medial border to lateral trochlear edge = **2 quadrants = abnormal** · acute dislocation: **> 2 quadrants at 30°**

**Apprehension** — guarding with passive lateral translation · **moving patellar apprehension test 0–45°** most sensitive in chronic / lax knees (TO)

**Medial apprehension (TO)** — after **over-aggressive lateral release**, **over-tensioned MPFL**, **overcorrected tibial tubercle**

**J sign** — lateral in extension → "pops" into groove in early flexion (inverted J) · **associated with patella alta**

**Tight lateral retinaculum (TO)** — **cannot evert patella to neutral** (parallel to table) · repeat at **EUA** → decides **lateral lengthening**

**Beighton (TO)** — thumb to forearm, MCP hyperextension, elbow hyperextension, knee recurvatum (bilateral) + palms to floor → **/9**

**Rotational exam (TO)** — prone femoral anteversion (greater trochanter parallel to floor) + **thigh-foot axis**

**Aspiration** — tense effusion · **fat globules → fracture**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| X-ray | Exclude fracture / loose body — **medial patellar facet (most common)**, LFC |
| AP (± scannogram, TO) | **Malalignment**, OA, angular deformity |
| **Lateral** | **Trochlear dysplasia** + **patellar height** · **Blumensaat line → inferior pole at 30°** |
| **Sunrise / Merchant** | **Lateral tilt**, lateral PF angle, congruence + sulcus angles |
| **CT** | **TT-TG** |
| **MRI** | Loose bodies, **bone bruise medial patella + LFC**, **MPFL tear**, TT-PCL, trochlear depth |

**Bone bruise — dislocation** — **medial patellar facet + anterior / lateral LFC**\
✗ Wrong options: patellar tendon / quadriceps rupture · ACL · PCL · LCL

**Bone bruise — ACL** — LFC at **sulcus terminalis (central)** + **posterior lateral tibial plateau**; patella spared

**Trochlear dysplasia (TO)** — present in **96%** with objective dislocation · trochlear groove normally **0.8 mm posterior** to anterior femoral cortex line

| Lateral X-ray sign | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Crossing sign** | Trochlear floor reaches anterior border of condyles → **flat groove** at that point; **96%** of true dislocations |
| **Double contour** | Anterior LFC border **anterior** to MFC border → convex groove / **hypoplastic medial facet** |
| **Supratrochlear spur** | Proximal (superolateral) trochlea → **more severe** dysplasia |

**Trochlear depth (TO)** — **< 3 mm** on axial MRI = dysplasia (sensitive + specific)

### Patellar instability classification

| Type | Features |
|---|---|
| **Acute traumatic** | M = F; may follow direct blow |
| **Chronic patholaxity** | Recurrent subluxation; **more in women**; malalignment |
| **Habitual** | **Painless**, every flexion; **proximal** cause (tight ITB, vastus lateralis); no effusion |

### Patellar height

| Index | Measure | Normal | Alta | Baja |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Blumensaat** | Lateral at **30°** | Inferior pole at line | Above | Below |
| **Insall-Salvati** | **Patellar tendon length ÷ patellar length** | **0.8–1.2** | **> 1.2** | **< 0.8** |
| **Blackburne-Peel** | Plateau line → lower articular margin ÷ articular length | **0.5–1.0** | **> 1.0** | **< 0.5** |
| **Caton-Deschamps** | Lower articular margin → anterior tibial plateau ÷ articular length | **0.6–1.3** (\~1) | **> 1.3** (TO) · **≥ 1.2** risk / **> 1.2** surgery (TO) | **< 0.6** |
| Plateau-patella angle | — | **20–30°** | — | — |

**Insall-Salvati (TO)** — **ratio of patellar tendon to patella** (✗ Wrong options: patella to tendon) · **> 1.2 = alta** (✗ Wrong options: baja) · tendon measured along its **posterior surface**, lower pole → tibial insertion

**Best index (TO)** — **Caton-Deschamps "best"** · Blackburne-Peel **more accurate than IS** (lower articular margin easier to define)

![Blumensaat, Insall-Salvati, Blackburne-Peel, Caton-Deschamps](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17141203-1.png)

### Axial & rotational measurements

| Measure | Normal | Abnormal / meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **Lateral PF angle** | **> 11°** (Orthobullets) · **> 0°, opens laterally in 97%** (TO) | **< 11°** = excess tilt (OB) · subluxation: **parallel 60%**, opens medially 40% |
| **Congruence angle** | **−6°** (medial = negative) | **> +16°** (lateral) → subluxation |
| **Sulcus angle** | **138–142°** (TO) · Merchant **138° ± 6** · **130–145°** (TO answer) · **137° ± 8°** (OB) | **> 140°** (Orthobullets, OB) · **> 145°** (OB, Dejour A) · **> 150°** (TO) — read the options |
| **Patellar tilt** | **< 5°** (OB) | **> 20°** → tight lateral retinaculum, lateral release / lengthening |
| **TT-TG (CT)** | **9–13 mm** (Orthobullets) · **8–10 mm** (OB) | **> 20 mm** highly associated with instability · **< 12 mm** not a risk factor |
| **TT-PCL (MRI, TO)** | **11.9 ± 4.67 mm** | **> 20 mm** pathological · **> 24** in TO procedure table |

**TT-TG** — perpendiculars from posterior condylar line to **tibial tubercle** and **trochlear groove** · highly specific for recurrence (TO)

![TT-TG distance](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3020-tt-tg-distance.jpg)

**TT-PCL (TO)** — tubercle midpoint → **medial border of PCL** · tibial references only → **negates femoral rotation**

### Dejour classification of trochlear dysplasia

| Type | Lateral X-ray | Axial | Trochleoplasty |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | Crossing sign | Shallow, symmetric, concave · sulcus **> 145°** | **None** — not high-grade |
| **B** | Crossing + **supratrochlear spur** | **Flat or convex** | **Sulcus-deepening** |
| **C** | Crossing + **double contour** | **Convex lateral facet + hypoplastic medial** | no deepening (no spur) · lateral facet elevating (controversial) |
| **D** | Crossing + **spur + double contour** | **Cliff** (vertical asymmetric facets) | **Sulcus-deepening** |

**Severity** — **A + C mild** · **B + D severe** (spur = what is resected)

![Dejour classification](../../MCQ/attachments/q211634-illa-dejour-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Lateral patellar dislocation
├─ First-time, MRI: no fragment / loose body → nonoperative: brace + PT
├─ First-time + osteochondral fracture / loose body → arthroscopic removal vs fixation ± MPFL repair
│      (fragment on X-ray → MRI before surgery)
├─ Recurrent, normal bony anatomy → isolated MPFL reconstruction
└─ Recurrent + bony risk factor → MPFLR + TTO / distalisation / trochleoplasty
```

### Nonoperative care

**Indications** — **first-time dislocator without loose body / intra-articular damage** · **habitual dislocator**

**Protocol** — short immobilisation → **6 wk controlled motion** (Orthobullets) · **2 wk brace locked at 0°** → ROM brace (TO)

**Rehab** — **closed chain short arc quads**, core + hip (abductors, gluteals) · patellar sleeve / **"J" brace**

**First dislocation (TO)** — **operative only for osteochondral fracture** · recurrence = secondary indication\
✗ Wrong options: MPFL reconstruction · always conservative · MPFL + retinaculum repair

**Surgical indication after 1st dislocation (TO)** — **X-ray shows osteochondral fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: complete MPFL rupture on MRI · MPFL laxity · grade B dysplasia

**13-yr-old female, no prior history of knee injury, MRI findings Figure A (MPFL rupture, no fracture / loose body)** — **bracing + PT**\
✗ Wrong options: recurrent + TT-TG 26 → distal realignment · Figure B: MPFL rupture + medial facet fracture + loose bodies → arthroscopy · recurrent despite PT → MPFLR

### Acute dislocation – MRI findings, osteochondral fragments & initial management

**Most consistent injury (TO)** — **MPFL tear**\
✗ Wrong options: patellotibial / patellomeniscal ligaments · LFC trochlea fracture · VMO disruption

**MPFL tear site** — **patellar insertion** commonest (Orthobullets) · **femoral attachment** common avulsion site (OB) · **soft-tissue avulsion** most common pattern; midsubstance + soft-tissue avulsion > **bony avulsion** (OB) — read the options

**Fragment origin** — **medial patellar facet (most common)** > **LFC** / lateral trochlear ridge · **transverse patella fracture not associated**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral facet · odd facet · medial / central trochlea

![Medial patellar facet osteochondral fragment after dislocation](../../MCQ/attachments/q211667-c-img-5547-2.jpg)

**Radiograph after self-reduced dislocation, 3 quadrants** — **Figure C = medial patellar facet fragment**\
✗ Wrong options: A lateral facet fracture · B knee dislocation · D LFC fracture (less common) · E inferior pole / transverse fracture

**Fragment on X-ray + large effusion** — **next = magnetic resonance imaging (MRI)**\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs + PT · aspiration + cortisone · ORIF · arthroscopy before MRI

**Fragments missed on X-ray** — **up to 40%** of first dislocations have large fragments → MRI

**12-yr-old, 1st dislocation, large fragment in lateral recess + partial MPFL tear on MRI** — **arthroscopic loose body removal versus osteochondral fragment fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: aspirate + brace · PT · immediate isolated MPFLR · TTO + MPFLR ± trochleoplasty

**Fixation** — screws / pins if enough bone; else removal (arthroscopic or open)

**Vertical intra-articular patella fracture after dislocation (TO)** — **internal fixation (ORIF)**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopy · cylinder cast · patellectomy

| Patella fracture (TO) | Rule |
|---|---|
| Commonest mechanism | **Direct impaction on flexed knee**; indirect = eccentric quads contraction |
| **Nonop** | Displacement **< 4 mm**, step-off **< 2 mm**, **active SLR intact** → brace \~10° flexion, neutral rotation (avoid baja) |
| **Surgery** | Displacement **> 4 mm** and / or step-off **> 2 mm**, extensor disruption |
| Other ORIF | Open fracture, articular gap **> 3 mm**, loose bodies, **osteochondral fractures**, paediatric **sleeve fracture** |

### MPFL repair vs reconstruction

**MPFL repair** — **acute first-time dislocation with bony fragment** / avulsion with intact midsubstance · within first few days · no studies prove it beats nonop · not after many dislocations (attenuated tissue)

**MPFL reconstruction** — **recurrent instability without significant malalignment** · autograft or allograft

**Graft** — **gracilis or semitendinosus** (stronger than native), two-tailed · **open physes → medial quadriceps turndown on suture anchors** (no femoral tunnel, TO)

**Graft choice** — **all equal rates of postoperative instability** (allograft = autograft = quadriceps = synthetic) · infection + PROs also equal

**Complications (TO)** — knee stiffness, **patella fracture**, continued instability · femoral graft position is critical

### MPFL reconstruction – femoral tunnel malposition

| Femoral tunnel | Result |
|---|---|
| **Too proximal ("high and tight")** | **Graft tight in flexion** → loss of flexion / failure, overconstrains medially |
| **Too distal ("low and loose")** | Graft **loose in flexion** |
| Too posterior | Not tighter in flexion than a proximal tunnel |
| **Schöttle point** | Correct — anteroproximal quadrant |

**Proximal tunnel traps** — ✗ Wrong options: tight in extension (minimal MPFL tension in extension) · lateral tilt · patella baja · cause = **cam-shaped distal femur**

**Tension** — at **30° flexion** (OB)

### Operative criteria

| Finding | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Recurrent, no significant malalignment** | **Isolated MPFLR** |
| **TT-TG > 20 mm (CT)** · TT-PCL > 24 | **TTO** (AMZ / Elmslie-Trillat) ± MPFLR |
| **Patella alta, CDI > 1.2** | **Distalisation** ± MPFLR |
| **Dejour B or D** | **Trochleoplasty + MPFLR** ± TTO |
| Dejour A or C | MPFLR ± TTO |
| **Femoral anteversion > 25°** | **Femoral derotation osteotomy + MPFLR** |
| **Lateral tilt > 20°** / tight lateral retinaculum | Lateral release (TO) / **lengthening** (OB) + MPFLR |
| **Genu valgum > 10°**, ≥ 6 mo growth | **Guided growth** (8-plate, staples) |

### Surgical selection – isolated MPFL vs tibial tubercle osteotomy (TT-TG) vs trochleoplasty

**Isolated MPFLR (TO)** — **TT-TG ≤ 20 mm**, **normal or Dejour A trochlea**, **CDI < 1.2**, **tilt < 20°**

**Isolated MPFLR (OB)** — **IS < 1.2**, **TT-TG < 20 mm**, **sulcus < 140°**, **negative crossing sign** · e.g. **Insall-Salvati ratio 1.15, TT-TG distance 15 mm, sulcus angle 138°, negative crossing sign**\
✗ Wrong options: sulcus 150° + crossing · TT-TG 21 · IS 1.3 · IS 1.25 + TT-TG 20 + sulcus 150°

**Best isolated-MPFLR candidate** — **recurrent patellar dislocation, 3 quadrants lateral translation at 30 degrees knee flexion, presence of J sign, TT-TG of 15mm**\
✗ Wrong options: first-time (surgery controversial) · TT-TG 24 → consider TTO

**Residual instability after isolated MPFLR** — **severe trochlear dysplasia = most important predictor** · failure risks: dysplasia, ↑ TT-TG, **age < 20** · IS ratio + tibial slope **not** factors

**14-yr-old recurrent, TT-TG 20, sulcus 150°** — **presence of trochlear dysplasia places him at risk for recurrent instability**\
✗ Wrong options: MPFLR alone poor (it does well) · IS 1.2 protective (normal) · age protective · slope 15°

**Recurrent, TT-TG 12, CDI 1.2, no dysplasia, 7 dislocations** — **MPFL reconstruction alone**\
✗ Wrong options: MPFL repair (attenuated) · TTO medialisation / distalisation · trochleoplasty

**3rd dislocation, mild valgus, CDI 1.2, TT-TG 14.5 mm, mild dysplasia, wants fast RTS** — **MPFL reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: MPFL repair · TTO alone · MPFLR + TTO (longer recovery, no proven benefit) · lateral release

**TT-TG 20 exactly** — isolated MPFLR still good · some add TTO only if **> 20** · TTO importance controversial

**TTO threshold** — usually **not** done if TT-TG < 15–20 mm (e.g. 13 mm → no TTO)

### Tibial tubercle osteotomy (Fulkerson / AMZ)

**Fulkerson** — oblique osteotomy → tubercle **anterior + medial** (anteromedialisation, AMZ) · medialisation restores TT-TG, anteriorisation **unloads PF** · cut **long (> 5 cm) + thick (> 0.75 cm)** for **2 screws** (OB)

**Indication (OB)** — **TT-TG > 20 mm** (e.g. **25mm**) + recurrent instability ± cartilage restoration\
✗ Wrong options: open apophysis · medial defect · sulcus 165° · tilt 25°

**Indication (TO)** — **grade 3 and 4 chondromalacia patella and recurrent patellar dislocation** (**lateral facet or distal pole** arthritis) + TT-TG > 20 mm\
✗ Wrong options: habitual dislocation · grade 1–2 · Osgood-Schlatter

**Contact pressures after AMZ** — **↑ proximal & medial** → **proximal / medial facet arthritis = contraindication** · medial patellar defect = relative contraindication\
✗ Wrong options: distal · lateral

**Correction target** — **TT-TG 10–15 mm, never < 10** (Orthobullets) · **\~10 mm** (TO) · TO: medialisation = TT-TG − 20 mm

**Other TTOs (TO)** — **Elmslie-Trillat = direct medial** · **Maquet = direct anterior** · TTO when **TT-TG > 20**, ↑ TT-PCL, **CDI > 1.2**

**Distalisation (TO)** — for **CDI > 1.2** → target **CDI 1** on C-arm · usually **\~10 mm** bone removed

**Open tibial apophysis** — **absolute contraindication** → growth arrest → **recurvatum** · paediatric: **preserve physis**

**TTO complications (TO)** — **tuberosity nonunion**, painful hardware, **tibial shaft fracture** · longer recovery

**Skin approach** — midline incision → **infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve** → inferolateral numbness

### Trochleoplasty

**Best suited (TO)** — **Dejour type B and D** (spur)\
✗ Wrong options: A + B · B + C · C + D

**Indications (TO)** — **high-grade dysplasia** + instability / maltracking, **no established OA** · revision with residual instability · rarely done in USA

**Not for (TO)** — **flatness alone** (spur must be present) · type A (no trochlear procedure) · type C (lateral facet elevating instead)

**Contraindications (TO)** — **open physes**, **PF arthrosis**

**Complications (TO)** — **arthrofibrosis / stiffness (most frequent)** · **trochlear shingle fracture** propagation (brittle subchondral bone in PF arthritis)

**Techniques** — arthroscopic or open sulcus deepening · open recession wedge

**Recurrent + Dejour D (cliff + spur)** — **MPFL reconstruction with trochleoplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: MPFL repair ± VMO · TTO alone · MPFLR alone (residual instability)

### Lateral release – lateral patellofemoral angle

**Isolated lateral release** — **not indicated for instability** → **iatrogenic medial instability**

**Lateral lengthening vs release** — lengthening: **better outcomes**, less quadriceps atrophy, **less medial instability** · only for **excess tilt / tightness after medialisation**

**2nd dislocation, 3 quadrants, J sign, tight lateral retinaculum, TT-TG 13, CDI 1.15** — **MPFL reconstruction + lateral retinacular lengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated release / lengthening · MPFLR + release · AMZ TTO

**Main indication for isolated release** — **lateral patellar (facet) compression syndrome**, no instability

**Measurement for lateral release** — **lateral patello-femoral angle** (**negative tilt**) on CT / sunrise\
✗ Wrong options: congruence angle · Q-angle · sulcus angle · patellar height

**Congruence + sulcus angles** — assess **malalignment + instability**, not release

### Other bony procedures

**Guided growth** — **genu valgum > 10°** + instability + **≥ 6 months growth** · tension band **8-plate** or **staples** (more rigid, faster)

**Femoral osteotomy (TO)** — severe valgus → distal femoral osteotomy · **anteversion > 25°** → supracondylar derotation + MPFLR · hemiepiphysiodesis in immature valgus

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Recurrent dislocation** | Nonop **15–60% at 2–5 yr** · highest if 1st dislocation **< 20 yr** |
| **Medial dislocation + medial PF arthritis** | **Almost exclusively iatrogenic** (prior stabilisation) |
| Inferolateral numbness | **Infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve** (TTO midline incision) |
| MPFLR | Stiffness, patella fracture, recurrence, high-and-tight tunnel |
| TTO / trochleoplasty | Nonunion, tibial fracture / arthrofibrosis, shingle fracture |

## X vs Y

| | **Patellar dislocation** | **ACL tear** |
|---|---|---|
| Bone bruise | **Medial patella + anterior / lateral LFC** | **LFC sulcus terminalis + posterolateral tibial plateau** |
| Patella involved | **Yes** | No |

| | **High and tight** | **Low and loose** |
|---|---|---|
| Tunnel | **Too proximal** | Too distal |
| Graft | **Tight in flexion** | Loose in flexion |

| | **Dejour A / C** | **Dejour B / D** |
|---|---|---|
| Spur | No | **Yes** |
| Severity | Mild | **Severe** |
| Trochleoplasty | No (C: lateral facet elevation) | **Sulcus deepening** |

| | **Lateral release** | **Lateral lengthening** |
|---|---|---|
| Instability | Not alone → medial instability | Adjunct to MPFLR |
| Outcome | Worse | **Better, less medial instability** |

## How it's asked

**"Most important static stabiliser of patella (TO)?"** → **MPFL**

**"Most consistent injury after lateral dislocation (TO)?"** → **MPFL tear**

**"First dislocation — recommended treatment (TO)?"** → **operative only in case of osteochondral fracture**

**"First-time dislocation — surgical indication (TO)?"** → **X-ray shows osteochondral fracture**

**"Self-reduced dislocation, skyline shows vertical fracture (TO)?"** → **internal fixation**

**"Fulkerson osteotomy is done for (TO)?"** → **grade 3–4 chondromalacia + recurrent dislocation**

**"Sulcus-deepening trochleoplasty suits (TO)?"** → **Dejour B and D**

**"Maximum patella-trochlea contact (TO)?"** → **90°**

**"Normal sulcus angle (TO)?"** → **130–145°**

**"Q-angle increased by (TO)?"** → **increased femoral anteversion**

**"PF pain risk factors EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **ligamentous laxity without patellar instability**

**"Insall-Salvati — true statements (TO)?"** → **> 1.2 = alta; ratio of patellar tendon to patella**

**"Predisposing factor EXCEPT?"** → **insufficiency of the vastus lateralis**

**"Highest risk of persistent instability?"** → **previous patellar instability event**

**"Protective factor for recurrence?"** → **age > 20**

**"13-yr-old, 1st dislocation — recurrence risk factor?"** → **age**\
✗ Wrong options: MPFL rupture · sex

**"Beighton 6/9, valgus, hip IR 70°, alta — at risk of?"** → **patellar instability**

**"Risk after MPFL reconstruction, sulcus 150°?"** → **trochlear dysplasia → recurrent instability**

**"Most likely MRI injury site?"** → **soft-tissue avulsion of MPFL**

**"Femoral MPFL landmark on lateral X-ray?"** → **anterior to posterior cortical line, proximal to Blumensaat**

**"Structure between medial epicondyle and adductor tubercle?"** → **MPFL**

**"Asterisk 1 mm anterior to posterior cortex — which reconstruction?"** → **MPFL**

**"Femoral attachment of injured structure (twisting, clunk)?"** → **MFC, distal to adductor tubercle**

**"Primary function of structure between AT and ME?"** → **restraint to lateral patellar translation 0–30°**

**"Graft tightens with flexion — position?"** → **A (too proximal)**

**"Where to drill the MPFL femoral tunnel?"** → **A — Schöttle point**

**"Femoral tunnel too proximal?"** → **graft tight in flexion**

**"MPFL graft with highest instability?"** → **all equal**

**"Loose fragment origin?"** → **medial patellar facet**

**"Axial MRI — medial patella + lateral LFC bruise?"** → **lateral patellar dislocation**

**"Medial facet + anterior LFC signal?"** → **patellar dislocation**

**"Which radiograph after self-reduced dislocation?"** → **Figure C (medial facet fragment)**

**"Nonop bracing + PT best for?"** → **13-yr-old, first time, no fragment**

**"Medial patellar avulsion fragment, effusion — next step?"** → **MRI**

**"12-yr-old, large fragment on MRI?"** → **arthroscopic removal vs fixation**

**"AMZ indicated by?"** → **TT-TG 25 mm**

**"Best isolated MPFLR candidate?"** → **recurrent, 3 quadrants, J sign, TT-TG 15 mm**

**"Imaging parameters for arthroscopy + isolated MPFLR?"** → **IS 1.15, TT-TG 15, sulcus 138°, no crossing sign**

**"Contact pressure after Fulkerson?"** → **proximal + medial**

**"Tight lateral retinaculum, TT-TG 13?"** → **MPFLR + lateral retinacular lengthening**

**"7 dislocations, TT-TG 12, CDI 1.2, no dysplasia?"** → **MPFL reconstruction alone**

**"3rd dislocation, TT-TG 14.5, wants fast return?"** → **MPFL reconstruction**

**"Cliff pattern + spur, 5 dislocations?"** → **MPFL reconstruction + trochleoplasty**

**"Measurement indicating lateral release?"** → **lateral patello-femoral angle**
