# Patellar Tendinitis

*Knee & Sports · 38 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Risk factors & diagnosis

**Incidence** — up to **20% of jumping athletes** (Orthobullets) · prevalence **8.5%** in non-elite athletes (OB) — **volleyball highest (14.4%)**, soccer lowest (2.5%)

**Who** — **adolescents / young adults** · **M > F (2:1)** · **quadriceps** tendinopathy = older adults

**Risk factors** — **poor quadriceps + hamstring flexibility** · **hamstring tightness** · male, **younger, taller, heavier**, type of sport (jumping) · patellar hypermobility · quadriceps atrophy · **hard surfaces** · **↑ training frequency**

**Predisposing factor (OB)** — **hamstring tightness**\
✗ Wrong options: female · ↑ quadriceps strength · soft surfaces · low-frequency training

**Risk factor EXCEPT** — **older age**\
✗ Wrong options: male · higher weight · quads / hamstring inflexibility · type of sport

**Witvrouw vs Zwerver (OB)** — **inflexibility = only significant factor**; height, weight, age, Q-angle, LLD, strength not (Witvrouw) · **younger, taller, heavier** = risk (Zwerver) — read the options

**Malalignment** — **not strongly associated** · malalignment → generalised PF syndrome, maltracking

**Mechanism** — **repetitive forceful eccentric contraction** of extensor mechanism · jumping, acceleration / deceleration, deep flexion

**Histology** — **degenerative, not inflammatory** · **micro-tears** at bone-tendon junction of **inferior pole** · angiofibroblastic + mucoid degeneration\
✗ Wrong options: intra-substance inflammatory reaction

**Theory (Rath, OB)** — **impingement of deep tendon fibres on inferior pole** · flexion + quads contraction ↓ pain

### Sinding-Larsen-Johansson & jumper's knee – site (TO)

**Jumper's knee (TO)** — tendinitis at **tendo-osseous junction of lower (inferior) pole of patella** · common in jumpers\
✗ Wrong options: **superior pole**

**Tooth sign (TO)** — **periosteal reaction on anterior surface of patella** · seen in jumper's knee (TO)

**Jumper's knee treatment (TO)** — eccentric quads strengthening, avoid provoking activity, taping, **deep transverse friction massage**, cryotherapy

**Sinding-Larsen-Johansson (TO)** — **osteochondrosis of inferior pole of patella** · often **bilateral** · **overuse** traction by patellar tendon on partly cartilaginous lower pole · **active adolescents 10–14 yr**

**SLJ clinical (TO)** — pain + tenderness **at lower pole of patella** (↑ loading in flexion), subpatellar oedema · later tendon thickening, **fragmentation of lower pole**, bursitis

**SLJ EXCEPT (TO)** — **pain at lower end of patellar tendon** (false) — pain is at **proximal tendon / inferior pole**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **insidious anterior knee pain at inferior border of patella** · early: **after** activity · late: **during** activity · **"movie theater sign"** (prolonged flexion)

**Exam** — swelling over tendon, tenderness inferior pole · local soft-tissue oedema but **no effusion**, no apprehension, no crepitus

**Basset's sign** — **tender at distal pole in full extension**, **not in full flexion** → **tenderness greater in extension than flexion** ✓

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| X-ray (AP, lateral, skyline) | **Usually normal** · chronic: **inferior traction spur (enthesophyte)** |
| **Ultrasound** | Tendon thickening, **hypoechoic areas** |
| **MRI** (chronic, surgical planning) | **Tendon thickening** (more diagnostic than oedema) · **↑ signal on T1 + T2** at proximal tendon (✗ Wrong options: decreased) · loss of posterior fat-pad border (chronic) · partial tear |

![Hypoechoic patellar tendon on ultrasound](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3015-hypoechoic-ultrasound.jpg)

![Proximal patellar tendinopathy on MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3015-mri.jpg)

### Blazina classification

| Stage | Orthobullets (3 phases) | OB illustration (4 stages) |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | Pain **after** activity only | Pain only after sports |
| **II** | Pain **during and after** activity | Pain at start, goes after warm-up, returns with fatigue |
| **III** | **Persistent pain with or without activity** (↓ performance) | **Constant pain at rest and with activity** |
| **IV** | — | **Complete rupture of patellar tendon** |

**Stage III** — pain **at rest** + with activity · **surgical indication** if refractory

## 5 Management

```
Patellar tendinopathy
├─ Early / Blazina I–II → rest, activity modification, quads strengthening, eccentric PT
├─ Blazina III, in season → eccentric PT + rest from sport and jumping
└─ Refractory / Blazina III / partial tear → surgical excision + repair
```

### Nonoperative care – eccentric training & rest

**First line** — ice, **rest, activity modification**, PT (**quads + hamstring stretching**, **eccentric** programme, **quadriceps strengthening**, isometric + short-arc work) · NSAIDs, moist heat, ultrasound, phonophoresis

**Offloading** — taping or **Chopat's strap** ↓ tension across patellar tendon

**Eccentric = isotonic with muscle lengthening** ✓\
✗ Wrong options: plyometric · isotonic shortening = concentric · isometric

**As effective as surgery** — **eccentric training** · also works for Achilles insertional tendinopathy\
✗ Wrong options: electrotherapy · concentric · massage · taping (none proven)

**Most recommended exercise (TO)** — **decline squat** · **3 × 15 reps, twice daily**, single-leg eccentric squat, upright torso, on a decline board\
✗ Wrong options: plyometric · treadmill protocol · concentric loading

**Why eccentric (TO)** — **promotes tendon remodelling** (collagen) · ↓ recurrence

**Plyometric (TO)** — rapid eccentric stretch → concentric of same muscle (jumping, hopping) · builds **power** · **late** rehab · early → can exacerbate (OB)

**Corticosteroid injection** — **contraindicated** → **patellar tendon rupture** · no benefit at 6 months (eccentric + heavy slow resistance keep effect)

**PRP / sclerosing agents** — **experimental**, inconsistent · PRP not first line

**18-yr-old, 6 wk, plays through pain** — **rest, activity modification, quadriceps strengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: low-dose steroid · PRP · arthroscopic debridement · **no sport until pain-free** (complete rest → weakness + inflexibility)

**Blazina III, in season, pain at rest** — eccentric PT + **rest from sport and jumping** · playing through season → no improvement\
✗ Wrong options: steroid · debridement without nonop trial · PRP · sclerosant

### Refractory cases – surgical debridement

**Indications** — **Blazina stage III**, chronic pain / dysfunction refractory to nonop, **partial tears** · severe tendinopathy, extensor disruption

**Technique** — open or arthroscopic · **resect angiofibroblastic + mucoid degenerative tissue** · bone abrasion at insertion · suture repair / anchors

**Rehab** — **immobilisation in extension** → progressive ROM · WBAT

**Outcome** — **80–90% return** to sport · aching 4–6 months · 89% normal / near normal at 12 months (OB)

**18 months pain, can't jump, MRI partial tear** — **debridement and repair**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · aggressive eccentric overload · ice + play on · steroid

**Persistent pain with and without activity, failed PT (pole vaulter)** — **surgical excision of the affected tissue**\
✗ Wrong options: concentric stretching · intratendinous / intra-articular steroid · patellar chondroplasty

## X vs Y

| | **Jumper's knee** | **Sinding-Larsen-Johansson** |
|---|---|---|
| Lesion | Tendinopathy (degenerative) | **Osteochondrosis** of lower pole |
| Age | Adolescent / young adult athlete | **10–14 yr** |
| Site | Tendo-osseous junction, **inferior pole** | **Inferior pole** (proximal tendon) |
| Imaging | Thickening, ↑ T1 / T2 signal · tooth sign (TO) | Fragmentation of lower pole |

| | **Patellar tendinopathy** | **Quadriceps tendinopathy** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | **Adolescents / young adults** | **Older adults** |

## How it's asked

**"Most recommended rehab exercise (TO)?"** → **decline squat**

**"Jumper's knee — all true EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **involves superior pole of patella**

**"Sinding-Larsen-Johansson — all true EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **pain at lower end of patellar tendon**

**"Predisposing factor for chronic patellar tendinitis?"** → **hamstring tightness**

**"Risk factor EXCEPT?"** → **older age**

**"Volleyball player, inferior pole pain — true?"** → **tenderness greater in extension than flexion**

**"Rehab as effective as surgery?"** → **eccentric training**

**"Best rehab protocol?"** → **isotonic with muscle lengthening**

**"Plays through pain, aching with prolonged sitting?"** → **rest, activity modification, quadriceps strengthening**

**"Blazina III in season — add to eccentric PT?"** → **rest from sport and jumping**

**"18 months pain, can't jump, partial tear on MRI?"** → **debridement and repair**

**"Persistent pain with and without activity, failed PT?"** → **surgical excision of the affected tissue**

# Patellar Tendon Rupture

*Knee & Sports · 39 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & risk factors

**Incidence** — rare, **< 1 / 100,000 / yr**

**Who** — **3rd–4th decade** (Orthobullets) · **< 40 yr** (OB) · males in 30s–40s · **M > F**

**Quadriceps vs patellar** — **quadriceps tendon rupture more common (2:1)** · quadriceps **> 40 yr**, patellar **< 40 yr**

**Systemic (→ bilateral)** — **diabetes**, **SLE**, **RA**, **chronic renal disease** · also infectious + metabolic disorders

**Local** — **patellar degeneration (most common)** · previous injury · **patellar tendinopathy**

**Drugs** — **corticosteroid injection** · chronic steroids · **anabolic steroids**

**Pathobiology** — end-stage **chronic tendon degeneration** → eccentric load on a diseased tendon

### Anatomy & biomechanics

**Extensor mechanism** — quadriceps tendon → patella → **patellar tendon** → tibial tubercle

**Blood supply** — **infrapatellar fat pad** + retinacular (**medial + lateral inferior geniculate arteries**)

**Forces** — **3× BW** ascending stairs · **17× BW** to rupture a normal tendon

**After TKA** — approach disrupts extensor blood supply → poorer healing · acute → primary repair · chronic → **allograft reconstruction**

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **tensile overload**: **sudden quadriceps contraction with knee flexed** (jumping, missing a step) · eccentric load (basketball, football, soccer)

**Flexion angle** — **greatest tendon force at > 60° flexion** · PT : QT force ratio **> 1 at < 45°**, **< 1 at > 45°** (distal-pole contact gives quads advantage at low flexion)

| Pattern | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Avulsion ± bone from inferior pole of patella** | **Most common** · strain at tendon-bone interface **3–4× midsubstance** |
| Midsubstance | Less common · reconstruction / augmentation if poor tissue |
| Distal avulsion from tibial tubercle | Least common |

**Associated** — tibial tubercle avulsion, patella fracture, **TKA extensor mechanism rupture**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — **jump / sudden quads contraction**, **"pop"** · infrapatellar pain, **immediate swelling**, can't weight-bear · may mimic ACL (buckling, giving way)

**Inspection** — **high-riding patella**, **large hemarthrosis + ecchymosis**, **palpable gap below inferior pole**

**Straight leg raise** — **cannot actively SLR** or hold passively extended knee · retinaculum intact → active extension with **a few degrees of lag**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| **X-ray — AP + lateral at 30° flexion** (± merchant) | **Patella alta** in complete rupture |
| **MRI** | **Most sensitive** · partial vs complete · site, degeneration, associated injury · **not needed** for complete tear |
| Ultrasound | Detects + localises · **operator-dependent** |

![Patella alta after patellar tendon rupture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3024-patella-alta-lateral-xray.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria — patella alta indices

| Index | Calculation | Normal | **Rupture (alta)** | Baja |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Insall-Salvati** | **Tendon length (Y) ÷ patellar length (X)** | **0.8–1.2** (\~1) | **> 1.2** (e.g. 1.3) | **< 0.8** → quadriceps rupture |
| **Blackburne-Peel** | Plateau line → lower articular margin (Y) ÷ articular length (X) | **0.5–1.0** | **> 1.0** (e.g. **Y/X 1.1**) | < 0.5 |
| **Caton-Deschamps** | Anterior tibial plateau → lower articular margin (Y) ÷ articular length (X) | **0.6–1.3** | **> 1.3** (Orthobullets) · **> 1.2** (OB) | **< 0.6** |

![Blackburne-Peel ratio (Y / X)](../../MCQ/attachments/q217470-b-blackbourne-peel.jpg)

**Ratio traps** — **X/Y** inverted ratios are meaningless · IS 0.95 and CD 1.1 = **normal**

**Most reliable index** — **Insall-Salvati** (highest intra- + inter-observer reliability on X-ray, CT, MRI; Verhulst) · **Blackburne-Peel** most useful — articular-surface based (Seil) — read the options

**Diagnosis** — complete tear = **exam + X-ray** · partial tear may need MRI

### Classification

| Type | Features | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Incomplete** | **Extensor mechanism intact** | Nonop possible |
| **Complete** | **Patella alta + palpable defect** | **Surgical repair** |

## 5 Management

```
Patellar tendon injury
├─ Partial, extensor mechanism intact → brace locked in extension 6 wk, weight bearing
├─ Complete, acute → primary repair within 2 wk
│      (suture anchors: less gap formation)
└─ Chronic > 6–8 wk / degenerate / poor tissue → tendon reconstruction
```

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Complete rupture**, tendon can be approximated | **Primary repair** (end-to-end, transosseous, **suture anchors**) |
| Subacute **> 2 wk** | Repair + **quadplasty / scar release** |
| **Chronic > 6–8 wk**, severely disrupted / degenerate tendon | **Reconstruction** |
| Midsubstance tear, poor tissue | Reconstruction / **graft augmentation** |
| Revision or chronic | **Achilles allograft** |

### Treatment – primary repair (suture anchors vs transosseous)

**Timing** — **repair within 2 weeks** → prevents **extensor mechanism contracture** · **timing = most important prognostic factor**

**End-to-end** — needs a cuff of tissue on both sides · often **not feasible** (rupture at inferior pole, no proximal stump) · nonabsorbable locking sutures

**Transosseous** — **#5 nonabsorbable** sutures through **2 trans-patellar tunnels**, tied over top · ± **cerclage wire / tape** patella → tubercle

**Suture anchors** — **≥ 2 anchors** · **less gap formation** (0.9–4.1 mm vs 2.9–10.3 mm transpatellar) · **↑ pull-out strength / ultimate load to failure** (Orthobullets, OB) · load to failure **not different**, gap still less (Danaher, OB) — read the options

**Anchor vs transosseous — no difference** — **re-operation rate**, infection, PROMs, **return to sport**, release from care

**Re-rupture** — **\~8% transosseous vs 1–2% anchors** (Orthobullets) · **7.5% vs 0%** (O'Dowd, OB)

**Best for gap formation (footballer)** — **suture anchor repair**\
✗ Wrong options: end-to-end · rehab then autograft · Achilles allograft · transosseous

**Anchor construct vs bone tunnels** — **increased pull-out strength**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ re-operation · ↓ infection · better PROMs · quicker RTS

**High load to failure + less gap** — **primary repair with suture anchors**\
✗ Wrong options: 2 or 3 transosseous tunnels · end-to-end · gracilis reconstruction

**24-yr-old, patella alta after rebound** — **primary surgical repair**\
✗ Wrong options: PT then BPTB reconstruction · brace at 30° · medialising TTO + lateral release · arthroscopy

**40-yr-old, alta on X-ray** — **open surgical repair**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI first · ice + observation · PT · arthroscopic repair

**CKD + T2DM, 1 wk after fall** — **primary repair of injured structure**\
✗ Wrong options: + AMZ tubercle transfer (no instability) · immobilisation (partial tears only) · hamstring reconstruction first · CTA (pulses present)

**Partial tear, extensor intact** — **hinged brace locked in extension 6 wk**, weight bearing · progressive **active flexion / passive extension**

### Tendon reconstruction

**Grafts** — ipsilateral **semitendinosus / gracilis**, **central quadriceps tendon-patellar bone** autograft, contralateral **BPTB** autograft or allograft, Achilles allograft

**Technique** — tendons through transosseous holes in patella + tibial tubercle → **circle graft** · retracted patella → quadplasty, scar release

### Postoperative rehabilitation

**Brace** — **locked in extension, immediate weight bearing**, **6 wk** · brace-free by 6 wk

**Early motion** — from **7–10 days** · **passive extension + active flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: any active extension

**ROM exercises** — **heel slides** (active closed-chain flexion) · **prone open-chain flexion** · both with passive extension

**Active extension** — **none for at least 4–6 wk** (OB) · permitted at **3 wk** (Matava, OB) — read the options

**Later** — open-chain strengthening (**leg extensions**) + weight-bearing resistance (**squats, lunges, leg press**)

**Goals** — **full extension + 120° flexion by 6 wk** · **full RTS at 6 months** (100% return at 6 months with cerclage repair, OB)

**Chains** — **closed** = distal segment fixed (squat, leg press, pull-up) · **open** = distal free (leg extension, hamstring curl) · open → more shear · open terminal knee extension = most anterior shear

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Knee stiffness** | **Most common** · risk: delayed surgery, delayed ROM · **MUA** if flexion < 120° at 6–12 wk · **lysis of adhesions** if < 120° after > 12 wk |
| **Quadriceps atrophy** | **2nd most common** · doesn't compromise strength return · PT |
| **Re-tear** | **\~8% transosseous / 1–2% anchors** · acute + good tissue → repair · chronic / poor tissue → reconstruction |
| Infection | **\~2%** |
| Delayed repair | Functioning extensor but **persistent quads atrophy + ↓ flexion** |

## X vs Y

| | **Patellar tendon rupture** | **Quadriceps tendon rupture** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | Less common | **More common (2:1)** |
| Age | **< 40** | **> 40** |
| Patellar height | **Alta (IS > 1.2)** | **Baja (IS < 0.8)** |
| Common site | **Inferior pole of patella** | — |

## How it's asked

**"Buckling, patella alta on X-ray + MRI?"** → **patellar tendon rupture**

**"Preoperative finding before tendon repair?"** → **Insall-Salvati ratio of 1.3**

**"Value suggesting rupture (Figures A–C)?"** → **Y/X 1.1 in Blackburne-Peel**

**"24-yr-old, alta after landing — management?"** → **primary surgical repair**

**"40-yr-old, pop, alta?"** → **open surgical repair**

**"CKD + diabetes, can't extend, 1 wk?"** → **primary repair of injured structure**

**"Best repair for gap formation?"** → **suture anchor repair**

**"Suture anchors vs bone tunnels?"** → **increased pull-out strength**

**"High load to failure, less gap?"** → **primary repair with suture anchors**

**"Rehab that limits stress on repair?"** → **active flexion, passive extension**

**"Immediate post-op active ROM exercise?"** → **heel slides**

# Patellofemoral Joint

*Knee & Sports · 40 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy & stabilisers

**Articulation** — **patella ↔ trochlear (intercondylar) groove** · **LFC diameter > MFC**

**Bony constraint** — **trochlear groove = primary restraint to lateral translation at > 30° flexion**

**MPFL (this note)** — **adductor tubercle → superomedial border of patella** · **primary restraint 0–20°** · instability note: between **medial epicondyle and adductor tubercle**, **0–30°** — read the options

| Passive restraint to lateral subluxation | Share of restraining force |
|---|---|
| **MPFL** (primary, at 20°) | **60%** |
| Medial patellomeniscal ligament | **13%** |
| Medial retinaculum | **10%** |

**Dynamic** — **vastus medialis** resists **lateral** translation · vastus lateralis resists **medial** translation · quadriceps

**Blood supply** — superior medial + lateral geniculate · inferior medial + lateral geniculate · anterior geniculate · descending geniculate

### Q-angle & patellofemoral contact pressures

**Q-angle** — **ASIS → centre of patella → tibial tuberosity** · **13° M / 18° F** (Orthobullets) · 14° M / 17° F (TO) · 14° M / 18° F (OB) — read the options

![Q-angle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3002-qangle-moved.jpg)

**Effect of Q-angle** — valgus axis → **laterally directed force** → **↑ lateral facet pressure between 40–90° flexion** · 10° ↑ Q → ↑ peak pressure, lateral facet

**Pressure vs flexion** — ↑ from extension to **\~90°** (maximum) · falls to **1/3 by 130°** · normally even medial / lateral

**Maximum contact** — **45°** (Orthobullets; OB: max bony contact \~45°) · **90°** (TO, instability topic) — read the options

**Location** — deeper flexion → **higher pressure in proximal patella** (patella slides **caudally**)\
✗ Wrong options: distal

**Osteotomy** — medialisation and AMZ both **partially correct** high-Q lateral pressures, similarly

**OB traps** — ✗ Wrong options: patella-trochlea articulation primary at 0–30° (MPFL is) · vastus medialis = primary restraint near extension · forces highest in extension

### Biomechanics

**Function** — transmits quads force to patellar tendon · **↑ extensor lever arm** · **patellectomy ↓ extension force 30%**

| Activity (TO) | PFJRF (× body weight) |
|---|---|
| Level walking | **0.5–1** |
| Vertical jumping | **2.4–4.6** |
| Stair ascent / descent | **3.5–6** (descent > ascent) · 2–3 descending (Orthobullets) |
| Running | **4.5–7** |
| Squat | **Up to 7** |
| **Deep squat** | **Up to 20** |

**PFJRF order (TO, this topic)** — **walking < jumping < stair ascent < stair descent < running < squat < deep squat**\
✗ Wrong options: jump after stair descent · ascent > descent · less force with more flexion

**PFJRF order (TO, instability topic)** — walking < ascent < descent < running < squat < deep squat < **jumping** — read the options

**Flexion** — **↑ flexion → ↑ PFJRF**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Shows |
|---|---|
| **AP** | Alignment, fracture, arthritis |
| **Lateral** | **Patella alta / baja**, condylar dysplasia, arthritis, **transverse** patella fracture |
| **Axial** | **Malalignment**, trochlear depth, arthritis, **vertical** patella fracture |
| CT | Alignment, fracture, trochlear geometry, **TT-TG** |
| **MRI** | **Best for articular cartilage** · **T2** best sequence |

**Measurements** — patellar height (Insall-Salvati) · **lateral PF angle opens laterally** (normal) · **congruence angle −6°** (normal)

## How it's asked

**"PFJRF true statement (TO)?"** → **walking < jumping < stair ascent < stair descent < running < deep squat**

**"PF biomechanics — true?"** → **higher Q-angle → ↑ lateral contact pressure 40–90°**

# PCL Injury

*Knee & Sports · 41 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **5–20%** of all knee ligament injuries · **rare in isolation** → usually multiligament (PLC, ACL), knee dislocation

**Sports (TO)** — football, soccer, rugby, skiing → highest incidence of PCL tears

### Anatomy

**Origin (femur)** — **anterolateral medial femoral condyle** · broad crescent-shaped footprint · arthroscopic view from **anterolateral portal** shows AL + PM bundle origins on MFC

**Insertion (tibia)** — **posterior tibial sulcus below (distal to) the articular surface**\
✗ Wrong options: superior to articular margin

**Size** — 38 mm long × 13 mm diameter · **30% larger than ACL** · strength 2500–3000 N · resists 95% of posterior tibial displacement

**Synovial status (TO)** — **cruciates = intra-articular, extrasynovial** (own blood supply, not fed by synovial fluid) · collaterals = extra-articular, extrasynovial · menisci = intra-articular, intrasynovial (fed by synovial fluid)

**Blood supply** — **middle geniculate artery** + fat pad

**Meniscofemoral ligaments** — PCL lies between them · **Humphrey anterior, Wrisberg posterior** · arise posterior horn lateral meniscus → insert into PCL substance

| Bundle | Tight in | Fact |
|---|---|---|
| **Anterolateral (AL)** | **Flexion** | Strongest · main posterior stabiliser at **90°** · **"PAL"** = PCL Antero-Lateral · reconstructed in single-bundle |
| **Posteromedial (PM)** | **Extension** | Reciprocal to AL |
| ✗ Wrong option: anteromedial / posterolateral | — | **ACL** bundles (distractor) |

### Biomechanics

**Function** — **primary restraint to posterior tibial translation** · prevents hyperflexion / sliding · **isolated injury → greatest instability at 90° flexion**

**Popliteus** — synergistic with PCL → controls ER, varus, posterior translation (see PLC)

## 2 Mechanism

**Most common overall** — **direct blow to proximal (anterior) tibia, knee flexed (dashboard)** (TO, OB)

**Most common in athletes** — **fall on flexed knee with foot plantarflexed** → posterior force on tibia\
✗ Wrong options: foot dorsiflexed · blow to back of tibia · ER-valgus or IR-varus twist

**Other (TO)** — non-contact **hyperflexion > hyperextension** (both less common) · hyperextension (stepping into hole) · rotational with varus / valgus · hyperextension often **ACL + PCL**

**Clue in stem** — scars over **anterior proximal tibia + dorsum of ankle / forefoot** → foot plantarflexed + blow to tibia → **PCL** (can extend knee → not patella fracture, patellar tendon or tubercle avulsion; Lachman negative → not ACL)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — high vs low energy · **posterior knee pain** · instability subtle / asymptomatic if isolated · ask about dislocation / nerve injury

**Diagnosis mainly clinical (TO)** — **posterior drawer**; MRI best test for cartilage, ACL, meniscus **but not PCL** (TO) · MRI = confirmatory study (Orthobullets) — read the options

### Clinical tests (posterior drawer, sag, quadriceps active, dial)

| Test | Technique / positive | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **Posterior drawer (90°)** | Posterior force on proximal tibia · **most accurate** for PCL | Isolated: **> 10–12 mm neutral, 6–8 mm IR** · combined: **> 15 mm neutral, > 10 mm IR** |
| **Posterior sag sign** | Hips + knees 90°, ankles supported → tibia sags | Normal **medial plateau 10 mm anterior to MFC**; absent / posterior step-off = + |
| **Quadriceps active test** | Quad contraction at 90° | **Anterior reduction of tibia** = + |
| **Reverse Lachman** | Posterior translation near extension | PCL |
| **False Lachman** | Posterior subluxation mimics ↑ anterior travel | PCL (✗ Wrong options: ACL) |
| Varus / valgus at **0°** | Laxity | **Collateral + PCL** (cruciate) |
| Varus / valgus at **30° only** | Laxity | **Isolated MCL / LCL** |
| KT-1000 / KT-2000 | Arthrometer | Less accurate than for ACL |

### Dial test (TO)

| Dial result (ER asymmetry vs other side) | Lesion |
|---|---|
| **> 10° at 30° only** | **Isolated PLC** |
| **> 10° at 30° AND 90°** | **PLC + PCL** |
| **ER 15° more at 30°, further ↑ at 90° (TO)** | **PCL + PLC** |
| 2° asymmetry / symmetric at 30° + 90° | No PLC → isolated PCL |

**Threshold** — **> 10°** (Orthobullets, OB) · **10–15°** (OB) · **15°** (TO supine description) — read the options

**Prone dial (TO)** — **thigh–foot angle in prone position** (angle between sagittal plane of sole and body)\
✗ Wrong options: foot–leg angle · tibial tuberosity angle supine · foot divergence

**Supine dial (TO)** — leg over edge of bed, stabilise distal femur, hold heel → max ER at 30° → note **tibial tuberosity position** → repeat at 90°

**Combined exam (OB)** — 2B reverse Lachman + sag + **varus at 0° / 30°** + negative dial + negative reverse pivot → **PCL + LCL**\
✗ Wrong options: complete PLC (needs + dial / reverse pivot)

![Posterior sag sign](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3009-posterior-sag-sign-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| AP + supine lateral X-ray | Avulsion fractures (acute) · **posterior tibiofemoral subluxation** · chronic → medial + PF arthrosis |
| **Lateral stress view (knee 70°)** | Asymmetric posterior displacement · **becoming gold standard** for diagnosing + quantifying · side-to-side **> 12 mm → PCL + PLC** |
| Kneeling stress X-ray | Quantifies posterior translation |
| Sagittal CT | Tibial PCL bony avulsion fragment |
| **MRI** | **Confirmatory** · essential to plan concomitant injuries in avulsions (TO) |

![Lateral stress radiographs in PCL injury](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3009-pcl-injury-lateral-stress-views.jpg)

### PCL grading

| Grade (posterior tibial subluxation at 90°) | Injury | Translation |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Partial** tear | **1–5 mm** |
| **II** | **Complete isolated** tear | **6–10 mm** · anterior tibia **flush with femoral condyles** |
| **III** | Row blank in source table | — |

**Veltri (OB)** — isolated PCL **< 10 mm** posterior laxity → nonop · **> 10 mm or combined** → reconstruct

## 5 Management

```
PCL injury
├─ Isolated grade I / II → protected WB + quadriceps rehab (RTS 2–4 wk)
├─ Isolated grade III → immobilise in extension 4 wk → quadriceps strengthening
│      (surgery if bony avulsion or young athlete)
├─ Bony tibial avulsion (displaced > 5 mm) → ORIF (Burks–Schaffer posteromedial)
├─ Combined (ACL / PLC / grade III MCL or LCL) → PCL reconstruction + all lesions
└─ Chronic, functionally unstable / varus → reconstruction ± medial opening-wedge HTO
```

### Nonoperative management – quadriceps rehabilitation

**Principle** — **quadriceps strengthening (knee extensors)** pulls tibia anteriorly → counteracts PCL deficit · ACL-deficient knee → hamstring rehab\
✗ Wrong options: hamstrings · hip · gastroc-soleus

**Grade II protocol (OB)** — **relative protection 10–14 days → ROM + gentle closed-chain quadriceps** strengthening · global strengthening at **4–6 wk** · RTS at full ROM + strength

**Quadriceps-first, possibly definitive (OB)** — **isolated PCL rupture** → **closed-chain** quad strengthening (foot fixed) · no clear prognostic factors\
✗ Wrong options: ACL rupture (hamstring rehab) · patellar tendon rupture · bucket-handle meniscal tear · meniscal root tear (surgery)

**Prone ROM** — flexion done **prone** → limits posterior sag

**Avoid** — **resisted hamstring** until healed (**4–6 wk**) · **plyometrics** early · prolonged immobilisation\
✗ Wrong options: long leg cast 4 wk → stiffness · 12 wk in extension

**Isolated grade II exam (OB)** — 2+ posterior drawer, 1+ stable Lachman, negative dial, no valgus laxity → **PT with quadriceps focus** · persistent anterior / medial pain or "loose" → reconstruct later\
✗ Wrong options: delayed or acute reconstruction

**Grade III isolated** — relative immobilisation **in extension 4 wk** + limited daily ROM → quad strengthening

### Tibial avulsion – ORIF

**Tibial-sided avulsion (TO)** — **most common form of isolated bony PCL disruption** · mechanism: dashboard > sports hyperextension

**Displaced > 5 mm (TO)** — **surgical fixation (arthroscopic or open)** · MRI to plan concomitant injuries \
✗ Wrong options: **long leg cast in full extension 4 wk**

**Young active, displaced (OB)** — **ORIF of fragment** · nonop historically → loss of motion · good results with repair of avulsions\
✗ Wrong options: reconstruction · hinged brace + therapy · HTO

**Exam** — isolated PCL: posterior drawer **11 mm neutral / 7 mm IR**, stable varus / valgus, negative dial

![Tibial PCL bony avulsion, lateral radiograph](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17119378-1.png)

**Burks–Schaffer approach (TO)** — **posteromedial approach for PCL avulsion** · **prone** · horizontal limb over popliteal crease + vertical limb over medial gastrocnemius\
✗ Wrong options: ACL avulsion · TA repair

**Interval (TO)** — **medial gastrocnemius – semimembranosus** → blunt finger dissection to posterior capsule · **ligate middle geniculate artery** if needed · **preserve tibial nerve motor branch to medial head gastrocnemius** · retract medial gastrocnemius **laterally** (protects NV bundle) · vertical capsulotomy

### Operative criteria

| PCL repair / reconstruction indicated | Nonop |
|---|---|
| **Combined: PCL + ACL or PLC** | Isolated grade I, II |
| PCL + **grade III MCL or LCL** | Isolated grade III (unless avulsion / young athlete) |
| **Isolated grade II / III with bony avulsion** | |
| **Isolated chronic, functionally unstable** | |
| **Chronic deficiency + varus → HTO** | |

### Reconstruction technique – graft tensioning, tibial inlay, killer turn

**Graft** — usually **allograft** (Achilles, BPTB, hamstring, tibialis anterior) · no technique clearly superior · results < ACL reconstruction, residual posterior laxity common · **midsubstance primary repair fails** · success needs concomitant ligaments addressed

**Single-bundle** — reconstruct **anterolateral bundle** · **tension at 90° flexion** (ACL tensioned nearer extension; closest to isometric)\
✗ Wrong options: 0° · 30°

**Double-bundle** — **AL at 90°, PM in extension** · biomechanical advantage; clinical advantage unproven · useful with PCL + PLC (rotational control; PLC grafts loosen)

**Transtibial (arthroscopic)** — drill anterior → posterior · **accessory posteromedial portal** 1 cm above joint line, posterior to MCL (**saphenous nerve** branches at risk) · fix graft at **90° with anterior drawer** · risk to popliteal vessels

**Posteromedial corner view** — **70° arthroscope** through notch (modified Gillquist) or posteromedial portal

**Tibial inlay (open)** — posteromedial incision between **medial gastrocnemius + semimembranosus** · graft bone block fixed directly to posterior tibia · screw **within 20 mm of popliteal artery** · ORIF of avulsions

**Killer turn (TO)** — sharp (\~**90°**) bend of graft around **proximal posterior tibia** at tibial tunnel exit → abrasion, thinning, elongation → **main cause of residual laxity + graft failure** · Markolf: **thinning 40.6%, elongation 65.3% (9.8 / 15 mm)**

![Killer turn at the posterior tibial tunnel exit](../../MCQ/attachments/TO21733233-1.jpg)

| Reduce killer turn (TO) | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Tibial inlay** (Jakob, Berg) | No tibial tunnel · posterior arthrotomy · anatomic · bone-to-bone healing |
| **Anterolateral tibial tunnel** | ↓ angulation, no ↑ translation vs anteromedial (theoretical: ↓ compressive force → ↑ translation) |
| **Tunnel 15–20 mm deep from posterior tibial margin** (jig) | ↑ tunnel angle → smaller turn |
| **Tunnel exit lower in footprint** | One acute turn → two smoother turns |
| **Aperture fixation** (interference screw) | no suspensory (endobutton) "bungee cord" micromotion · stiffer construct |
| Smooth aperture | Punch / rasp |
| **All-inside** | Half tunnels (sockets) · bone stock · ↓ tunnel widening / convergence · ↓ "windshield effect" |
| ✗ Wrong option: **tunnel exit superiorly (upper half of fossa)** | Does NOT avoid it |
| ✗ Wrong option: **transtibial in place of inlay** | Does NOT help (transtibial causes it) |

**Inlay vs transtibial (OB)** — only advantage = **eliminates the 90° killer turn**\
✗ Wrong options: less invasive · cosmesis · shorter time · faster rehab · better clinical results · normal kinematics

### Rehabilitation after PCL reconstruction (OKC vs CKC)

**Early** — immobilise in extension, **protect against gravity** · early motion **prone** · focus on quadriceps

**Avoid** — **resisted hamstring curls (prone / OKC)** → posterior pull on tibia stresses graft

**4 wk post-op, highest posterior shear (TO)** — **OKC resisted hamstring curls 0–90°** (esp. deep flexion **> 60°**)\
✗ Wrong options: CKC leg press 0–60° · OKC quad extension 90→0° · CKC wall squat 0–45°

**Allowed (OB, TO)** — quadriceps activation · closed-chain terminal extension · **non-WB resisted extension 60°→0°** · prone passive flexion + active terminal extension · ankle pumps · **CKC up to 60°**

**PCL + PLC post-op (OB)** — **no active flexion, active extension 90→0°** · hinged brace + crutches, partial WB first 4 wk

| Kinetic chain (TO, Steindler 1955) | OKC | CKC |
|---|---|---|
| Distal segment | **Free** (leg extension, hamstring curl, SLR) | **Fixed** (squat, leg press, lunge, step-up) |
| Joints | Single (isolation) | Multi-joint, functional |
| Knee force | **Shear** | **Compressive** + quad–hamstring **co-contraction** |
| OKC extension | **Anterior tibial shear**, peaks **0–30°** → stresses ACL | — |
| **ACL rehab** | Restrict 0–45° first **12 wk** (or 90–45° only); full range at **12–16 wk** | **Early (2–4 wk)** — protects ACL |
| **PCL rehab** | **Extension safe** (anterior shear unloads PCL) | **Modify**: co-contraction → posterior shear, esp. **> 60°** |
| Patellofemoral | **90–45°** → lower PFJ force than deep CKC | **0–45°** → lower PFJ force than OKC |
| TKA | SLR, terminal extension (supplement) | Early sit-to-stand, partial squat |
| Fracture | Strength when WB restricted | Axial load → callus (**Wolff's law**) |

### Combined PCL / PLC / LCL injuries & slope-increasing osteotomy

**Dial + at 30° and 90°, sag sign** — **PCL + PLC**\
✗ Wrong options: ACL only · PCL only · PLC + ACL (dial + at 30° only + Lachman)

**Chronic PCL + PLC, varus thrust, 10° varus** — **HTO to INCREASE slope + correct varus, then PCL + PLC reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: bracing · PCL alone · ACL + PCL (high failure if coronal alignment untreated)

**Medial opening-wedge HTO** — **↑ tibial slope + tibia shifts anterior** · addresses varus + PCL deficiency\
✗ Wrong options: decreased · posterior

![Tibial slope: closing wedge (ACL deficient) vs opening wedge (PCL deficient)](../../MCQ/attachments/q2848-illa-3374001-ksrr-24-61-g006.jpg)

### Posterior compartment access (Gillquist portal / manoeuvre)

**Gillquist portal (TO)** — **central transpatellar portal** · midline **1 cm below inferior pole of patella** · used in ACL reconstruction after graft harvest\
✗ Wrong options: anterolateral · anteromedial · supraclavicular

**Gillquist manoeuvre (TO, 1979)** — **visualise posterior compartment** · 5-mm 30° scope through patellar tendon → **between MFC and PCL** → swap to **70°** · sees posterior horn, meniscocapsular junction, **ramp lesion**, PCL, loose bodies\
✗ Wrong options: PF tracking (superolateral portal)

**Modified Gillquist (TO)** — via **anterolateral or anteromedial** portal · McGinnis: inferior notchplasty of lateral MFC to pass scope (tight in intact cruciates / OA)

| Knee portal (TO) | Site | Use |
|---|---|---|
| **Anterolateral** | 1 cm above lateral joint line, 1 cm lateral to patellar tendon | **Main viewing, first portal** · misses anterior horn LM, periphery of posterior horn MM, PCL |
| **Anteromedial** | 1 cm above medial joint line, 1 cm medial to tendon | **Main working** · views lateral compartment |
| **Posteromedial** | — | Posterior horn meniscal repair, **PCL**, posterior loose bodies |
| **Superolateral** | Lateral to quadriceps tendon, 2.5 cm above superolateral patella | **Patellofemoral articulation** |
| **Central transpatellar (Gillquist)** | Midline, 1 cm below inferior pole | ACL reconstruction · posterior compartment |

| Shoulder portal (TO) | Site | Use / at risk |
|---|---|---|
| **Posterior (soft spot)** | 1–2 cm medial, 2 cm inferior to posterolateral acromion · aim coracoid | First, viewing · **axillary n.** inferior, **suprascapular a. + n.** medial |
| **Anteroinferior (Wolf)** | Lateral + inferior to coracoid | Anchors for capsulolabral repair · axillary + musculocutaneous n., axillary a., cephalic v. |
| Anterosuperior | Between coracoid + acromion, anterior to LHB | Outflow / retrieval in Bankart |
| **Wilmington** | 1 cm lateral + 1 cm inferior to posterolateral acromion, through cuff | **Posterior SLAP** |
| **5 o'clock** | — | Inferior glenoid anchors at right angle (Bankart) |
| **Neviaser (superior / supraclavicular)** | Supraspinatus fossa: clavicle, scapular spine, medial acromion | SLAP anchors |
| Lateral subacromial | 2–3 cm below lateral acromion, in line with posterior AC joint | Subacromial |
| Posterolateral subacromial | \~2 cm below lateral acromion, in line with posterior edge | Scope for **cuff repair** |
| Anterolateral subacromial | 2–3 cm below lateral acromion, in line with anterior edge | Subacromial |

## 6 Complications

**Loss of motion (TO)** — **most common complication of PCL reconstruction** · **flexion loss > extension loss** (10–20° flexion loss) · graft malposition / inadequate rehab\
✗ Wrong options: popliteal artery · residual instability · cartilage

**Femoral tunnel (TO)** — **more critical than tibial** · anterior + distal to isometric point → ↑ graft tension → **flexion loss** · extension loss / flexion contracture → prolonged immobilisation in flexion

**Popliteal artery injury** — lies **just posterior to tibial PCL insertion, separated only by posterior capsule** · risk ↑ with **transtibial drilling in knee extension** → drill in **flexion** · accessory posteromedial incision (finger retraction), oscillating drill, tapered bits\
✗ Wrong options: saphenous nerve (that is posteromedial portal) · medial inferior geniculate artery

**Residual posterior laxity** — common; failure to reconstruct both bundles → rotational instability

### PCL deficiency – medial & patellofemoral arthritis

**Chronic PCL deficiency** — ↑ contact pressure **patellofemoral + medial compartment** (varus) · late patellar + MFC chondrosis (controversial) · reconstruction may delay OA vs nonop\
✗ Wrong options: isolated lateral · lateral without PF · isolated PF · medial only · normal (short term only)

**Medial + PF degeneration, old dashboard injury → MRI** — **PCL rupture**\
✗ Wrong options: PHMM tear (medial only) · ACL · trochlear dysplasia (PF only) · MFC OCD

**Young labourer, medial OA after old PCL injury** — **medial opening-wedge HTO** (↑ slope helps PCL deficiency)\
✗ Wrong options: UKA (needs cruciates) · TKA · isolated PCL reconstruction in arthritic malaligned knee · lateral closing wedge

## X vs Y

| | **ACL deficient** | **PCL deficient** |
|---|---|---|
| Rehab focus | Hamstrings · CKC | **Quadriceps** · OKC extension safe |
| HTO slope | **Decrease** (closing wedge) | **Increase** (opening wedge) |
| Graft tension angle | Nearer extension | **90° flexion** |
| Arthritis | — | **Medial + patellofemoral** |

| | **Tibial inlay** | **Transtibial** |
|---|---|---|
| Approach | Open posteromedial (med. gastroc–semimembranosus) | Arthroscopic + accessory posteromedial portal |
| Killer turn | **Eliminated** | **Present** |
| Proven clinical superiority | None | None |

## How it's asked

**"MRI best investigation for all EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **PCL tear (clinical diagnosis — posterior drawer)**

**"PCL is (TO)?"** → **intra-articular, extrasynovial**

**"Dial test angle measured (TO)?"** → **thigh–foot angle in prone position**

**"Landed on flexed knee, foot plantarflexed (TO)?"** → **PCL**

**"Most common mechanism of PCL injury (TO)?"** → **blow on front of upper tibia, knee flexed, foot plantarflexed**

**"Isolated PCL tear in football player — mechanism?"** → **fall on flexed knee, foot plantarflexed**

**"Motocross scars over anterior tibia + dorsum of foot, negative Lachman?"** → **PCL tear**

**"True of injured structure on MRI after dashboard injury?"** → **AL bundle tight in flexion, PM tight in extension**

**"Untreated isolated PCL — long-term X-ray?"** → **medial + patellofemoral degeneration**

**"11 mm drawer neutral, 7 mm IR, ORIF planned — image?"** → **tibial PCL bony avulsion**

**"Muscle to strengthen for this footprint injury?"** → **quadriceps**

**"Grade II PCL nonop rehab?"** → **quadriceps strengthening + prone ROM as tolerated**

**"Initial rehab isolated grade 2 PCL?"** → **protection 10–14 days, then ROM + closed-chain quadriceps**

**"2+ posterior drawer, negative dial — treatment?"** → **PT with quadriceps focus**

**"Dial 2° asymmetry at 30° + 90° after dashboard injury?"** → **quadriceps strengthening**

**"Quadriceps strengthening as definitive treatment for which MRI?"** → **isolated PCL tear**

**"Grade 1 PCL at 18, medial OA at 35, labourer?"** → **medial opening-wedge HTO**

**"Compartments degenerate after PCL deficiency?"** → **medial + patellofemoral**

**"Medial + PF OA, old car accident — MRI shows?"** → **PCL rupture**

**"Killer turn avoided by all EXCEPT (TO)?"** → **tibial tunnel exiting superiorly (upper half of fossa)**

**"Most common complication of PCL reconstruction (TO)?"** → **loss of motion**

**"NOT effective for killer-turn failure (TO)?"** → **transtibial tunnel in place of inlay**

**"4 wk post-op, highest posterior shear (TO)?"** → **OKC resisted hamstring curls 0–90°**

**"Single-bundle PCL graft tension angle?"** → **90 degrees flexion**

**"Avoid in early rehab after PCL reconstruction?"** → **prone hamstring curls**

**"Advantage of tibial inlay?"** → **eliminates the 90° killer turn**

**"Hyperextension injury, reconstruction planned — inlay advantage?"** → **avoidance of the "killer" 90-degree curve**

**"Transtibial drilling in full extension → risk?"** → **popliteal artery injury**

**"ER 15° more at 30°, more at 90° (TO)?"** → **PCL + PLC**

**"Chronic PCL + PLC, 10° varus, varus thrust?"** → **HTO increasing slope + correct varus + PCL & PLC reconstruction**

**"Sag sign + dial 20° at 30° and 90°?"** → **PCL + PLC**

**"Opening-wedge HTO for chronic PCL + PLC — slope / tibia?"** → **increased / anterior**

**"Reverse Lachman, sag, varus at 0° / 30°, dial negative?"** → **PCL + LCL**

**"Tibial PCL avulsion — NOT true (TO)?"** → **long leg cast in full extension for 4 weeks**

**"Burks–Schaffer approach (TO)?"** → **PCL avulsion fixation (posteromedial)**

**"Displaced PCL tibial avulsion, young infantryman?"** → **ORIF of avulsed fragment**

**"Gillquist portal (TO)?"** → **central portal through patellar tendon**

**"Gillquist manoeuvre purpose (TO)?"** → **visualise posterior compartment**

# Piriformis Muscles Syndrome

*Knee & Sports · 42 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Sciatic nerve anatomy relative to piriformis

**Normal exit** — **inferior to (deep / anterior to) piriformis**, superior to superior gemellus · via greater sciatic foramen

**Commonest pattern** — **single nerve anterior to piriformis** · **79%** (OB, Pokorny) · **96%** (TO) — read the options

**Atypical anatomy** — **~21%** (Pokorny, 91 cadavers)

**Commonest variant** — **14%**: nerve splits above piriformis → **one branch anterior + one through** the muscle (OB) · usually **peroneal part pierces**, tibial part runs below / above (TO) · whole nerve may pass through the belly (TO)

**Short external rotators (superior → inferior)** — piriformis, superior gemellus, obturator internus, inferior gemellus, obturator externus, quadratus femoris

![Sciatic nerve exiting deep to piriformis](../../MCQ/attachments/q2850-illa-piriformis.jpg)

### Pathophysiology

**Definition** — sciatica-like pain from **extrapelvic sciatic nerve compression** at the hip · aka **back pocket / wallet sciatica** (TO)

**Site** — **anterior to piriformis** or posterior to obturator internus / gemelli at **ischial tuberosity level** (Orthobullets) · in piriformis at **greater sciatic notch** (TO)

**Primary (TO)** — anatomic: **split piriformis**, **split sciatic nerve**, anomalous nerve path · Orthobullets adds **bipartite piriformis**, tumours, **inferior gluteal artery aneurysm**

**Secondary (TO)** — trauma, **LLD**, cerebral palsy, narrowed sciatic foramen · macrotrauma to buttock (spasm) · microtrauma / overuse (long-distance walking, running, **dancing**) · **wallet in back pocket**

**FAI link** — ↓ IR → short external rotator contracture → sciatic compression

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **posterior gluteal pain radiating down back of leg**, burning / aching

**Pattern (TO)** — worse **rising after sitting / squatting > 15–20 min** · pain on IR (cross-legged sitting) · **improves with ambulation, worse with rest**

**Referred (TO)** — spasm stresses **sacrotuberous ligament** → **pudendal** compression → groin / pelvic pain · **fibular (peroneal) branch** compression → paraesthesia sacrum → buttock → posterior thigh

| Test (TO) | Positive when |
|---|---|
| **FAIR** (Orthobullets) | **Flexion, adduction, IR** stretches piriformis → reproduces symptoms |
| **Pace sign** | FAIR recreates **sciatic symptoms** |
| **Piriformis sign** | Supine, foot lies **externally rotated** · active return to midline → pain |
| **Freiberg sign** | Pain on **passive hip IR** |
| **Beatty test** | Side-lying on unaffected side, lifts top knee ~4 inches → sciatic pain |
| Lasègue | Hip + knee 90°, extend knee → posterior thigh pain |

![FAIR test](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3095-fair-test.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Imaging** — X-ray unremarkable · hip MRI usually unremarkable · **lumbar MRI** to exclude spinal cause · **EDS** document sciatic impairment

### Differential – ischiofemoral impingement

**Dancer, buttock pain → at rest, pain on hip extension + ER, quadratus femoris oedema (TO)** — **ischiofemoral impingement**\
✗ Wrong options: FAI · piriformis syndrome · quadriceps tendinopathy

**IFI definition (TO)** — chronic groin / buttock / hip pain + **narrowed space between lesser trochanter and ischial tuberosity**

**Ischiofemoral distance on axial MRI (TO)** — **normal > 2 cm** · **< 1.3 cm critical**

**MRI (TO)** — **quadratus femoris ↑ T2 signal**, fibres intact · QF oedema can compress / displace **sciatic nerve** → radicular pain

**Causes (TO)** — **iatrogenic after hip surgery**, hip trauma, **ischial exostosis**, primary in young athletes

**IFI test (TO)** — pain on **extension + adduction + ER** (one TO explanation) · **extension + abduction + ER** (other TO explanation) · tenderness over ischiofemoral space

**IFI treatment (TO)** — NSAIDs, guided steroid injection, PT → failure: **endoscopic resection of lesser trochanter**

| Condition (TO) | Key features | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Ischiofemoral impingement** | Buttock pain, extension + ER, **QF oedema**, distance < 1.3 cm | NSAIDs, injection → LT resection |
| **Piriformis syndrome** | Buttock → posterior leg, worse with sitting, **FAIR / Pace** | Stretch, NSAIDs → release + neurolysis |
| **Snapping hip — internal** | Anterior click · **iliopsoas over iliopectineal eminence** | Stretch → iliopsoas release / lengthening |
| **Snapping hip — external** | **More common** · lateral · **ITB over greater trochanter** | Stretch → ITB release / lengthening |
| **Snapping hip — intra-articular** | **Labral tear, loose body** · dynamic USG / MRI | **Arthroscopic loose body removal + labral repair** |
| **Iliopsoas tendinopathy** | Musculotendinous junction · pain on active flexion, stairs, getting out of car | Rest, stretch, NSAIDs, PT → release at LT / lengthening |
| **Iliopsoas tendonitis after THR** | Groin pain on active flexion · painful SLR, passive SLR pain-free | CT-guided steroid + stretch → **iliopsoas tenotomy** |

**Snapping hip (TO)** — coxa saltans / **dancer's hip** · ballet dancers, extreme flexion + abduction · **extra-articular more common** than intra-articular · resistant extra-articular → local steroid into sheath

**Iliopsoas bursa (TO)** — **largest bursa in the body** · 3 causes of bursitis: **RA, acute trauma, overuse**

**Snapping hip sign / extension test (TO)** — palpate inguinal crease, flex → abduct + ER → back to neutral → palpable click

**Iliopsoas after THR — diagnosis (TO)** — **cross-table lateral** shows **cup prominent beyond anterior rim** (snapping over anterior lip) · relief after **USG / CT-guided local anaesthetic** into iliopsoas sheath confirms

## 5 Management

```
Piriformis syndrome
├─ First line → rest, NSAIDs, muscle relaxants, ice, PT stretching, steroid injection
└─ Refractory → piriformis release + external sciatic neurolysis
```

**Nonoperative** — **stretch piriformis + short external rotators** · strengthen abductors + adductors (TO) · **corticosteroid injection near piriformis** · early conservative Rx most effective (TO)

**Surgery** — only after failed conservative care · release piriformis tension + **explore sciatic notch** for fibrous bands / constrictions

## How it's asked

**"Most common sciatic nerve pattern at exit?"** → **single nerve anterior to piriformis**

**"Dancer, extension + ER pain, quadratus femoris oedema (TO)?"** → **ischiofemoral impingement**

**"MRI finding separating IFI from piriformis syndrome (TO)?"** → **quadratus femoris oedema, ischiofemoral distance < 1.3 cm**\
✗ Wrong options: piriformis syndrome (MRI usually normal)
