# Quadriceps Tendon Rupture

*Knee & Sports · 51 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & risk factors

**Frequency** — **more common than patellar tendon rupture** (2:1 in the patellar tendon rupture chapter)

**Age** — usually **> 40 yr** (Orthobullets, OB) · **patellar tendon rupture < 40 yr** (OB)

**Sex + side** — **M > F (up to 8:1)** · **nondominant limb > 2× as often**

**Site** — usually at the **insertion of the tendon on the patella**

**Systemic risk factors** — **renal failure**, **diabetes**, **rheumatoid arthritis**, hyperparathyroidism, connective tissue disorders

**Local / drug risk factors** — **steroid use** · **intra-articular injections (in 20–33%)** · previous knee surgery (OB)

**Pathobiology (OB)** — degeneration from **reduced blood supply** + **repetitive microtrauma** · **little evidence that inflammation is active** · high mechanical load + poor blood supply impede repair

### Anatomy

**Quadriceps tendon** — **2 to 4 distinct layers** → matters for partial vs complete tear and for repair

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **eccentric loading** of extensor mechanism · **foot planted, knee slightly bent**

**Younger patients** — usually **direct trauma**

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis – patella baja & palpable defect

**History** — preceding pain from **underlying tendinopathy** · "pop" · large haematoma / effusion

**Palpable defect** — usually **within 2 cm of superior pole of patella**

**Extensor function** — **can't extend against resistance** · **no straight leg raise in complete rupture** · e.g. **extension lag 30°**

**29-yr-old basketball, 30° lag, suprapatellar defect** — **surgical repair of ruptured quadriceps tendon**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · diagnostic arthroscopy · immobiliser 6 wk then sport brace · limited WB 3 wk then PT

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| **X-ray — AP + lateral knee** | **Patella baja** |
| **MRI** | Only if **diagnosis uncertain** · **partial vs complete** tear |

![Patella baja after quadriceps tendon rupture (lateral radiograph)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3023-quad-rupture.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria – Insall-Salvati index

**ISI** — **patellar tendon length ÷ patellar height** (superior → inferior pole) · tendon and patella ≈ equal, no variation > 20% (Insall + Salvati)

| ISI | Meaning | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| **< 0.8** | **Patella baja** | **Quadriceps tendon rupture** |
| **0.8–1.2** | Normal | — |
| **> 1.2** | **Patella alta** | **Inferior pole patellar sleeve avulsion**, patellar tendon rupture |

**Worked example (OB)** — patella **4 cm**, tendon **3 cm** → **ISI 0.75 = baja** → **quadriceps tendon rupture**

**Distractors (OB)** — ✗ Wrong options: OCD (no ISI change) · sleeve avulsion (alta, ISI > 1.2) · MPFL disruption (instability, abnormal **patellar tilt**) · nail-patella syndrome (**small or absent patellae**)

**MRI ISI (Shabshin)** — normal **0.79–1.52** · **females higher** ratios than males

**Recurrent subluxation (Aglietti)** — normal knees: ISI \~1, congruence angle −8, **Q angle 15°** · subluxating: **ISI 1.23**, congruence +16, **Q angle 20°**

![Insall-Salvati index: tendon length ÷ patellar length](../../MCQ/attachments/q5821-illa-insall-salvati.jpg)

### Classification

| Type | Extensor mechanism | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Partial** | **Intact** | Brace immobilisation |
| **Complete** | **Disrupted** (no SLR, baja) | **Primary repair to patella** |

## 5 Management

```
Quadriceps tendon rupture
├─ Partial, extensor mechanism intact → knee immobilisation in brace
├─ Can't tolerate surgery → knee immobilisation in brace
├─ Complete, acute → primary repair to patella (anchors or transosseous)
└─ Chronic > 2 wk, retracted → repair + V-Y lengthening (Codivilla) ± graft
```

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Complete rupture with loss of extensor mechanism** | **Primary repair with reattachment to patella** |
| Chronic, retracted (**> 2 wk → up to 5 cm**) | Repair + **tendon lengthening (Codivilla V-Y)** |
| Tendon can't reach patella | **Auto- or allograft** tissue |

### Repair technique – transosseous vs suture anchors

**Approach** — **midline incision**

**Sutures** — **nonabsorbable**, **running locking** in tendon, free ends through **transpatellar drill holes** or tied to **suture anchors** · retinaculum → **heavy absorbable** sutures

**End-point** — knee should **flex to 90° after repair**

**Strength (OB)** — ↑ strands across repair + ↑ suture calibre → ↑ strength but **wound complications**

**Biomechanics** — **suture anchors → less gap formation + higher ultimate load to failure** (Orthobullets, OB)\
✗ Wrong options: transosseous less gap · transosseous higher load

**Petri (OB)** — 30 cadaver knees · **5.5-mm double-loaded anchors** vs transpatellar tunnels with No. 2 suture + **Krackow** → anchors less gap on cyclic loading, higher ultimate failure

**Kindaya (OB)** — **knotless suture-tape anchors** → less displacement, ↑ stiffness, ↑ load to failure vs transosseous **and** vs traditional anchors

**Onggo (OB)** — 212 knees (114 quadriceps) → anchors smaller gap; higher load to failure in **2 of 5** studies

**Conflict** — anchors vs simple patellar holes **no apparent biomechanical advantage** (OB7683) vs anchors biomechanically superior (OB210177, OB219988, Orthobullets) — read the options

**Clinical outcomes** — **equivalent functional + clinical outcomes** ✓ (OB, Orthobullets) · possibly **better PROMs with anchors** (Orthobullets) vs **no PROM difference** (OB) — read the options

**Mehta (OB)** — transosseous: **higher ROM**, **lower complication rate** (not lower re-rupture) · **no Lysholm difference**

**Transosseous vs anchors — true (OB)** — **equivalent functional outcomes**\
✗ Wrong options: less gap · higher load to failure · higher complication rate · worse PROMs

![Transosseous patellar tunnel repair (A)](../../MCQ/attachments/q210177-a-quad-tendon-transosseous.jpg)

![Suture anchor repair (B): less gap, higher load to failure](../../MCQ/attachments/q210177-b-quad-tendon-suture-anchor.jpg)

### Postoperative care

**Post-op** — immobilisation in **brace, cast or splint** → progressive flexibility + strengthening

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Strength deficit** | **33–50%** of patients |
| **Stiffness** | Common |
| **Functional impairment** | **50% can't return to prior activity / sport** |
| **Re-rupture** | ↑ with **pre-existing inflammatory arthropathy** |

## X vs Y

| | **Quadriceps tendon rupture** | **Patellar tendon rupture** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **More common** | Less common |
| Age | **> 40 yr** | **< 40 yr** |
| Patellar height | **Baja (ISI < 0.8)** | **Alta (ISI > 1.2)** |
| Defect | **Within 2 cm of superior pole** | Below inferior pole |

| | **Suture anchors** | **Transosseous tunnels** |
|---|---|---|
| Gap formation | **Less** | More |
| Ultimate load to failure | **Higher** | Lower |
| Complications (Mehta) | Higher | **Lower** |
| ROM (Mehta) | Lower | **Higher** |
| Functional outcome | **Equivalent** | **Equivalent** |

## How it's asked

**"Patella 4 cm, tendon 3 cm — cause?"** → **quadriceps tendon rupture** (ISI 0.75)

**"Inferior pole sleeve avulsion — ISI?"** → **> 1.2 (alta)**

**"30° extension lag + suprapatellar defect — management?"** → **surgical repair of ruptured quadriceps tendon**

**"Anchors (B) vs transosseous (A)?"** → **B: higher ultimate load to failure + less gap formation**

**"Transosseous vs anchor repair — tell the patient?"** → **equivalent functional outcomes**

**"Which repair has lower complication rate (Mehta)?"** → **transosseous**

**"Chronic retracted rupture — added procedure?"** → **Codivilla V-Y lengthening**

**"Complication in 33–50%?"** → **strength deficit**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Quadriceps Tendonitis

*Knee & Sports · 52 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Who** — **adult athletes** · **M:F 8:1** · often **recurrent**

**Risk factor** — **jumping sports**: basketball, volleyball, athletics (long jump, high jump)

**Age contrast** — quadriceps tendinopathy = **older adults** · patellar tendinopathy = adolescents / young adults (patellar tendinitis chapter)

### Pathophysiology

**Mechanism** — **repetitive eccentric contractions** of extensor mechanism

**Pathoanatomy** — **microtears at bone-tendon interface** (superior pole of patella)

**Quadriceps tendinosis** — **chronic degeneration, no inflammation**

**Jumper's knee** — **patellar tendonitis** · usually **patellar tendon insertion at patella**, less often at tibial tubercle

### Anatomy

| Quadriceps tendon layer (trilaminar) | Muscle |
|---|---|
| **Anterior (superficial)** | **Rectus femoris** |
| **Middle** | **Vastus medialis + vastus lateralis** |
| **Deep** | **Vastus intermedius** |

**Blood supply** — **medial, lateral + peripatellar arcades**

**Nerve** — **muscular branches of femoral nerve (L2, L3, L4)**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — overuse in a **jumping athlete** · **recent ↑ demand** / activity

**Symptoms** — pain at **superior border of patella** · worse with activity · swelling

**Exam** — **tender on deep palpation at quadriceps insertion** · **pain on resisted open-chain extension** · **can actively extend against gravity** ✓

**Palpable gap** — suggests **quadriceps tendon tear** (not tendonitis)

**Also check** — knee alignment, swelling, **patellar subluxation**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| X-ray — AP + lateral (± **sunrise / Merchant** for instability) | **Usually normal** · **tendon calcinosis** in chronic degeneration · varus / valgus alignment |
| X-ray — patellar height | Exclude rupture: **Blumensaat's line reaches inferior pole at 30° flexion** · **Insall-Salvati 0.8–1.2** |
| **Ultrasound** | Detects + localises disruption · **operator- and user-dependent** · tendinosis: **thicker, hypoechoic, power Doppler flow** |
| **MRI** | **Most sensitive** · **intrasubstance signal + tendon thickening** |

**Normal tendon width on US (OB)** — **2–3 cm** (figure shows 3.9 cm in tendinosis)

**Imaging morphology (King, OB)** — localised thickening, **hypoechoic areas**, **↑ vascularity**

![Quadriceps tendinosis on ultrasound: thick hypoechoic tendon with Doppler flow](../../MCQ/attachments/q220028-a-screenshot-2024-12-28-at-4-15-27-pm.jpg)

![Quadriceps tendinitis on sagittal MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3016-quad-tendonitis-mri.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Quadriceps tendinopathy
├─ First line → rest, ice, NSAIDs, activity modification, PT (ROM → eccentric)
├─ Failed nonop → PRP (leukocyte-rich) or polidocanol injection
└─ Failed ≥ 3 months, severe → arthroscopic / open debridement (very rare)
```

### Eccentric strengthening

**Best rehab for overuse tendinopathy (TO)** — **eccentric strengthening** (TO answer not given; eccentric = first line in OB)\
✗ Wrong options: strict bed rest 1 month · plaster 1 month · steroid injection first line then strength training

**Nonop** — **mainstay** · rest until pain improves → PT **ROM first → eccentric exercises** · hamstring, quadriceps + core strengthening

**Cortisone injection** — **contraindicated** → **quadriceps tendon rupture**

**CrossFit, 1 month pain, US tendinosis** — **PT for eccentric strengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic debridement · open debridement + repair (needs ≥ 3 months failed nonop) · leukocyte-poor PRP · jumper's knee strap

**PRP** — **leukocyte-rich** PRP effective for **patellar** tendinopathy · **not first line**\
✗ Wrong options: leukocyte-poor

**Dragoo RCT (OB)** — 23 patients, patellar tendinopathy · eccentric exercise + US-guided dry needling + **LR-PRP** → **faster recovery**, benefit **dissipates over time** · no failures at 26 wk

**Injections after failed nonop (King, OB)** — **PRP** + **sclerosing agents (polidocanol)** → good outcomes

**Jumper's knee strap** — **patellar tendon strap** → for **patellar** tendinopathy, not quadriceps tendinosis

**Surgery** — **quadriceps tendon debridement**, **very rarely** · only after **≥ 3 months** failed nonop (OB) · arthroscopic or open → good outcomes

### Kinesiology taping

**K-tape (TO)** — "**kinesis**" = movement · **stretchy cotton / synthetic** tape mimicking **skin elasticity** + muscle extension capacity · invented by Japanese chiropractor **Kenzo Kase, 1970s**

**vs classic rigid taping (TO)** — classic = immobilisation / stability · K-tape → **joints stay mobile**

**Stretch (TO)** — **40–60% of original length** on average · up to **120–150%** by manufacturer

**Mechanism (TO)** — tape contracts → **lifts skin + subcutis off fascia** → space → ↑ blood + **lymph flow** (removes inflammatory factors) → ↓ pain + swelling · ↑ proprioception + muscle function · applied over **trigger points**

**Wear (TO)** — **3–5 days** · waterproof **acrylic adhesive** (avoid rubbing while bathing) · trim hair · **caution with anticoagulants** (skin haemorrhages)

**Pre-stretch (TO)** — manufactured on backing paper with **10% stretch** · still called "**unstretched**" · keep this pre-stretch on application

| Method (TO) | Stretch applied to |
|---|---|
| **Neutral technique** ✓ | **Tissue, not tape** |
| **Stretch technique** | **Tape, not tissue** |

| Application (TO) | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Muscle** | ↑ or ↓ tone · \~**10%** stretch · **tonus decreasing = insertion → origin** · **tonus increasing = origin → insertion** |
| **Ligament** | Chronic tendinopathy, ligament overload, trigger points · **maximum stretch**, fixed to **both osseous insertions** → receptor stimulation |
| **Corrective** | Functional correction, e.g. **patellar maltracking** · **Y tapes**, maximum pre-stretch → shifts bone position |
| **Lymphatic** | **I tapes**, \~**25%** pre-stretch → skin lift → lymphatic drainage |

**Shapes (TO)** — **I, Y, X, web, fan** strips

![Kinesiology tape shapes: I, Y, X, web, fan](../../MCQ/attachments/TO19356182-2.png)

**Myths (TO)** — **"↑ performance" — false** (treatment only; **not banned** under anti-doping unless coated with banned substance; no active drug) · **colours differ — false** (cyan, magenta, beige, black — **same properties**; colour theory only: red stimulating, blue calming) · "can't heal without tape" — false

**K-tape — true (TO)** — **neutral application stretches tissue, not tape** = best pick\
✗ Wrong options: tonus decreasing origin → insertion · colours differ in stretch / hold · "40–60%" (only an average, varies up to 120–150%)

## X vs Y

| | **Quadriceps tendinopathy** | **Patellar tendinopathy (jumper's knee)** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | **Superior pole** of patella | **Inferior pole** of patella |
| Age | **Older adults** | Adolescents / young adults |
| Strap | No | **Patellar tendon (jumper's knee) strap** |
| Steroid injection | **Contraindicated** (rupture) | **Contraindicated** (rupture) |

| | **Tendonitis / tendinosis** | **Quadriceps tendon rupture** |
|---|---|---|
| Active extension against gravity | **Preserved** | Lost (no SLR) |
| Palpable gap | No | **Yes, within 2 cm of superior pole** |
| Patellar height | Normal | **Baja** |

## How it's asked

**"Best rehabilitation for overuse tendinopathy (TO)?"** → **eccentric strengthening**

**"CrossFit athlete, US tendinosis, 1 month — treatment?"** → **PT for eccentric strengthening exercises**

**"Kinesiology taping — true statement (TO)?"** → **neutral application stretches the tissue, not the tape**

**"Tonus-decreasing K-tape direction (TO)?"** → **insertion → origin**

**"K-tape stretch capacity (TO)?"** → **40–60% (up to 120–150%)**

**"Do tape colours differ (TO)?"** → **no — same properties**

**"Most sensitive imaging?"** → **MRI**

**"Why avoid cortisone?"** → **quadriceps tendon rupture**

**"Deep layer of quadriceps tendon?"** → **vastus intermedius**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Rectus Femoris Strain

*Knee & Sports · 53 / 61*

## 1 Basics

**Who** — **soccer + football** players

**Anatomy** — rectus femoris **crosses hip + knee** → **flexes hip, extends knee** · origin **AIIS (direct head)** → one of four quadriceps → **patellar tendon**

**Site by chronicity** — **acute → distal thigh** · **chronic → near origin**

### Rectus femoris origin avulsion (AIIS)

**Adolescents** — proximal **bony avulsion of AIIS** at the **direct head** of rectus femoris (see AIIS avulsion chapter)

**Track athlete, AIIS fragment above acetabulum** — **rectus femoris**\
✗ Wrong options: TFL · gluteus medius · gluteus minimus · biceps femoris

![AIIS avulsion in an adolescent (arrow)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3104-aiis.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **sudden forceful eccentric contraction** · **sprinting from standing**, **kicking a ball hard**

## 3 Presentation

| Pain site | Lesion |
|---|---|
| **Anterior hip** | Strain or **avulsion at AIIS** |
| **Mid-thigh** | Strain in **muscle fibres** (belly) |
| **Distal (near knee)** | **Most common finding** |

**Exam** — tenderness over the injured zone · **palpable defect = full tear (unusual)**

**Provocative test** — **pain on resisted hip flexion or extension** (Orthobullets) · AIIS avulsion → **most pain on resisted knee extension** (OB) — read the options

**11-yr-old hurdler, AIIS avulsion — most painful test (OB)** — **resisted knee extension** (rectus contraction greatest)\
✗ Wrong options: resisted adduction (adductors arise from **symphysis pubis**) · resisted abduction (**outer iliac crest**) · resisted hip extension (**posterior iliac crest**)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | **Adolescents with suspected AIIS avulsion** · otherwise **normal in most** |
| **MRI** | Strain extent · pseudotumour vs neoplasm |
| Ultrasound | Strain |

![Rectus femoris strain on MRI (arrow)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3104-1892024.jpg)

### Mid-substance strain / pseudotumour

**14-yr-old, 3 wk, firm tender mid-rectus mass after soccer** — **midsubstance partial rupture = "pseudotumour"**

**MRI = injury, not tumour** — **no mass effect on T1** · **defined fluid signal on T2** · **no significant gadolinium enhancement**

## 5 Management

```
Rectus femoris strain
├─ Vast majority → NSAIDs, rest, ice, stretching / strengthening
│      (resolves in 4–6 weeks)
└─ Midsubstance pseudotumour → NSAIDs + PT + repeat MRI at 6–8 weeks
       └─ Not resolved at 3–6 months → late debridement + repair
```

**Nonop** — **definitive treatment for the vast majority** · **resolves within 4–6 wk**

**Pseudotumour** — **nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs, physical therapy + repeat MRI scan in 6–8 weeks** to confirm resolution\
✗ Wrong options: I&D of abscess · open biopsy · haematoma evacuation + musculotendinous repair · wide resection + radiotherapy

## X vs Y

| | **Acute strain** | **Chronic strain** | **Adolescent AIIS avulsion** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Site | **Distal thigh** | **Near origin** | **AIIS (direct head)** |
| Imaging | MRI / US | MRI / US | **X-ray** |

## How it's asked

**"Teen track athlete, AIIS avulsion — muscle?"** → **rectus femoris**

**"Hurdler, AIIS avulsion — most painful test?"** → **resisted knee extension**

**"Firm mid-rectus mass, T2 fluid, no enhancement — management?"** → **NSAIDs, PT, repeat MRI in 6–8 weeks**

**"Most common pain site in rectus strain?"** → **distal**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Rib Stress Fracture

*Knee & Sports · 54 / 61*

## 1 Basics

**Incidence** — **uncommon** stress fracture site · elite > non-elite rowers · sweep (1 oar) = sculler (2 oars) (OB)

| Site | Where | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **First rib** | **Anterolateral** · weak point = **subclavian artery groove** | **Baseball pitching**, basketball, weightlifting, ballet |
| **Middle ribs 4–9** | **Lateral + anterolateral** | **Competitive rowers** |
| Rowing (OB) | **Ribs 4–8 (or 5–9)**, anterolateral / lateral | Rowers |
| Middle ribs 4th–9th (TO) | — | **Rowers** — **serratus anterior** traction |
| Cough (OB) | **Anterior → posterior axillary line, ribs 5–9** | Chronic cough |
| **Posteromedial ribs** | — | **Novice golfers** |

**Risk factors** — **amenorrhoea**, osteopenia / osteoporosis, extreme overuse, **repetitive coughing** · OB: low BMD, **poor technique + ↑ training volume**, low calcium, low vitamin D, eating disorders, **low testosterone**, **depot medroxyprogesterone**

**Rowing triggers (OB)** — **bigger blade** (↑ load per stroke) · ↑ volume incl. **long endurance rows at low stroke rate**

## 2 Mechanism

**Repetitive contraction** — stress on rib from attached muscle · **muscles strengthen faster than bone** during training

**Muscle fatigue** — ↓ ability to absorb + dissipate opposing forces

**First rib** — **scalenes pull superiorly** vs **serratus anterior + intercostals pull inferiorly** → fails at subclavian groove

**Rowers (OB, Karlson)** — deforming forces of **serratus anterior + external oblique** (attach ribs 5–9)

![First rib stress fracture at subclavian groove](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12108-firstrib.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **insidious** pain · ↑ with **coughing, deep inspiration, overhead activity** · acute "**snap**" = completion of fatigued bone (throwing, batting, lifting)

**Exam** — **focal tenderness over the rib** · palpable **callus** when advanced

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use / finding |
|---|---|
| **AP chest X-ray** | **Negative in up to 60%** of rib fractures (any cause) · may show line or callus |
| Bone scan | X-ray negative + suspicion · ↑ activity · **rowing and cough fractures look identical** |
| CT | Suspected **pathologic** fracture · localises uptake at **costotransverse** region · clear fracture pattern |
| **MRI** | X-ray negative + suspicion · **no radiation** · **used more than bone scan in athletes** · marrow oedema ± line |

**Inspiration / expiration films** — for **pneumothorax**, which is **not associated**

![Bone scan — rib stress fracture](../../MCQ/attachments/q4718-a-fig-a.jpg)

## 5 Management

**Nonop (majority, heal uneventfully)** — **rest, analgesia, stop inciting activity**, correct training errors / mechanics

**Duration conflict** — **\~4–6 weeks** (Orthobullets) · **1–2 weeks off rowing**, then **slow return** at low intensity, pain-free (OB) — read the options

**Rower, cough-aggravated chest pain, hot rib on bone scan** — **rest, analgesia, slow return to rowing**\
✗ Wrong options: inspiration / expiration X-rays · operative stabilisation (only if recalcitrant) · scan differs from cough fracture · **more** protraction / retraction

**Stroke modification (OB)** — **less scapular protraction at catch + less retraction at finish** (less reach + pull-through) · proposed, **not clinically evaluated**

## 6 Complications

**Non-union** — the listed complication

## How it's asked

**"Olympic rower, bigger blade, rib uptake on bone scan — correct?"** → **rest, analgesia, slow return to rowing**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Snapping Hip (Coxa Saltans)

*Knee & Sports · 55 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Aka (TO)** — coxa saltans = **dancer's hip** · a **rare** disease (TO)

**Who** — athletes + dancers in **teens / twenties** · **ballet dancers** + athletes needing **repetitive extreme hip flexion + abduction** (TO)

**Common in (TO)** — **dancers** · applies to snapping hip generally **and** internal snapping\
✗ Wrong options: gymnasts · long-distance runners · cyclists

**Painless snapping** — up to **10% of general population** → **normal** (OB)

### Types & causes

| Type | Cause | Snap / pain |
|---|---|---|
| **External** — **most common** (Orthobullets, OB, TO) | **ITB over greater trochanter** · also **TFL or gluteus medius** over GT (OB) | **Lateral** · **visible** |
| **Internal** | **Iliopsoas** over **femoral head**, **iliopectineal ridge / eminence**, **lesser trochanter exostoses**, **iliopsoas bursa** · iliofemoral ligament (OB) | **Anterior** · **audible** click |
| **Intra-articular** | **Loose bodies** (traumatic, **synovial chondromatosis**), **labral tears**, osteochondral fragments (TO) | **Catching, locking**, clicking |

**Extra- vs intra-articular (TO)** — **extra-articular more common** · divided into internal + external · **external more common than internal**

**See vs hear** — external snapping **seen** from across the room · internal snapping **heard** from across the room

**Internal snapping cause (TO)** — **thickened iliopsoas tendon snapping over iliopectineal eminence**\
✗ Wrong options: labral tear · ITB over GT · FAI

**Iliacus (OB)** — may contribute as it overlies **anterior capsule + superior pubic rami**

### Internal snapping (iliopsoas) – anatomy

**Iliopsoas origin + insertion** — **anterior surfaces + lower borders of L1–L5 transverse processes + iliac fossa → lesser trochanter**\
✗ Wrong options: greater trochanter

**Distractor structures (OB)** — anterior → posterior acetabulum = **labrum** (tear → pain on **FADIR**) · **external lip of iliac crest → lateral tibial condyle (Gerdy's tubercle)** = **ITB**\
✗ Wrong options: medial condyle

**Snap mechanics** — iliopsoas tendon **lateral to iliopectineal eminence in full flexion** → **snaps medially** as hip returns to neutral

**Tendon bands (Philippon, OB)** — single **28.3%**, **double 64.2%**, triple 7.5% · **psoas major most medial**, primary iliacus tendon lateral → **more than one tendon may need release**

![Internal snapping: iliopsoas sliding over the femoral head](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3096-illustration.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **snapping** in / around hip · **painful or painless** · patient can **reproduce** it · worse with activity

**Clicking / locking** — suggests **intra-articular** pathology

**Pain (TO)** — tendon inflamed → snapping becomes painful · internal → **anterior** pain + audible click · external → **lateral** pain + click

### External snapping (iliotibial band) – Ober test

**Exam** — palpate **greater trochanter** as hip **actively flexed** · **pressure stops the snapping** → confirms

**Ober test** — **limited hip adduction with hip extended** ✓ · lateral decubitus, **can't adduct across midline**\
✗ Wrong options: adduction in flexion · abduction flexed / extended · bow-legged gait

**Why** — **TFL flexes + abducts** hip → contracture limits **extension + adduction**

**Prone Ober (Gautam)** — stabilises pelvis, holds extension → **quantifies** contracture

![Ober test](../../MCQ/attachments/q2857-illa-ober-test-image.jpg)

### Internal snapping – provocative manoeuvres

| Source | Manoeuvre reproducing internal snap |
|---|---|
| **Orthobullets** | Passive **flexed + ER → extended + IR** |
| **OB (Yen)** | **Flexion-abduction-ER (FABER) → extension, adduction, IR** |
| **OB (Frank, JAAOS 2013)** | **Flexed + IR → extended + ER** |
| OB | Supine, **flex > 90° → extend to neutral**; accentuate with FABER in flexion, adduction + IR while extending |
| OB | **Flexion + extension**, or **flexion + ER + abduction** |
| **TO** | Rotate the **extended + adducted** hip IR / ER · **flex the extended hip** · **extend the flexed hip** |

**Manoeuvre conflict** — Orthobullets / Yen (**ER → IR** on extending) vs Frank (**IR → ER** on extending) — read the options; FABER → neutral is the commonest stem

**Dancer, popping on FABER → neutral** — **internal snapping** · no pain on IR of flexed hip → **labral tear unlikely**

**Released psoas tendon — pre-op finding** — **snapping with active hip flexion** ✓ · **FABER → neutral triggers popping** ✓

| Finding (OB distractor) | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| **Positive Ober** | **External** snapping (ITB / TFL) |
| Pain on IR + ER with hip + knee **extended** · IR only to neutral at 90° flexion, full ER | **Pincer FAI** |
| ↓ IR, limited **FABER → FADIR**, positive impingement (flexion, adduction, IR) | **Cam FAI** |
| ↓ active ROM, **catching, locking, grinding** on passive motion | **Intra-articular loose bodies** |
| **Loose bodies on X-ray** | **Synovial chondromatosis** → loose body removal |
| Labral tear on MRI | **Secondary to FAI** → repair vs debridement |
| **Pain on half sit-up + tender pubic ramus** | **Athletic pubalgia** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical** for external + internal (TO: mainly clinical in extra-articular) · **advanced imaging** usually needed for **intra-articular**

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| X-ray — AP pelvis / hip | **Usually normal** · **rule out synovial chondromatosis** · occasional **calcification near lesser trochanter** (OB) |
| **Dynamic ultrasound** | **Best to confirm internal snapping** (shows tendon in motion) · shows snapping band (internal or external) · **guides diagnostic injection** (trochanteric bursa / iliopsoas sheath / joint) |
| **MRI** (often **arthrogram**) | **Rule out intra-articular** pathology + mimics · inflamed bursa · dynamic USG / MRI help **intra-articular** diagnosis (TO) |
| **Iliopsoas bursography** | Contrast into bursa under **fluoroscopy** → tendon seen · ± therapeutic injection · **radiation to pelvis** |

**Confirm internal snapping (OB ×2)** — **ultrasound**\
✗ Wrong options: conventional MRI · CT arthrogram · weight-bearing X-ray · fluoroscopy without contrast · bone scan · CT · plain radiographs

**US anatomy (OB)** — **hyperechoic oval iliopsoas tendon** anterior to **superior pubic ramus**, posterior to **hypoechoic iliacus**

## 5 Management

```
Snapping hip
├─ Painless → no treatment
├─ Painful, acute (< 6 months) → activity modification
├─ Persistent, painful, limits ADLs → PT stretching + corticosteroid injection
└─ Failed nonop → surgery by type
       ├─ External → GT bursa excision + ITB Z-plasty
       ├─ Internal → iliopsoas tendon release / lengthening
       └─ Intra-articular (MRI loose body / labral tear) → hip arthroscopy
```

**Nonop (TO)** — **NSAIDs, ice, rest**, ultrasonic + interferential therapy, **stretching of iliopsoas + ITB** → **effective in majority** · **local anaesthetic + corticosteroid** into bursa / tendon sheath

**Resistant (TO)** — **surgical release + lengthening of iliopsoas tendon and ITB**

**Intra-articular (TO)** — **arthroscopic loose body removal + labral repair = treatment of choice**

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Painful external snapping**, failed nonop | **GT bursa excision + ITB Z-plasty** |
| **Snapping after THR** | **GT bursa excision + ITB Z-plasty** |
| **Painful internal snapping**, failed nonop | **Iliopsoas tendon release** |
| **Intra-articular**, failed nonop + **MRI loose bodies / labral tear** | **Hip arthroscopy** — loose body removal, labral debridement / repair |

### External snapping (iliotibial band) – Z-plasty

**Mainstay** — **stretching** (positive Ober = tight ITB) · often **PT alone** · occasional steroid injection for **profound GT bursitis**

**Runner pattern (OB)** — long symptom duration · **downward leg** (left leg running on left side of road)

**Most reliable surgery** — **Z-plasty of ITB** near **proximal origin** · may be **endoscopic**\
✗ Wrong options: **release at Gerdy's tubercle — not described** · iliopsoas release · labral debridement · FAI debridement

**Results (OB)** — Z-plasty good in 10 patients (**Faraj**) · release good in 16 patients / 17 hips (**White**)

![ITB Z-plasty lengthening](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3096-z-plasty.jpg)

### Internal snapping (iliopsoas) – release

**Nonop success (Gruen, OB)** — **63%** of 30 improve nonop · rest do well with **open lengthening**

**Open approaches** — anterior, medial, ilioinguinal, iliofemoral · **partial or complete** release · traditionally open → now **arthroscopic**

**Arthroscopic routes** — **endoscopic at lesser trochanter** · **transcapsular** at the hip joint: from **central compartment** (Orthobullets) · from **peripheral compartment** (OB) — read the options

**Ilizaliturri RCT (OB)** — **lesser trochanter (10) vs transcapsular (9)** → both ↑ WOMAC, **no difference** · both effective + reproducible

**Regeneration (Marquez Arabia, OB)** — 27 patients · tendon **regenerates in all** by 23 months (**84%** of original circumference) · **all 5/5 hip flexion** · muscle fibres attach directly to proximal femoral shaft → no retraction

**Post-op** — **no hip flexion strengthening for 6 wk**

## X vs Y

| | **External** | **Internal** | **Intra-articular** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Structure | **ITB over GT** | **Iliopsoas over iliopectineal eminence / femoral head** | **Loose body, labral tear** |
| Frequency | **Most common** | Less | Least (extra > intra) |
| Site | **Lateral**, visible | **Anterior / groin**, audible | Deep, catching / locking |
| Key test | **Ober**, palpate GT during active flexion | **FABER → neutral (extension)** | FADIR (labrum), locking |
| Best imaging | Clinical | **Dynamic ultrasound** | **MRI arthrogram** |
| Surgery | **ITB Z-plasty** + GT bursa excision | **Iliopsoas release** | **Arthroscopy** |

![External snapping: ITB sliding over the greater trochanter](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3096-iliotibial-band-snaping.jpg)

## How it's asked

**"Internal snapping hip common in (TO)?"** → **dancers**

**"Snapping hip syndrome common in (TO)?"** → **dancers**

**"Cause of internal snapping (TO)?"** → **thickened iliopsoas tendon over iliopectineal eminence**

**"Dancer, anterior popping FABER → neutral — confirm with?"** → **ultrasound**

**"Ballet dancer, painful popping on flexion + ER — best test?"** → **ultrasonography**

**"Pre-op finding before psoas tenotomy (MRI figure)?"** → **FABER → neutral triggers popping**

**"Psoas released arthroscopically — pre-op finding?"** → **snapping with active hip flexion**

**"Origin / insertion of structure in internal snapping?"** → **L1–L5 transverse processes + iliac fossa → lesser trochanter**

**"Positive Ober test?"** → **limited hip adduction when extended**

**"Runner, positive Ober, failed stretching — most reliable surgery?"** → **Z-plasty of the iliotibial band**

**"Painless lateral snapping, normal X-ray — structure?"** → **iliotibial band**

**"Most common type of snapping hip?"** → **external (ITB)**

**"Post-op restriction after iliopsoas release?"** → **no hip flexion strengthening for 6 weeks**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Spontaneous Osteonecrosis of the Knee (SONK)

*Knee & Sports · 56 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Who** — **middle-aged + elderly females** · **women > 55 yr** (Orthobullets, OB) · **women > 60 yr** (OB5977) — read the options

**Joints** — **99% only one joint**

**Site** — **epiphysis of medial femoral condyle** (weight-bearing MFC) · also **lateral femoral condyle**, **patella** (OB) · **tibial plateau** (OB)

**Pareek (OB)** — 223 patients with subchondral insufficiency fracture: **femur 69%**, tibia 55%, **medial compartment 89%** · > 5 yr: **47% surgery, 37% arthroplasty** (\~**⅓ → TKA**)

**Ecker + Lotke (OB)** — 15 patients (16 knees), **medial tibial plateau** tenderness · first X-ray lucent in **9 / 16**, all 16 eventually · **TKA 9, UKA 3**, 2 refused, 2 resolved

### Types of knee osteonecrosis

| Type | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Spontaneous (SONK)** | **Most common** · idiopathic · MFC · elderly women |
| **Secondary** | Risk factors (**lupus, alcohol, sickle cell**) · more **diffuse** · see osteonecrosis chapter |
| **Post-arthroscopic** | **Rarest** · after **meniscectomy**, age **> 55 yr** (Di Caprio) |

### Pathophysiology

**Core lesion** — **subchondral insufficiency fracture** in poor-quality bone → **crescent-shaped** osteonecrosis

**Cascade (OB)** — **SIF of MFC → osteonecrosis + flattening → degenerative posterior horn medial meniscus tear**

**Meniscus** — **posterior horn medial meniscal tears**, may involve the **root** · **root injuries in up to 80%** of SONK (OB)

**Root tear (Bhatia)** — loss of **hoop stresses** → **extrusion** → ↑ tibiofemoral contact pressure ≈ **total meniscectomy**

**Post-arthroscopic** — mostly after **partial meniscectomy** → altered biomechanics + **loss of shock absorption** → insufficiency fracture → crescent osteonecrosis

**Weight bearing** — exacerbates · **treatment based on size + signal** (Zaremski)

## 3 Presentation

**Onset** — **sudden severe knee pain** · **worse at night** + with weight bearing

**Exam** — **effusion** · ROM limited by pain · **tenderness over MFC** / medial joint line · slight synovitis

**Tibial plateau lesion (OB)** — medial pain + tenderness, ROM normal, no deformity · **misdiagnosed as degenerative meniscus or OA**

**Post-arthroscopic pattern** — well at 2 wk after partial medial meniscectomy → **↑ medial pain at 6 wk** → MRI: **osteonecrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: osteomyelitis (no infection signs) · advanced OA · inflammatory arthritis · osteochondral lesion

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| X-ray — **standing AP + lateral of hip, knee + ankle** | **Diagnostic in advanced disease** · **radiolucent subchondral lesion** · flattened MFC · **osteopenic area** in medial tibial plateau on lateral · early films may be normal |
| **MRI** | **Most useful study** · confirms + **sizes** lesion → guides treatment |
| MRI — lesion | **Crescent-shaped** · **subchondral hypointense crescent line**, cortical irregularity |
| MRI — signal | **T1 marrow hypointensity** + **T2 hyperintensity (oedema)** · **serpiginous border** · localised low-signal area + surrounding oedema |

![SONK of the medial femoral condyle (radiograph)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3026-sonk.jpg)

![Subchondral crescent line + marrow oedema of the MFC (MRI)](../../MCQ/attachments/q219981-a-screenshot-2024-12-05-at-1-00-51-pm.jpg)

### Diagnosis – MRI (osteonecrosis vs bone bruise / insufficiency fracture)

**74-yr-old woman, acute medial pain, no trauma** — **osteonecrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: OA · RA · medial meniscal tear · transient osteoporosis

**52-yr-old man, sudden atraumatic pain, worse at night, tibial plateau lesion** — **osteonecrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: CRPS (**MRI normal**) · OA (both MFC + MTP involved) · osteosarcoma (bone-forming) · inflammatory arthritis (whole knee)

**28-yr-old skier, MRI after injury** — **bone bruise** · **↑ T2 = haemorrhage from trabecular microfractures** · common after **ACL + MCL** injury\
✗ Wrong options: osteosarcoma · bucket-handle tear · LCL tear · tibial spine avulsion

| Concurrent injury with SONK (OB) | Likely? |
|---|---|
| **Horizontal tear of posterior horn medial meniscus** | ✓ **Most likely** |
| Complex lateral meniscus tear | No (medial predilection) |
| PCL tear | No |
| Patellofemoral arthritis | No |
| LCL avulsion (lateral epicondyle) | No |

### Differential diagnosis

| Condition | Who / key fact |
|---|---|
| **SONK** | **Elderly women**, **MFC (weight-bearing)**, 99% one joint |
| **Osteochondritis dissecans** | **Adolescent / young males**, **lateral aspect of MFC** |
| **Transient osteoporosis** | **Young–middle-aged men** |
| **Bone bruise / occult fracture** | **Trauma**, bone fragility, overuse |
| Secondary osteonecrosis | Risk factors, multifocal |

## 5 Management

```
SONK
├─ Most / pre-collapse / small (< 3.5 cm) → NSAIDs, protected WB, quads PT, unloader brace
├─ Failed nonop, unicompartmental → UKA
├─ Failed nonop, large (> 5 cm) / collapse / diffuse → TKA
└─ Younger with angular malalignment → HTO
```

**Nonop** — **mainstay** (most resolve, **self-limiting**) · **NSAIDs, narcotics, protected WB** · **PT for quadriceps strengthening** · **unloader brace**, sometimes **bisphosphonates** (OB) · good results

**Post-arthroscopic** — most nonop · **protected WB 4–8 wk** (Di Caprio)

**Timing (OB)** — most patients seen **post-collapse** → **arthroplasty** treatment of choice · pre-collapse treatment **controversial**

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Procedure |
|---|---|
| Failed nonop, **small lesion**, one compartment | **UKA** |
| Failed nonop, **large lesion / bone collapse**, diffuse / multicompartment | **TKA** |
| **Angular malalignment** (younger) · offload an osteochondral allograft | **HTO** |
| Younger / active (OB) | Microfracture, **osteochondral allograft**, HTO |
| Lesion size (OB) | **< 3.5 cm → nonop** · **> 5 cm + failed nonop → arthroplasty** |

**59-yr-old librarian, MFC lesion, failed nonop** — **UKA**\
✗ Wrong options: open biopsy (no matrix / periosteal reaction) · chest CT + bone scan (subchondral condyle not a metastatic site) · OATS (defect size + age) · valgus HTO (59 yr, no varus)

**58-yr-old, antiphospholipid, crescentic collapse, failed PT / NSAIDs / PRP** — **arthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: core decompression (can't treat full-thickness collapse) · HTO (younger + angular deformity) · medial root repair (doesn't fix cartilage loss) · stem cells (not FDA approved)

### Treatment – unicompartmental / total arthroplasty & outcomes

**UKA for SONK — true** — **reliable long-term outcomes** · **treatment of choice for severe localised disease** · TKA **rarely needed** as primary

**UKA traps** — ✗ Wrong options: only for spontaneous (works for **secondary** localised too) · higher failure than OA (**comparable**) · tibial stress fracture commonest failure · TKA needed because UKA fails

| UKA series (OB) | Result |
|---|---|
| **Myers** (systematic review) | TKA / UKA for SONK ≈ for OA (correct indications) |
| **Parratte** | Pain relief **96%** (≥ 3 yr) · 12-yr survivorship **96.7%** · spontaneous + secondary |
| **Heyse** (52) | **97.3%** satisfied · survival **93.1% at 10 yr**, **90.6% at 15 yr** |
| **Bruni** (84) | 10-yr survival **89%** · revisions: **tibial subsidence 4**, aseptic loosening 3 · **no lateral progression** |
| **Kaneko** (61) | 10-yr implant survival **90%** |
| **Chalmers** (46) | **Secondary ON less durable** than SONK · failure from **arthritis progression** |

**Commonest reason for revision after UKA** — **progression of arthritis** (OB213949) · **aseptic loosening** most often (OB213790) · loosening + progression both (OB213790 answers) · subsidence + loosening, no progression (Bruni) — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: aseptic loosening · bearing dislocation · implant failure · periprosthetic fracture

**UKA contraindications (OB)** — **inflammatory arthropathy**, **fixed deformity > 5–10°**, **motion < 90°**, **previous meniscectomy**, symptomatic other compartment

### Bone marrow oedema adjuncts

**Persistent BME (Akhavan)** — prognostic for pain, dysfunction, cartilage damage · surgery: **core decompression**, bone-graft injections, arthroplasty

**Zoledronic acid (Flores-Robles)** — primary BMES (ankle > hip) · **75% complete response** at 12 months

**Subchondroplasty (Cohen)** — fluoroscopic **calcium phosphate** injection into bone marrow lesions · ↑ VAS + IKDC to 2 yr

## X vs Y

| | **SONK** | **Secondary osteonecrosis** |
|---|---|---|
| Who | **Women > 55 yr** | Risk factors (steroids, lupus, alcohol, sickle cell) |
| Extent | **One joint (99%)**, MFC epiphysis | Multiple compartments, **80% bilateral** (osteonecrosis chapter) |
| Cause | **Subchondral insufficiency fracture** ± root tear | ↓ blood flow |
| UKA durability | **Better** | Less durable (Chalmers) |

| | **SONK** | **OCD** | **Bone bruise** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Patient | **Elderly woman** | **Adolescent male** | Young, trauma |
| Site | **Weight-bearing MFC** | **Lateral aspect of MFC** | After ACL / MCL injury |
| MRI | **Crescent**, T1 dark + T2 bright, serpiginous border | Osteochondral fragment | **↑ T2**, trabecular microfractures |

## How it's asked

**"74-yr-old woman, acute medial pain — MRI diagnosis?"** → **osteonecrosis**

**"Skier, MRI after injury?"** → **bone bruise**

**"52-yr-old, sudden atraumatic pain, worse at night — MRI?"** → **osteonecrosis**

**"Medial pain 6 wk after partial meniscectomy, crescent ↑ T2?"** → **osteonecrosis (post-arthroscopic)**

**"Lupus, SONK on MRI — concurrent injury?"** → **posterior horn medial meniscus tear (Injury C)**

**"59-yr-old, MFC lesion, failed nonop — best treatment?"** → **unicompartmental knee arthroplasty**

**"UKA for SONK — true?"** → **reliable long-term outcomes**

**"Commonest reason for revision after UKA for SONK?"** → **progression of arthritis**

**"Crescentic collapse, failed PRP / NSAIDs — treatment?"** → **arthroplasty**

**"OCD vs SONK — site?"** → **lateral aspect of MFC (OCD) vs weight-bearing MFC (SONK)**
