# Steroids & Stimulants

*Knee & Sports · 57 / 61*

## 1 Basics

### Anabolic steroids – mechanism & adverse effects (lipids, aggression, alopecia, surgical risk)

**What they are** — **synthetic derivatives of testosterone** (anabolic-androgenic steroids, AAS) · oral or injection · mimic testosterone + dihydrotestosterone → ↑ protein synthesis

**Primary mechanism of ↑ muscle** — **↑ production of messenger RNA**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ cortisol production · VEGF · ↓ HDL (HDL falls but doesn't affect muscle)

**Other actions** — **stimulate GH release** · **reverse / attenuate cortisol (catabolic hormones)** → ↓ catabolism · more intense + sustained workouts · ↑ strength, ↓ recovery time · used to **gain muscle + lose fat** · ↑ erythropoiesis\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ fat

**Examples** — **methandrostenolone (Dianabol)**: first made 1960, most used + abused steroid worldwide · **stanozolol**: Winstrol (oral) / Winstrol Depot (IM), derived from **dihydrotestosterone** · **metandienone**, **DHMCT** (TO doping cases)

| AAS adverse effect (cardiometabolic) | Fact |
|---|---|
| **HDL** | **↓ HDL (20–70%)** + ↓ **apolipoprotein A1** (main HDL particle protein) ✓ (✗ Wrong options: ↑ HDL) |
| **LDL** | **↑ LDL > 20%** (OB) · **↑ or no effect** on LDL (OB) · ↑ LDL, hypercholesterolaemia (Orthobullets) |
| **Coronary risk** | Lipid changes → **CAD risk 3–6×** · premature CV events |
| **MI in young athlete** | **↑ LDL** (+ ↓ HDL) ✓ · ↑ O2 demand at peak exercise + accelerated atherosclerosis (✗ Wrong options: direct endothelial damage · ↓ BP · conduction change) |
| **Blood pressure** | **↑ systolic + diastolic** (hypertension) (✗ Wrong options: ↓ BP) |
| Heart | **LVH**, impaired diastolic filling, thrombosis |
| **Ventricular arrhythmia** | Cause of **sudden death** (✗ Wrong options: cause of CAD) · fatal cases in small case series |
| **Coagulation** | **↑ factor VII, IX + thromboxane** → hypercoagulable → **↓ bleeding time** (✗ Wrong options: ↑ bleeding time) |
| **Liver** | **Liver tumours** (Orthobullets) · **↑ liver enzymes** (OB) · oral AAS **induce hepatic enzymes** → less sensitive to anaesthetics (✗ Wrong options: impaired liver function) |
| **Fluid** | **Mineralocorticoid effect** + **diuretics used to mask** it → **fluid + electrolyte imbalance** ✓ |
| Ventilation | Large muscle mass + high calories → ↑ O2 use + CO2 → **↑ ventilatory requirement** (✗ Wrong options: lowered O2 requirement) |
| Spleen | **No effect** (✗ Wrong options: splenomegaly) |
| Immune | Immunosuppression (OB) |

| AAS adverse effect (endocrine, skin, MSK, psych) | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Gonadal** | **Testicular atrophy (40–51%)** · ↓ gonadotropins + sex hormones (oestrogen, testosterone) → **↓ BMD**, **oligo- / azoospermia (↓ sperm count)** · uterine atrophy |
| **Gynaecomastia** | **10–34%** |
| **Skin** | **Acne (40–54%)** · cutaneous striae (34%) · injection-site pain (36%) · **alopecia / male-pattern baldness** |
| Female | **Irreversible deepening of voice** (Orthobullets) |
| Child | **Growth retardation** |
| **MSK** | **Tendon + muscle injury / rupture** (e.g. **pectoralis major**) · osteonecrosis of hip (OB) · ↑ body weight |
| **Behaviour** | **Aggression, hostility, violence = most common** ✓ · psychosis, depression, mania **rare** · suicidal behaviour · **no drug dependence** (✗ Wrong options: like narcotics) |
| **Not linked** | **Decreased vision** ✓ (methandrostenolone EXCEPT question) |

**Reversibility** — **alopecia = only nonreversible effect** (OB) · Orthobullets also lists **irreversible female voice deepening** — read the options · on stopping: hypertrophy + training gains lost quickly, acne + personality effects reversible

**Lipids after stopping** — **HDL / LDL ratios return to pre-existing levels** (OB) · **changes persist after withdrawal** (Hartgens, OB) — read the options

**Surgery + anaesthesia in AAS user** — **fluid and electrolyte imbalance** ✓\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ bleeding time (actually ↓) · impaired liver function · lowered O2 requirement · splenomegaly

![Gynaecomastia in anabolic steroid user](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3126-gynecomastia-steroids.jpg)

### Other hormones – HGH, androstenedione, DHEA, EPO, somatotropin

| Agent | Source / mechanism | Effect | Adverse |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Human growth hormone (HGH)** | **Most abundant pituitary product** · direct anabolic (amino acids → protein) · **popular AAS substitute** · IGF-1 similar | **↑ muscle size, not strength** | **Myopathy, carpal tunnel syndrome, insulin resistance** (Orthobullets) · **acromegaly**, heart disease, cardiac failure, glucose intolerance (OB) |
| **Androstenedione** | Androgen from **adrenal glands + gonads** | Potent anabolic · **liver → testosterone** | As testosterone |
| **DHEA** | Natural, **adrenal cortex** | → androstenedione → testosterone | **= testosterone** effects |
| **Erythropoietin (EPO)** | Stimulates Hb / RBC production · doping agent | **↑ O2-carrying capacity** | **↑ blood viscosity → stroke / MI** · ischaemic + thromboembolic events, HTN, flu-like symptoms, **hyperkalaemia** (OB) |
| **Somatotropin** | Growth hormone | **Type 1 hypertrophy**, type 2 atrophy | — |
| **Glucagon** | Fasting hormone | **Catabolic** to skeletal muscle | — |

**Glucagon** — mobilises + uses stored nutrients to maintain fasting glucose → **catabolic** ✓ · acts **mainly on liver** (insulin: liver, adipose, muscle)\
✗ Wrong options: anabolic · no effect · nootropic (cognition / memory) · ergogenic (any external influence enhancing performance)

### Hormonal & metabolic effects (glucagon, endurance adaptations)

**Endurance training adaptations** — **↑ storage + utilisation of intramuscular lipids** ✓ → ↓ CHO contribution, **preserves muscle glycogen** · **type I** hypertrophy (✗ Wrong options: type II) · **↑ Krebs cycle enzymes** (✗ Wrong options: ↓) · **↓ rate of glycogen depletion** (depletion = fatigue) (✗ Wrong options: ↑) · **blunted catecholamine response** → ↓ HR at same intensity (✗ Wrong options: ↑ sympathetic activity)

### Creatine – side effects

**What it is** — from **glycine, arginine + methionine** · **supplement, not an anabolic steroid** · → **phosphocreatine → ATP source** (phosphate storage substrate) · off-season weightlifting

**Performance** — **mixed results** (Orthobullets) · ↑ work in **anaerobic** trials but **cannot ↑ peak force** (Orthobullets) · ↑ strength accrual + **↑ force / power in short maximal bouts** (Terjung, OB) — read the options · mild ↑ body composition + strength, limited proof of performance / recovery (OB) · greatest effect in **loading cycle**; not running / swimming (Branch, OB) · does not reduce muscle-damage markers (Rawson, OB)

**For endurance (TO)** — **not primarily endurance** · benefits **short bursts of power, high-intensity short-duration**

**Mechanism of dehydration** — intracellular creatine **pulls water from intravascular → intracellular space** → **dehydration** ✓

**Side effects** — **dehydration**, **muscle cramping** (✓ more common than with testosterone), water retention, nausea · strains, stiffness (Terjung) · ↑ muscle injury · **renal insufficiency (rare)**\
✗ Wrong options: tendon tears · ↓ HDL · hair loss, testicular atrophy, acne (testosterone)

**High-school football (McGuine, OB)** — **30%** use creatine · perceived benefit = faster recovery · biggest risk = **dehydration**

### Stimulants – caffeine, ephedra, amphetamines

**Class** — **caffeine**, **ephedra**, **ephedrine**, **"ma huang"** (energy drinks), **amphetamines**, methylphenidate (TO)

**Stimulant risks** — **dehydration**, **impaired heat management**, **high blood pressure**, nervous system impairment

**Caffeine dose** — **2–3 mg/kg** improves performance · ↓ fatigue + ↑ alertness · stimulates CNS, **↓ perceived exertion** · ↑ endurance, reaction time, alertness · **↑ fat oxidation** (TO) \
✗ Wrong options: **"no proven effect"** (TO)

**Caffeine status** — previously banned by IOC, **now allowed up to 12 µg/ml urine** (Orthobullets) · removed from WADA list **January 2004** (Exercise Science chapter) · **2018 WADA Monitoring Program, not prohibited** (TO) — read the options

**Caffeine adverse** — **↑ blood pressure** (OB)

**Ephedra** — **cardiac arrhythmias (can be fatal)** · psychiatric, autonomic, GI symptoms, palpitations (OB) · "apparently effective" + weight-loss supplement (TO)

**Other drugs (OB distractors)** — **furosemide = most commonly used banned diuretic** · **α-ketoglutarate** = supplement claimed to **remove excess ammonia** · **propranolol** → ↓ anxiety → steadier hands, even HR, focus

### Sports nutrition & supplements (protein, amino acids, carnosine, hydration) (TO)

| Protein requirement (TO) | g/kg/day |
|---|---|
| **RDA, healthy individual** | **0.8** · resting 0.8–1 |
| Endurance athlete | **1** (range 1.2–1.7) |
| **High-performance / top elite** | **1.6 = maximum required** ✓ |
| Healthy athletes, heavy workers | 1.2–1.6 · nitrogen-balance assay avoids excess |
| Physically active (strength or endurance) | 1.2–2.0 (0.5–0.9 g/lb) |

**Leucine (TO)** — **BCAA oxidised in endurance exercise** ✓ · enzyme **branched-chain oxo-acid dehydrogenase (BCOAD)**: \~4–7% active at rest → **\~25%** at exercise onset by **dephosphorylation** · amino acid oxidation = only **1–6%** of endurance energy cost\
✗ Wrong options: arginine, lysine, isoleucine

**Amino acid metabolism (TO)** — **liver** (> kidney) principal site · excess can't be stored / excreted → oxidised or → CHO + lipid · α-amino group removed → carbon skeleton → **pyruvate, acetyl-CoA, TCA intermediates**

**Vitamin K (TO)** — **bone mineralisation; deficiency → stress fracture** ✓ · with 25(OH)D + calcium regulates bone resorption / activation / distribution · **carboxylates osteocalcin** (mature bone cells, regulated by 25(OH)D) → binds Ca → mineralisation, ↓ bone loss, possibly ↓ fractures · deficiency → **non-carboxylated (inactive) osteocalcin** → Ca release from bone + **soft-tissue / arterial calcification** · 25(OH)D toxicity possible without vitamin K\
✗ Wrong options: B, C, A

**Carnosine (TO)** — dipeptide of **beta-alanine + L-histidine** ✓ · discovered **1900, Gulevich (Kharkov)** · **vitamin B6** essential precursor · high in brain, skeletal + cardiac muscle, GI · **antioxidant**, anti-ageing, glycaemic control · **animal origin** → vegan deficiency · **nutritional supplement, not doping** · ↑ fatigue recovery · may prevent diabetes, atherosclerosis, cognitive impairment · loading benefits **high-intensity exercise** · side effects + mechanism need research\
✗ Wrong options: glutamine / glutathione / alanine · arginine · methionine + cysteine

**Dietary supplement (TO)** — taken **by mouth**, contains "dietary ingredient" (vitamins, minerals, herbs / botanicals, amino acids, enzymes, organ tissues, glandulars, metabolites, extracts) · tablets, capsules, softgels, liquids, powders, bars · **legal — regulated as food, not drugs** · safe at recommended doses; excess may harm

| Effectiveness (TO; classification debated) | Supplements |
|---|---|
| **Apparently effective** | **Creatine, ephedra, sodium bicarbonate, beta-alanine** |
| Possibly effective | **BCAAs**, green tea extract, calcium, hydroxymethyl butyrate (HMB) |
| **Apparently ineffective** | **Glutamine, carnitine** |

| Purpose (TO) | Supplements |
|---|---|
| Muscle building | Creatine, essential amino acids, HMB, glutamine |
| Weight loss | Green tea extract, carnitine, ephedra, caffeine |
| Performance | Creatine, sodium bicarbonate, caffeine, beta-alanine, glutamine |
| Recovery | Antioxidants |

| Supplement (TO) | Function | Performance effect | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Creatine** | ↑ ATP for short bursts | **High-intensity, short-duration** | Meat, fish |
| **Caffeine** | CNS stimulant, ↓ perceived exertion, **↑ fat oxidation** | **Endurance**, reaction time, alertness | Coffee, tea, energy drinks |
| **Sodium bicarbonate** | **Buffers lactic acid** (✗ Wrong options: acidifier) | Anaerobic high-intensity · delays fatigue | Baking soda |
| **BCAAs** | **Essential amino acids** · muscle protein synthesis ✓ | ↓ soreness, **delays central fatigue** (compete with **tryptophan** uptake into brain) | Meat, dairy |
| Glutamine | Immune function, muscle repair | Recovery; small direct effect | Meat, dairy, eggs |
| Carnitine | Fat oxidation | Limited / inconsistent benefit | Red meat, dairy, avocado |
| **Antioxidants** (vit C, E, polyphenols) | ↓ oxidative stress | Recovery · **excess / pre-workout impairs adaptation** (blunts mitochondrial biogenesis) | Fruit, vegetables, nuts |

**Endurance strategy (TO)** — **caffeine pre-workout + sodium bicarbonate pre-workout + BCAAs intra-workout** ✓\
✗ Wrong options: creatine (power only) · carnitine (inconsistent) · pre-workout antioxidants

| Sports drink (TO) | Carbohydrate | Purpose | Best for |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hypotonic | **< 6%** | Rapid hydration, minimal energy | **< 60 min** |
| **Isotonic** | **6–8%** (6–8 g / 100 ml) | Hydration + energy | **Endurance + team sports** |
| Hypertonic | **> 8%** | Energy, **slower absorption** | **Post-exercise recovery** |

**Isotonic drink (TO)** — Na+, K+, Mg2+, Cl− · osmolarity **\~300 mOsm/kg (≈ plasma)** → fast absorption · Gatorade, Powerade, Lucozade Sport · homemade: **500 ml water + 30–40 g sugar + ¼ tsp salt + 200 ml orange juice** (K+) · for **> 60 min**, marathon, cycling, soccer, basketball, **hot + humid** · ↓ cramps

**Soccer 90 min, hot + humid (TO)** — **isotonic sports drink, 6–8% CHO + electrolytes** ✓ · Exercise Science chapter: **low-osmolarity (< 10%)** CHO-electrolyte fluid\
✗ Wrong options: water only · hypertonic · skip until after

## 2 Mechanism

### Beta blockers – pharmacology (TO)

**Action** — **competitive antagonists** of adrenaline + noradrenaline at β-receptors · **β1: heart, kidney** · **β2: lungs, GI tract, vascular smooth muscle, skeletal muscle** · **β3: fat cells**

**Haemodynamics** — **↓ HR + ↓ cardiac output, no effect on stroke volume** · ↓ myocardial O2 demand

**In endurance sport** — ↑ perceived exertion, ↓ cardiac output + **VO2max**, **earlier fatigue + lactate threshold**, may worsen **EIB / asthma** → **avoid in asthmatics + endurance athletes**

**Why banned in precision sports** — **↓ HR, ↓ hand tremor, anxiolysis** → steadiness (**archery, shooting, billiards**)

### Gene doping (TO)

**Definition** — non-therapeutic gene therapy · DNA inserted via vector to **enhance performance** (✗ Wrong options: restore missing gene function) · **banned by WADA in 2003** ✓ (✗ Wrong options: 2001, 2006, 2008)

| Gene doping (TO) | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Viral vector** | Efficient · **immune response may ↓ effect** · = **transduction** |
| **Non-viral (bacterial plasmid)** | **No immune response** · = **transfection** |
| Strength genes | **IGF-1, myostatin** |
| Endurance genes | **EPO**, **PPARδ** (fibre-type switch; TO text prints "fast type 1 → slow type 2"), PEP carboxylase, **VEGF** |
| Tissue repair genes | **BMPs**, endorphins |
| Detection — direct | **Doping vector** |
| Detection — indirect | Consequences: **transcriptomics (mRNA)**, **proteomics** (protein set), **metabolomics** (non-protein low-MW metabolites) |
| Risks | **Immune response to viral vector (SIRS)**, autoimmunity to recombinant protein, no expression, **insertional mutagenesis** |

### Blood spinning – Orthokine vs PRP (TO)

**Orthokine (Kobe Bryant, knee)** — **variation of blood spinning**, **prohibited by WADA** (TO) · **20 cc blood** + tube with "factors" · **heated + cultured with glass beads** ("fever-like" response) → spun → injected · platelets + lymphocytes aid healing · WBCs make **IRAP** (IL-1 receptor antagonist)

**Orthokine vs PRP** — both autologous blood · Orthokine **cultured at higher temperature** · **↑ growth factors** per procedure · **blocks IL-1 → anti-inflammatory** · FDA: too much manipulation → **unapproved drug** \
✗ Wrong options: **"stored at 5 °C"** (EXCEPT answer)

![PRP vs Orthokine preparation](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17136111-1.jpg)

**Blood doping (TO)** — artificially recreates **high-altitude training** → banned · **EPO + CERA** (continuous erythropoietin receptor activator) give same effect **without blood removal** → banned in cycling + endurance sports

### Pregnancy & performance (TO)

| Pregnancy change (TO) | Timing / size |
|---|---|
| Blood volume | ↑ throughout · **peak 28–30 wk** |
| **Plasma volume** | Starts **\~8 wk**, plateaus 28–30 wk · **↑ 50–60%** |
| **Red cell volume** | **Starts at 18 wk** → term (38–40 wk) · **↑ 30%** |
| Physiological "anaemia" | Dilution · **Hb ↓ \~2 g/dl by 28 wk** |
| **hCG** | **Peaks 7–10 wk** (25,700–288,000 mIU/ml) then declines |

**Performance benefit from pregnancy** — **after 18 weeks** ✓ (RBC rise) · same TO text also says "**first 3 months** — natural RBC surplus" — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: before 18 wk · before / after 10 wk

**hCG** — men: **dose-dependent ↑ testosterone** → ergogenic · non-pregnant women: ovary → progesterone + oestradiol, **not testosterone**; 10,000 IU → T ↑ only \~1.1 ng/ml (≈ half daily diurnal swing) → no measurable muscle effect · normal female T **0.52–2.43 nmol/l** · pregnancy: hCG → **oestrogens + progesterone — not ergogenic**

**Hormones** — oestrogen: uterus + breast enlargement, ↑ uterine / tubal contractility · progesterone: ↓ uterine contractility, sustains pregnancy

**Pregnancy / abortion doping** — misuse of pregnancy ± abortion to ↑ RBC mass + O2 delivery · pregnant **2–3 months** then abortion **days before event** → up to **10% enhancement** ("MC method", artificial insemination) · "pregnancy dopes your blood" · sanctions extend to anyone who assists or covers up

## 3 Presentation

**AAS user on exam** — **↑ body weight**, well-developed muscles, **testicular atrophy**, **gynaecomastia**, **acne**, alopecia · women: deep voice

**17-year-old wrestler, aggression + hostility** — **stanozolol** (AAS) ✓\
✗ Wrong options: furosemide · EPO · α-ketoglutarate · propranolol

**Young male, pectoralis major tear, HDL 25 mg/dL, LDL 85, Hb 17.0** — **Dianabol (AAS)** ✓\
✗ Wrong options: creatine · GH · EPO · ephedra (none cause tendon tears or ↓ HDL)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Low HDL** | **Should alert physician to steroid use** (Orthobullets, OB) |
| **Testosterone : epitestosterone** | **> 6:1** (Orthobullets — "blood testing") |
| LDL / apo A1 | ↑ LDL · ↓ apolipoprotein A1 |
| Hb high-normal | ↑ erythropoiesis (AAS); ↑↑ with EPO |
| Factors VII, IX, thromboxane | ↑ (AAS) |
| Gonadotropins, oestrogen, testosterone | ↓ (AAS) |
| Liver enzymes | ↑ (AAS) |
| Potassium | ↑ (EPO) |
| Glucose tolerance | ↓ / insulin resistance (HGH) |
| Melatonin (TO) | Night **80–120 pg/ml** (\~80% made at night) · day 10–20 pg/ml |
| Gene-doping targets (TO) | IGF-1, myostatin, EPO, PPARδ, PEP carboxylase, VEGF, BMPs, endorphins |

### Key radiology findings

**Pectoralis major tear** — **T2 axial MRI**: tendon tear with **retraction** · clinical: **loss of axillary fold** + arm bruising · think **AAS (Dianabol)** with ↓ HDL

![Pectoralis major tendon tear (T2 axial MRI)](../../MCQ/attachments/q9112-a-pec-tear.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria

| Adverse analytical finding (AAF) (TO) | Urine threshold |
|---|---|
| **Salbutamol** | **> 1000 ng/ml** (24-h urine) |
| **Formoterol** | **> 40 ng/ml** |
| **Cathine** | **> 5 µg/ml** |
| **Ephedrine, methylephedrine** | **> 10 µg/ml** |
| **Pseudoephedrine** | **> 150 µg/ml** |
| Caffeine | Old limit **12 µg/ml** (Orthobullets) · now monitoring only (TO) |
| Threshold drug **+ diuretic / masking agent** | **AAF at any quantity** unless TUE for **both** |
| Glucocorticoid | Above lab reporting level in **in-competition** urine |

| Urine sample suitability (TO) | Specific gravity |
|---|---|
| **90 to < 150 ml** | **≥ 1.005 refractometer** or **≥ 1.010 lab sticks** |
| **≥ 150 ml** (dilute urine) | **≥ 1.003, refractometer only** |
| Unsuitable sample | Doesn't invalidate analysis · **lab decides** with testing authority |

**Dysthymia (TO)** — DSM-5 **removed "dysthymia"** → merged with chronic MDD = **persistent depressive disorder (PDD)** · chronic, early onset, symptoms fluctuate but never fully resolve

| PDD criteria (DSM-5-TR) (TO) | Requirement |
|---|---|
| A | **Depressed mood** most of the day, more days than not, **≥ 2 yr** adults · children / adolescents: irritable allowed, **≥ 1 yr** |
| B — **≥ 2 of** | Poor appetite / overeating · insomnia / hypersomnia · low energy / fatigue · low self-esteem · **poor concentration** / indecision · hopelessness |
| C | Never symptom-free **> 2 months** in the 2 yr (1 yr children) |
| D | MDD criteria may be continuously present for 2 yr |
| E | **Never manic / hypomanic** |
| F–H | Not psychotic disorder · not substance / medical · clinically significant distress |
| **Not a feature** | **Lack of interest** ✓ (✗ Wrong options: poor concentration · appetite / eating change · low mood) |

### WADA prohibited list – structure (TO)

| Prohibited (TO, 2018 list) | Substances | Methods |
|---|---|---|
| **At all times** (in + out of competition) | Non-approved (e.g. veterinary) · **anabolic agents** · **peptide hormones, growth factors** + mimetics · **β2-agonists** · **hormone + metabolic modulators** · **diuretics + masking agents** | **Manipulation of blood**, **gene doping**, **chemical + physical manipulation** |
| **In-competition only** | **Stimulants**, **narcotics**, **cannabinoids**, **glucocorticoids** | — |
| **Particular sports** | **Beta-blockers** | — |

| WADA code (TO) | Class | Specified? |
|---|---|---|
| **S1** | Anabolic agents (DHMCT, metandienone) | **Non-specified** |
| **S3** | β2-agonists (terbutaline) · all times | **Specified** |
| **S5** | Diuretics + masking agents (HCTZ) · all times | **Specified** |
| **M2** | Chemical + physical manipulation (**IV infusions, since 2005**) | — |
| **P1** | Beta-blockers (incl. eye drops) | — |

| At-all-times class (TO) | Examples |
|---|---|
| **Other anabolic agents** | **Clenbuterol**, SARMs (andarine, LGD-4033, ostarine, RAD140), **tibolone**, zeranol, zilpaterol |
| **EPO + erythropoiesis agents** | EPO, darbepoetin (dEPO), EPO-Fc, **CERA**, EPO-mimetics (CNTO-530, peginesatide) · HIF activators (**argon, cobalt, xenon**, molidustat, roxadustat) · GATA inhibitor K-11706 · TGF-β inhibitors (luspatercept, sotatercept) · asialo EPO, carbamylated EPO |
| Peptide hormones | **hCG + LH** + releasing factors (buserelin, deslorelin, gonadorelin, goserelin, leuprorelin, nafarelin, triptorelin) · corticotrophins (corticorelin) · **GH**, GH fragments (AOD-9604, hGH 176-191), GHRH (CJC-1293 / 1295, sermorelin, tesamorelin), GHS (ghrelin, anamorelin, ipamorelin, tabimorelin), GHRPs (alexamorelin, GHRP-1 to 6, pralmorelin, hexarelin) |
| **Growth factors** | FGFs, HGF, **IGF-1**, MGFs, **PDGF**, thymosin-β4 (TB-500), **VEGF** + any affecting muscle / tendon / ligament synthesis, vascularisation, regeneration, fibre-type switching |
| **Hormone + metabolic modulators** | **Aromatase inhibitors** (anastrozole, letrozole, exemestane, formestane, aminoglutethimide, testolactone, 6-oxo, androstatrienedione, arimistane) · **SERMs** (raloxifene, **tamoxifen**, toremifene) · anti-oestrogens (**clomifene**, cyclofenil, fulvestrant) · **myostatin inhibitors** · AMPK activators (AICAR, SR9009), PPARδ agonists (GW1516), **insulins**, **meldonium**, trimetazidine |
| **Diuretics + masking agents** | **Desmopressin**, probenecid, **plasma expanders** (IV albumin, dextran, **hydroxyethyl starch**, mannitol) · **acetazolamide**, amiloride, bumetanide, canrenone, chlorthalidone, ethacrynic acid, **furosemide**, indapamide, metolazone, **spironolactone**, **thiazides (HCTZ)**, triamterene, vaptans (tolvaptan) |
| Diuretic exceptions | **Drospirenone**, pamabrom, **topical ophthalmic CAIs (dorzolamide, brinzolamide)**, felypressin in dental anaesthesia |

| In-competition class (TO) | Examples / rule |
|---|---|
| **Stimulants** | Cathine, ephedrine, methylephedrine, pseudoephedrine (thresholds above) · **cocaine** · **epinephrine** allowed only locally (nasal, ophthalmic, with LA) |
| Monitoring (not prohibited, 2018) | **Bupropion, caffeine, nicotine, phenylephrine**, phenylpropanolamine, pipradrol, synephrine |
| **Narcotics** | Buprenorphine, dextromoramide, **diamorphine (heroin)**, **fentanyl** + derivatives, hydromorphone, methadone, **morphine**, nicomorphine, oxycodone, oxymorphone, pentazocine, pethidine |
| **Cannabinoids** | Natural (cannabis, hashish, marijuana) + synthetic (Δ9-THC, cannabimimetics) · **except cannabidiol** |
| **Glucocorticoids** | **Oral, IV, IM, rectal** (2018) → **all injectable, oral, rectal** (2022) · betamethasone, budesonide, cortisone, deflazacort, dexamethasone, fluticasone, hydrocortisone, methylprednisolone, prednisolone, prednisone, triamcinolone |
| **Beta-blockers** (particular sports) | Acebutolol, alprenolol, **atenolol**, betaxolol, bisoprolol, bunolol, carteolol, carvedilol, celiprolol, esmolol, labetalol, levobunolol, metipranolol, **metoprolol**, nadolol, oxprenolol, pindolol, **propranolol**, sotalol, **timolol** |

**Not on the TO list** — **acetaminophen** (tibolone, cannabinoids, EPO all banned) — answer key not provided

| Beta-blocker ban by sport (TO) | All times (in + out) | In-competition only |
|---|---|---|
| 2018 WADA list | **Shooting** (ISSF, IPC) | Archery, automobile, billiards, darts, golf, ski jumping / freestyle aerials / halfpipe / big air, **underwater** (apnoea, spearfishing, target shooting) |
| Eye-drug explanation | **Archery, shooting** | Aeronautics, automobiles, billiards, boules, bridge, darts, golf, ninepin + tenpin bowling, powerboating, skiing / snowboarding |
| Angina case explanation | **Archery, shooting, underwater sports** | Billiards, golf, darts, automobile sports |

**Beta-blocker ban** — sport lists differ between TO explanations (above) — read the options · **hockey not on any list**

| β2-agonist (TO) | Allowed inhaled (no TUE) | Urine AAF |
|---|---|---|
| **Salbutamol** | **≤ 1600 µg / 24 h** · divided: **≤ 800 µg / 12 h** (2018 list) · **≤ 600 µg / 8 h** (newer list) · **≤ 600 µg / 12 h, "earlier 800"** (FNB 2022 key) | **> 1000 ng/ml** |
| **Formoterol** | **≤ 54 µg / 24 h** (delivered) | **> 40 ng/ml** |
| **Salmeterol** | **≤ 200 µg / 24 h** | — |
| **Vilanterol** | **≤ 25 µg / 24 h** (newer list) | — |
| **Terbutaline** | **No threshold → TUE required** · prohibited at all times, even therapeutic | Any |
| All others | Fenoterol, higenamine, indacaterol, olodaterol, procaterol, reproterol, tulobuterol, arformoterol, levosalbutamol, tretoquinol (trimetoquinol) — prohibited | — |

**β2 rule** — all selective + non-selective β2-agonists incl. optical isomers prohibited · urine above threshold = AAF unless **controlled pharmacokinetic study** proves therapeutic inhaled dose · salbutamol = albuterol (EIB prevention dose: Pulmonary Conditions chapter)

**Salbutamol urine "permissible level" (TO)** — **800 ng/ml** ✓ (safely below 1000 ng/ml; 1000 = borderline)\
✗ Wrong options: 1600 · 1200

### Doping control – sample collection (TO)

| Step (TO) | Done by |
|---|---|
| 1 Notification | **Chaperone** |
| 2 Report to doping control station | **Athlete** — **ASAP, within 60 min** · delay allowed for **press conference, training session** · chaperoned throughout |
| 3 Select collection vessel | **Athlete** — 1 individually sealed vessel, checks intact, keeps control |
| 4 Provide sample | Observed by **DCO / DCA of same gender** — 2 people in washroom · 3 if **minor / disabled** (representative doesn't view) |
| 5 Select doping control kit | **Athlete** |
| 6 Divide sample | **Athlete** (representative if disabled) · **B > 30 ml**, rest in **A > 60 ml** |
| 7 Seal A + B | **Athlete** |
| 8 **Measure specific gravity** | **DCO** ✓ (✗ Wrong options: athlete) |
| 9 Documentation | **DCO** |
| 10 Analysis | **WADA-accredited laboratory** |

![A and B urine sample bottles](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17136148-3.png)

**Urine volume** — **minimum 90 ml** ✓ · **150 ml if dilute** → tell athlete **not to over-hydrate**\
✗ Wrong options: 70, 120, 150

**Partial sample (< 90 ml)** — fill into **vial B**, use **partial kit** with **white lid** · "**Berlinger**" vial locks after **5–6 rotations** · later: collect interim sealing device, check number, pour extra urine into initial sample + mix

![Sealing of a partial urine sample](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17136170-1.jpg)

**Sample processing** — athlete chooses kit, checks undamaged + **all code numbers match** · divides B then A, seals · **DCO doesn't handle equipment** unless asked · athlete controls sample until sealed

**DCO duties** — supplies: **3 A&B kits + 3 urine vessels per sample** · sets up DCS · ensures notification + chaperoning · ensures collection, identification, sealing, storage, dispatch to WADA lab · **overall responsible** even when blood taken

**Chaperone** — notifies athlete · accompanies + observes to and in DCS · may witness urine provision if trained

**Blood control officer (BCO)** — prepares athlete, **venepuncture**, aftercare / first aid

**Blood volume** — **20 ml once, divided into two samples** ✓ (10 ml per tube, range 10–15 ml) · "> 5 ml per tube, \~15–17 ml total" (other TO key) — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: 10 ml twice · 15 ml twice · 10 ml once

**Blood collection** — confirm ID · **seated, feet on floor (not lying), ≥ 10 min** · **no exercise past 2 h** · athlete picks **BEREG kit (1 of 3)**, checks numbers · DCO / BCO don't touch materials · athlete sticks code label **longitudinally** on vacutainer · **non-dominant arm** usually · release tourniquet once blood flows · declare medication / supplements of **last 7 days**

**Blood tubes** — **purple = athlete biological passport (ABP)** · **yellow = GH or its markers** + other tests · kit: 1 disinfection pad, 2 cotton swabs, 1 band-aid, gloves, 3 accessory bags (tubes, butterfly needle)

### Notable doping cases (TO)

| Athlete (TO) | Substance | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| **Prithvi Shaw** (cricket) | **Terbutaline in cough syrup** | **Inadvertent**, for respiratory infection · BCCI ban **8 months** |
| **Yusuf Pathan** (cricket) | **Terbutaline** (cough syrup) | 2018 · backdated **5-month** ban |
| **Navjeet Dhillon** (discus, 2018 CWG medallist) | **DHMCT** (S1) | Supplement, unknowingly · **3-year** AIU ban |
| **Dhanalakshmi Sekar** (sprint) | **Metandienone** (S1) | Failed dope test |
| **Anish Kumar** (para discus) | **Hydrochlorothiazide (HCTZ)** (S5, specified) | BP medicine |
| **Kobe Bryant** (basketball) | **Orthokine** | Knee therapy |

**Terbutaline** — **SABA** (salbutamol + terbutaline = SABAs; salmeterol + formoterol = LABAs) · for bronchospasm in asthma, bronchitis, emphysema · ↑ time to exhaustion + muscle power output in cyclists · no help to a cricketer · asthmatic may use SABA / LABA with a **TUE certificate**

## 5 Management

### Antihypertensives & other medications in sport (beta blockers, diuretics) (TO)

```
Hypertensive athlete — choose drug
├─ Needs prior permission → DIURETICS (S5, banned at all times)
├─ Shooter / archer / billiards → NO BETA-BLOCKER
├─ On propranolol → HOCKEY ALLOWED
└─ Training-neutral choice → ACE INHIBITOR / ARB / CCB
```

**Diuretics** — **prohibited at all times; all specified substances** · masking agents\
✗ Wrong options: nitrates · CCB · ACE inhibitors

**Propranolol sportsman** — **hockey** ✓\
✗ Wrong options: billiards · darts / archery · shooting

**50 y, angina 3 yr ago, HTN on beta-blocker, half marathon in 8 months** — **change antihypertensive (ACEI / ARB / CCB) then full cardiac assessment** + BP monitoring ✓\
✗ Wrong options: treadmill stress test on beta-blocker (HR blunted) · refuse clearance · **TUE — not needed** (banned only in particular sports, not tested)

### Therapeutic use exemptions – glucocorticoids & IV fluids (TO)

**Recurrent shoulder pain after intra-articular steroid** — **give another dose + retroactive TUE** ✓\
✗ Wrong options: permission first · decline · "IA steroids banned for any reason"

**Glucocorticoids (2022 list)** — prohibited **in-competition** by **all injectable** (IV, IM, **intra-articular**, periarticular, peritendinous, intratendinous, epidural, intrathecal, intrabursal, intralesional, intradermal, SC), **oral** (oromucosal, buccal, gingival, sublingual) + **rectal** routes

**GC permitted at all times, no TUE** — inhalation, intranasal, eye drops, perianal, dermal, dental intracanal, topical

**Out-of-competition GC** — **not prohibited by any route** · but in-competition urine may still show it → AAF · clinical justification → **retroactive TUE** · no TUE → sanction

**IV infusions (M2.2, since 2005)** — **> 100 ml per 12 h prohibited** in + out of competition · **except** during **hospital treatment, surgical procedure, clinical diagnostic investigation** · **≤ 100 ml / 12 h permitted** unless substance prohibited

**Why IV is banned** — ↑ **plasma volume** · **mask** prohibited substance · distort **athlete biological passport**

**TUE needed for > 100 ml / 12 h outside hospital** — doctor's office, hotel, home, tent, vehicle · event medical facility, first-aid station, start–finish line · IV clinics outside hospital

**Prohibited substance given IV** — **TUE always**, whatever volume or setting · non-prohibited substance > 100 ml outside the 3 exceptions → TUE for the **method**

**Emergency** — haemodynamically unstable → **never withhold IV fluids** · "how much in dehydrated state?" → **life-saving emergency, any amount** ✓

**Team doctor, gastroenteritis, Ringer lactate in training camp** — **100 ml / 12 h** ✓ (not hospital → TUE first, else sanction)\
✗ Wrong options: 200 ml / 12 h · 200 ml / 24 h · excused because life-saving

| IV infusion TUE criteria (TO) | Requirement |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Well-described diagnosis** / condition |
| 2 | **Permitted alternative not medically reasonable** (e.g. why not oral rehydration) |
| 3 | Given by **qualified medical professional** |
| 4 | **Adequate medical records** (substance, rate, history, exam, co-morbidities) |

### Respiratory & eye drugs under WADA (TO)

**EIB at Olympics** — **vilanterol** ✓ (**ultra-LABA, 24 h** vs LABA 12 h → fewer inhalations) · first-line asthma = **reliever + inhaled corticosteroid** · salbutamol, formoterol, salmeterol allowed inhaled at therapeutic doses (2018)\
✗ Wrong options: terbutaline (TUE) · levosalbutamol · trimetoquinol

**Glaucoma drop, hockey player, not doping** — **dorzolamide** ✓ (topical CAI — **no diuretic effect** topically)\
✗ Wrong options: timolol (β-blocker) · nandrolone · prednisolone

| Eye preparation (TO) | WADA status |
|---|---|
| **Dorzolamide, brinzolamide** (topical CAI) | **Permitted** |
| **Acetazolamide** (systemic CAI) | **Prohibited in + out** — masking agent |
| **β-blocker drops** (timolol) | **Prohibited in particular sports (P1)** — systemic levels ≈ oral |
| Prostaglandin analogues, prostamides, apraclonidine, brimonidine | Permitted |
| Pilocarpine (topical) | Permitted (specifically exempted) |
| Antibacterial, antiviral, povidone-iodine | Permitted all routes |
| Antifungals | None prohibited · azoles exempt topically (imidazoles) |
| Topical + intracameral glucocorticoids | Permitted |
| NSAIDs, antihistamines, mast-cell stabilisers | Permitted all routes |
| Atropine, cyclopentolate, homatropine, tropicamide | Permitted |
| Phenylephrine | Monitoring list (2012) · not prohibited |
| Lidocaine, bupivacaine, proxymetacaine, tetracaine | Permitted all routes |
| **Cocaine** | **Prohibited in-competition** |
| Adrenaline | Local use only; else prohibited in-competition |
| Artificial tears, lubricants, fluorescein, verteporfin, pegaptanib, ranibizumab | Permitted |

### ADHD (TO)

**Drug of choice** — **methylphenidate** ✓ (stimulant; most commonly used; adults, teenagers, **children > 5 y**) · licensed: methylphenidate, lisdexamfetamine, dexamfetamine, atomoxetine, guanfacine\
✗ Wrong options: propranolol · methylprednisolone · guanfacine

### Sports psychology (self-efficacy, anxiety, mood disorders) (TO)

**Individual zone of optimal functioning (IZOF)** — **each athlete has own preferred anxiety level** ✓ · performance best when pre-competition anxiety within / near own zone\
✗ Wrong options: drive theory · inverted-U · Troutwine athletic profile

**Athletic self-efficacy** — **strong belief in ability to perform successfully** ✓ · feeling competent to cope · covers ability, fitness, leadership, coaching · affects performance, success, **stress coping**\
✗ Wrong options: vicarious experience · motivational behaviour · emotional arousal

| Personality (TO) | Traits |
|---|---|
| Type A | Competitive, ambitious, perfectionist · impatient, time urgency, **stress-prone** |
| Type B | Relaxed, patient, creative |
| Type C | Analytical, detail-oriented, introverted, wants order / logic · emotional stress |
| **Type D** | **Anxious, worried, negative outlook**, introverted, suppresses emotions ✓ (injured athlete fearing never returning) |

**Immediate reduction of performance anxiety** — cognitive restructuring / mindfulness / self-talk / goal setting — answer key not provided

**Key psychological factor for return to sport** — pain tolerance / psychological resilience / performance / physical strength — answer key not provided

### Team physician – travel, jet lag, media & medical bag (TO)

**Circadian system** — master clock = **suprachiasmatic nucleus (SCN)** + peripheral clocks · synchronised by **zeitgebers**: **light (most critical)** via retinohypothalamic tract, sleep–wake, activity, social cues, meals · light timing, intensity, duration, spectrum matter

**Jet lag** — circadian desynchronisation after rapid travel across **≥ 3 time zones** · poor sleep onset / maintenance, fatigue, ↓ motivation + concentration, ↓ appetite, altered bowel · phase markers: **core body temperature, melatonin**

| Jet lag intervention (TO) | Examples |
|---|---|
| **Melatonin** | **0.5–5 mg** · hypnotic + **chronobiotic** |
| Melatonin analogues | **Tasimelteon, ramelteon** |
| Sedatives | Benzodiazepines (**temazepam**) |
| **Stimulants** | Amodafinil, **caffeine** — **✓ used, not depressants** |
| Non-drug | **Timed exercise**, sleep, light, meal timing / composition |
| Caveats | Drugs only **up to 4 days after arrival** · consider **DVT risk** |

**Jet lag odd one out** — **depressants** ✓ (other options: glucocorticoids, melatonin analogues, sedatives)

**Melatonin timing** — **1–2 h before sleep**, up to **4 nights** after arrival ✓ · dose **1–5 mg** (child 1–2 mg) · after food, don't crush / chew · avoid with **caffeine, alcohol** · avoid in galactose intolerance, congenital lactase deficiency, glucose–galactose malabsorption · melatonin dose 0.5–5 mg (jet-lag table) vs 1–5 mg (dosing note) — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: 30 min · 2–4 h · 3–6 h

**Melatonin physiology** — **N-acetyl-5-methoxytryptamine from serotonin, pineal** · retina → SCN → pineal · rises after sundown, **peaks 2–4 am** · sleep = ↑ melatonin + ↓ body temperature · extrapineal: retina, marrow, platelets, skin, lymphocytes, cerebellum, **GI enterochromaffin cells** (follows tryptophan intake) · child side effects: agitation, bed-wetting, dizziness, drowsiness, headache · patches, tablets, liquids, gummies, spray

**Melatonin timing effect** — **afternoon / evening → phase advance** · **early morning → phase delay**

**↑ free tryptophan** — protein with more Trp than LNAA · **CHO** (insulin drives BCAA into muscle → ↑ free Trp : BCAA) · high-fat meal (↑ FFA) · strenuous exercise

**Media** — team doctor may give only **own name + country / practice** ✓\
✗ Wrong options: player name + injury · drugs / treatment · photo with athlete

| Team physician ethics (TO) | Point |
|---|---|
| Conflict of interest | Personal physician = athlete's health · **team physician = team's health** + fitness to compete |
| **Confidentiality** | Contract may require sharing with management · **never with media** |
| Decision-making | Explain all alternatives, risks, benefits |
| Autonomy | Hierarchical team culture → players may not feel free to opt out |
| **Informed consent** | **Competent · risks + benefits explained · understood · voluntary** |
| **Return to play** | **Main ethical conflict** · **sports medicine physician decides** on athlete's short + long-term health, not result or coach |
| Advertising | Once illegal in USA · still illegal in **Italy, Germany** |

| Medical bags (TO) | Contents |
|---|---|
| **Off-field** (pre / post game) | Physio supplies bag (strapping), doctor supplies bag (suture box, otoscope, HIA tablets in rugby), **medications case (locked)**, recovery devices, medical folder (allergies, medications) |
| **On-field** | Physio pitch bag (tape, blood management), doctor pitch bag (player-specific drugs, inhalers, contact lenses) |
| **Pitch-side** | Splints bag, **emergency bag (defibrillator, analgesia, O2)**, vacuum mattress, spinal board + blocks, collar |
| **Odd one out** | **"Athlete medical bag"** ✓ — athletes only clear **sports kits** through customs |

**Bag rules** — list agreed at **start of season** · check governing-body standards · **check customs rules for each country** + carry documentation · consistent packing · **checklist**; post-game list of items to replace

**Medications case** — oral analgesics, anti-inflammatories, antihistamines, antiemetics, Fucidin, chloramphenicol ointment, loperamide, Buscopan, GTN, aspirin, OCP, tranexamic + mefenamic acid

**On-field tips** — gloves · gauze + nose plugs under glove on **dorsal thenar of non-dominant hand** · saline pods · labelled contact lenses + pocket mirror · energy gels, hair bands · pocket mask, **methoxyflurane**, oropharyngeal airways in outer pockets

## X vs Y

| Drug (OB) | Distinguishing adverse effect |
|---|---|
| **AAS (Dianabol)** | **↓ HDL, tendon tears**, aggression, ↑ Hb |
| **Creatine** | **Dehydration**, cramps, water retention, nausea |
| **Human growth hormone** | **Acromegaly**, cardiac failure, glucose intolerance, myopathy |
| **EPO** | **Hyperviscosity → thromboembolism**, HTN, hyperkalaemia |
| **Ephedra** | **Arrhythmias (fatal)**, palpitations, psychiatric |
| **Caffeine** | **↑ blood pressure** |

| | **SABA** | **LABA** | **Ultra-LABA** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Drugs (TO) | **Salbutamol, terbutaline** | **Salmeterol, formoterol** | **Vilanterol** |
| Duration | Short | **12 h** | **24 h** |
| WADA | Salbutamol threshold · **terbutaline needs TUE** | Thresholds | 25 µg / 24 h |

| | **PRP** | **Orthokine** |
|---|---|---|
| Layer used | Platelets, re-injected at once | Serum **cultured with glass beads, heated** |
| Product | Platelets / growth factors | **IL-1 antagonist (IRAP)** + more growth factors |

| | **Travel fatigue** | **Jet lag** |
|---|---|---|
| Who | **All travellers**, any direction / time zones | **≥ 3 time zones**, east–west (transmeridian) |
| Cause | Cramped seats, mild hypoxia, climate change (trans-latitudinal), inactivity | **Circadian desynchrony** |
| Course | Acute or **chronic (cumulative)** | Episodic, **more severe + prolonged** |
